# Changelog

## Unreleased

### Breaking changes

- `guardianRefusalGrounds` and `GuardianRefusalGround` add `write_authority`: Guardian refused an execution because the step's own `trusted_authority.allowedEffects` grant no write. When the host's record gives the step write authority (`PendingExecution.writeAuthority`), the host sends the refusal back once in the same review, as it does a settled `retry_permission` or `committed_write` refusal, and a refusal that keeps resting on it counts as `correctable`, so it never lets the build end as a policy block. A step without write authority keeps the refusal as Guardian gave it. Before, an act step reviewed right after a read-only explore in the same conversation could be refused as final for lacking a write its own authority granted, and the build ended blocked. The execution refusal policy names the ground, and the output format's `refusalGround` enum lists it. An older reviewer never names it, so its refusals stand as before.
  - Guardian's session continuity policy says that earlier requests, reads, reasoning and verdicts neither grant nor limit the current request's authority, and that Guardian reads each step's allowed effects from that step's own `trusted_authority`, so an act step keeps the write its allowed effects grant after a read-only explore review. It adds text only; the conversation keeps its full context.
  - Migrate a host that maps each refusal ground, such as in an exhaustive check, by handling `write_authority`. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy, continuity text or output format needs recording again.
- A Guardian review whose request the model provider refuses as over the model's input limit, its context window, by the provider's code `context_length_exceeded`, fails with the new `ReviewFailure` code `InputTooLarge` instead of `Unavailable`. That is no outage, and the same request fails the same way, so `guardianOutageRetry` returns it at once: such a review used to be sent again for the whole five-minute outage budget, and every attempt failed the same way. Every other provider error is still `Unavailable` and still retried.
  - The build harness ends the attempt on a review that failed `InputTooLarge`, as it does once a review outage outlasts its budget, instead of answering `review_unavailable` with `retryable: true`, which had the minter resubmit the same failing review until the budget ran out. A blocked explanation's review that fails so ends blocked with the reason alone.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1. Migrate a host that maps each `ReviewFailure` code by adding `InputTooLarge` as a review that could not finish and that reviewing the same input again cannot finish either. A host that wraps the reviewer with `guardianOutageRetry` needs no change.
- Publishing a write refuses, before review, a composed output schema that lists a `refused` status the host can't read as a declared refusal, as the new `MintFailure` reason `refusal_unreadable` (code `PublicationUnavailable`). A run that returned such a refusal ended as an unknown outcome, like any other unconfirmed write. `checkWriteSession` from `pomerado/core/mint/write-session` runs the check when the host's `extract` returns the composed script's new optional `outputSchema`, and skips it when the new optional `registeredOutputSchema`, the output schema of the revision a repair or change replaces, has an unreadable refusal too, so a published tool is never refused for the shape it already has. The new `unreadableRefusal` from `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome` decides it: some branch of the schema lists `refused` as its `status`, and `declaredRefusalReasons` finds no reason. The local host runs the check on every write it publishes. The writes skill's `writes.declared-refusal` section adds a line that prefers each refusal as its own branch of the output's top-level union, and a listed `status` on every other branch.
  - Migrate a host that maps each publication refusal reason by handling `refusal_unreadable`. A host adopts the check by returning `outputSchema` from the `extract` it passes, and passing `registeredOutputSchema` for a republish; one that passes neither publishes as before. A host that composes the authoring directory with its own `writes.declared-refusal` text adds the line there. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact skill text needs recording again.
- The writes skill's sentences on when a declared refusal completes a run, under "What runs do with it", and the publication skill's line on how the publication review judges a write's refusals are the new named sections `writes.declared-refusal` and `publication.declared-refusal`, whose standalone text is unchanged. A host whose runs accept a declared refusal in more cases says so there.
  - Migrate a host that composes the authoring directory with its own section text by adding the two sections.
- A sign-in submit that another element covers, such as a fixed page footer or header, is pressed from the keyboard. The host's click of a covered submit came back `blocked`, and only a `dismiss` of the covering element's own control got past it, so an element with no such control, such as a fixed footer, stopped the sign-in. A window resize that uncovered the submit in the build's browser can't be recorded, so a run's replay on a fresh browser was blocked again.
  - A fill's first click that another element catches stays `blocked` with `blockedBy` and presses nothing, so a cookie banner over the submit is dismissed first: the minter names the banner's own control as `dismiss`, and the host's click again of that `blocked` screen (`resubmitAutofillStep`) clicks it, then the submit. When Playwright says another element intercepted that click again, as on a screen under a fixed footer with nothing to dismiss or one whose dismiss did not clear the element, the host focuses that same submit and presses Enter on it, only once focus landed on that exact element (`activeElement`, inside a shadow root too), under the same submission guard and the destination check already passed, and never clicks the covering element. A filled `AutofillStepReport` then says `submit: "clicked"` with the new optional `via: "keyboard"`. When focus goes anywhere else, the report is `blocked` as before. A host that reads `via` gets the minter's new keyboard notice (`keyboardSubmitNotice`) from `stepNotice`. The minter's `blocked` notice adds that a covering element with no such control, such as a fixed header or footer, means sending the same step again without `dismiss`, and without resizing or scrolling the page, so the host presses the submit. A host without the `resubmit` hook fills a screen sent again as a new fill, which presses nothing. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact `blocked` notice needs recording again.
  - Sign-in recipe version 8 (`KeyboardSubmitAutofillRecipe`) is version 7 whose steps may say `submitVia: "keyboard"`, only on a step with a submit. `recordStep` records it on a screen the host pressed so, `autofillRecipe` and `signInRecipe` write version 8 only when a step says it, and every other recipe keeps its version and bytes. `AutofillStep` adds the optional `submitVia`, which `screenSubmit` carries into each replayed step, so a run presses that submit from the keyboard first, once focus lands on it, and clicks it otherwise. A host that predates version 8 reads such a recipe as `unknown_version` and refuses it (`MissingRecipe`). Migrate a host that stores recipes by their version by adding version 8.
- Sign-in recipe version 7 (`WildcardAutofillRecipe`) is version 6 whose steps may list `anySegments`, the segments of the step page's path written as `*`, such as a later screen at `/team/<team>/password` where each account's team differs, so the published recipe names no account's segment. Runs never open a recorded screen, so a replay reads a page as that screen on the same origin with as many segments, any one non-empty segment at each listed place and every other segment the same (`pageMatches`, `pathMatches`). The first step and the marker's `urlPath` and `openPath` never hold `*`, and `checkSignInVersion` refuses a listed step off the site's own origin and the login's workspace host, such as another site's sign-in, or on the page the entry URL opens, as `wildcard_placement`. `autofillRecipe` and `signInRecipe` write version 7 only when a step lists segments, with `anySegments` right after `page`, so every other recipe keeps its version and bytes. Migrate a host that compares recorded screen pages with its own code by using `pageMatches`, or by refusing version 7 until it does, and handle `wildcard_placement` in any exhaustive check on a version finding's rule.
  - A version 7 step's page keeps a literal non-empty segment beside the ones it writes as `*`, so a page such as `/*` or `/*/*`, which would match any page with as many segments, does not decode and `checkSignInVersion` reports it as `invalid_recipe`. A `*` segment in the marker's `urlPath` or `openPath` is refused however the path is written, such as `*/home` with no leading slash. `autofillRecipe` and `signInRecipe` read an empty `anySegments` as none, so the recipe keeps its lower version and leaves the key out. `marker_is_login_path` also refuses a URL path marker that a sign-in screen's page matches with its `anySegments`, and `loginPathRefusal` takes that list as an optional third argument.
- A sign-in step may name `dismiss`, the own close, reject or accept control of a cookie, consent or similar notice that covers the screen, and a click another element caught is reported as such. Without either, a notice over a sign-in form's submit made every click of it fail, the minter sent the screen again, and the host refused the third send of the password (`credential_already_filled`), so the sign-in could not finish; a run's replay on a fresh browser met the notice again and failed the same way.
  - A filled `AutofillStepReport`'s `submit` adds `"blocked"`, with the new `blockedBy` (`CoveringElement`: the covering element's tag and ARIA role, each one of a fixed list, else `other` and null, never its text, id, label or class names, which page code may write from what was typed): Playwright named an element that intercepts pointer events, and that element sits over the submit's centre, so the click never reached the submit, nothing was sent and the fields still hold what was typed. A failed click for any other reason stays `failed`. `maySend` is false for it, a replay's fill failure is `submit_blocked`, and the minter's notice says to send the same step again with the notice's control as `dismiss`.
  - `SignInStep`, `AutofillStep` and `AutofillStepRequest` add the optional `dismiss` selector. The fill clicks it first when the page shows one visible match; a dismiss the page does not show is skipped. A dismiss that is, holds or sits in one of the step's controls, sits in the form of one, is, holds or sits in a control that submits a form or a link to another page, open shadow roots included, or matches several visible elements is refused before anything is typed (`AutofillRefusal` reason `dismiss_refused`, target `dismiss`). On a step that names `dismiss`, a notice drawn only once the fields were typed is dismissed when it catches the click, and the submit is clicked once more, with nothing typed again.
  - `resubmitAutofillStep` clicks again the submit of a step whose fill reported `blocked`, typing nothing: it clicks the step's `dismiss` first, checks that each field the fill typed still holds something (lengths only), and judges, guards and clicks the submit as the fill does. `AutofillHooks` and `SignInBrowser` add the optional `resubmit`, and the local host's browser implements it. The local recorder keeps the screen whose click was caught as the open sign-in's `blocked` screen (`AutofillSignInState.blocked`); the same screen sent again (`blockedScreen`) is clicked again through `resubmit` instead of filled, so the minter going on sends nothing and no secret counts as filled again, and the click that sends the values counts them once (`recordResubmit`). The screen's recorded step keeps the `dismiss`. A click again that finds a field emptied, as when the dismiss loaded the page again, clicks nothing: `recordResubmit` drops the screen's recorded step and returns what its fill typed, which the host counts as filled, and the recorder fills the screen again in the same step as a refill, within the password's two fills, so the recipe records the screen once, with its dismiss.
  - Sign-in recipe version 6 (`DismissAutofillRecipe`) is version 5 whose steps may carry `dismiss`, never one of the step's own controls. `autofillRecipe` and `signInRecipe` write it only when a step names one, so every other recipe keeps its version and bytes. `screenSubmit` keeps a recorded screen's `dismiss`, so a replay's fill dismisses the notice. A host that does not know version 6 refuses it as `unknown_version`, and a version 5 step with `dismiss` is `invalid`.
  - Whenever a cookie or tracking consent banner shows, the minter dismisses it before anything else on the page, in an `explore`, in each sign-in step as its `dismiss` and in the tool's own code, through the banner's own close or dismiss control, else one that rejects what is not essential, else accept when that is the only way out, and never anything else in the banner; a request that says otherwise about cookies wins. The core skill's new section says so, and the auth skill names the control as each sign-in step's `dismiss`. Guardian's execution policy allows such a dismissal without asking, and judges the same sign-in step sent again after `submit: blocked` as no resubmission.
  - The page code the fill runs changes with it: `autofillStepCode`'s `fill.check` and a `FillProgress`'s `check` are arrays of field indices (empty for none) instead of a number or null, and `AutofillFillCall` adds the `dismiss` call kind.
  - Migrate a host by handling `"blocked"` in any exhaustive check on a filled report's `submit`, `dismiss_refused` on a refusal's `reason` and `dismiss` on its `target`; a host that replays recipes with its own code by passing a step's `dismiss` through to its fill, or by refusing version 6 until it does; and a host with its own sign-in recorder by clicking a blocked screen again through `resubmitAutofillStep` when the minter sends it again. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact policy, skill or notice text needs recording again.
- Guardian keeps its prompt prefix the same across reviews and builds, so the provider's cache serves it. The build's request, `trusted_request`, moved out of the instructions every review shares into a second developer message after them, with a session's continuity policy, so the shared instructions are the same for every build under a host. The instructions travel as developer messages instead of top-level `instructions`: the shared one ends in a 12-hour cache breakpoint, and every request caches implicitly after it, so each call of a review, with or without a session, reads the calls before it. Each review's kind policy stays in its own user message as `trusted_review.policy`, and the shared instructions hold no kind's policy, so Guardian reads a review's policy with the review it governs. The layout and request policies say where the request and the review's policy are. The outcome reviewer's instructions travel the same way, with a 12-hour breakpoint.
  - A review's reasoning effort no longer changes the request's `reasoning.effort`, which kept the cached prefix from holding: every request keeps the default, and a review at another effort than its conversation's adds a `configuration_update` item before its request. A session request whose conversation holds one goes without the provider's automatic compaction, which the provider does not combine with it, and the session's own compaction re-adds the effort in force after its compaction item. A request that would reach the compaction threshold when the session could not compact first, after a failed compaction or in a restored session, keeps the provider's compaction instead: it goes without its configuration updates, carries the effort in force as its own `reasoning.effort`, and the effort follows the provider's compaction item again as an update. A session snapshot from this release can hold a configuration update, so it does not continue on an older release, which sends it with the provider's automatic compaction and has the request refused.
  - `GuardianModelOptions.policies` (`GuardianHostPolicies`) registers a host's own policy for each kind once, when the reviewer is built, and each review of that kind carries it in `trusted_review.policy`: `execution`, `recovery`, `question` and `update` follow the core policy of their kind, `publication` replaces the core publication policy (with no core `trusted_publication` index or 32-turn default, as a host publication policy had), and `hostKinds` holds each host-defined kind's policy by name, sent before the review's own `HostReview.policy` unless that is the same text. `specialize`'s `policy` is deprecated: it still reaches that review's `trusted_review.policy`, after the policy `policies` holds for the kind unless it is the same text, and a per-review publication policy there still replaces the core one.
  - `GuardianModelOptions.promptCacheKey` and `OutcomeReviewerModelOptions.promptCacheKey` send the host's `prompt_cache_key` on every request. `makeOpenAIReviewer` returns an `OpenAIReviewer`, whose `prewarm()` prepares the shared instructions' cache with a prewarm request that matches a session review's first request up to the breakpoint; `makeOpenAIOutcomeReviewer(...).prewarm()` does the same for the outcome reviewer. `GuardianUsage` and the shared model usage counts add `cacheWrite30mTokens` and `cacheWrite12hTokens`. Each Guardian session and outcome reviewer request after the conversation's first sets `prompt_cache_options.comparison_response_id` to the conversation's latest response, kept in the new optional `GuardianSessionSnapshot.latestResponseId`, and `GuardianUsage.promptCacheDiagnostics` lists each call's `prompt_cache_diagnostics` (`PromptCacheDiagnostic`: `type`, a known `reason`, `cacheMissedTokens`, `comparisonReusableTokens`).
  - Migrate a host that adds policy through `specialize` by registering it in `policies` instead, and one that defines its own kinds by registering their policies in `policies.hostKinds`. A host that reads the request's top-level instructions from a recorded request reads the leading developer messages instead. A recorded Guardian or outcome reviewer response keyed on the exact instructions, review request or reasoning settings needs recording again.
- `BuildBlocked`, `BlockedReport`, `JobBuildBlocked` and `MintStopReason` add the reason `owner_ended`: the owner chose in an answer to end the build, such as after confirming that a fact it needs is unknown. Such a build used to end under another reason, which told its caller to change the request or that the site refused. It stands on the owner's latest `request_input` answer with no other call through the turn's `MintActions` since: the host refuses it (`owner_not_ended`) when the build went on after that answer, such as running a step, or when that request kept only a secret or sign-in answer, which Guardian never sees. An unconfirmed sign-in keeps it, as it keeps `owner_declined`. Guardian reviews its explanation like any blocked ending. A `MintHarnessSnapshot` whose `terminal.blocked` holds it does not decode on a worker from an older release. The workspace guide and the `report_blocked` description name it.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `blocked.reason` by handling `owner_ended`.
- `BuildBlocked`, `BlockedReport`, `JobBuildBlocked` and `MintStopReason` add the reason `site_unavailable`: the site is temporarily down for the failing input, as its own pages show a maintenance or outage page or fail with a 5xx answer from its own service, while the tool's code and the caller's input are unchanged, never a 5xx answer only to the request the tool made. Such a build used to end `site_lacks_capability`, which told its caller the site can't do the task, when trying again later may work. Like `site_lacks_capability`, it needs no basis on record, and Guardian reviews its explanation before a caller reads it. An older core never reports it. A `MintHarnessSnapshot` whose `terminal.blocked` holds it does not decode on a worker from an older release. The workspace guide and the `report_blocked` description name it, and say to prefer it over changing the code or over `site_lacks_capability`, never for a bot check or an access-denied page. The `report_blocked` description now says it is never for anything recoverable in this attempt.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `blocked.reason` by handling `site_unavailable`, such as by telling its caller to try again later.
- `BuildBlocked`, `BlockedReport`, `JobBuildBlocked` and `MintStopReason` add the reason `caller_input`: the caller's own request stops the build, because what it states conflicts with itself or its cited sources, a file it needs is missing, or it rules out going on, such as a stop condition it set that cannot be met. A missing fact the caller could supply is still asked with `request_input`. Such a build used to end `site_lacks_capability`, which told its caller the site can't do the task, though the site offers it. The host takes it only when the report's `intent` cites the request, by a field name its input holds or four words in a row of its text (`citesCallerRequest`), and refuses it otherwise (`caller_input_not_cited`). `BlockedReport` adds an optional `intent`, which `report_blocked` now passes in the report, so a host that wraps `MintActions.reportBlocked` keeps it. Guardian reviews its explanation like any blocked ending, and the explanation says what the caller should supply or change. An older core never reports it. A `MintHarnessSnapshot` whose `terminal.blocked` holds it does not decode on a worker from an older release. The workspace guide and the `report_blocked` description name it, and the continuation prompt counts the caller's own request among what makes a task impossible as asked.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `blocked.reason` by handling `caller_input`, such as by telling its caller to change the request as the explanation says.
- Guardian keeps doubts about a step's returned values out of execution-review refusals. Whether a step returns the right value for the requested output is no reason to deny or escalate an execution: Guardian allows a step that is otherwise acceptable and puts its doubt in the new nullable `advice` of the shared output format, which the host returns to the minter with the allow, in the execution's `review`. Only publication review judges the output, so the outcome policy's paragraph on completing an incomplete capability now reaches publication reviews only. A recovery review's allow may carry advice too. `GuardianDecision`, `MintReviewFeedback` and the allowed outcomes of `MintBrowserRecoveryResult` add the optional `advice`; the host drops it on a deny or escalate and cuts it to 2,000 characters. Guardian's request policy adds that a returned field the request defines, by name, column, record or description, is decided by that definition, never by a page attribute's name or Guardian's own reading of a capture, and that when the evidence cannot settle which of two page values fits, Guardian says so and recommends that the builder confirm with the owner.
  - The minter's `execute` intent reaches the step's execution review as `submitted_call.agentNote`, the agent's own untrusted description of the step, cut to `agentNoteLimit` (500) characters. `MintActions.execute` takes it as an optional second argument, `MintDependencies.reviewAndExecute`'s request carries it as `agentNote` (`StepNote`), and `PendingExecution.agentNote` carries it to the review. The local host passes it, redacted like the input, for authored steps.
  - Guardian's sign-in step policy says that the host reads a code box's `codeBox` from the page and a step never carries it, so Guardian never asks for one in the step. When the screen shows no `codeBox` but the step lists, in order, every one of a run of one-time-code inputs that share their type and container, Guardian judges them by their attributes as the boxes of one code.
  - Migrate a host that builds `PendingExecution` for an execution review itself by passing the request's `agentNote`, screened like the input, as `agentNote`, and a host that returns Guardian's allow to the minter itself by passing `decision.advice` through, screened like the rationale. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text, instructions, review request or output format needs recording again.
- `ScriptQuestionDeclaration` from `pomerado/core/runtime/script-input` adds the member `{ type: "site_field" }`, which declares no prompt (see the site-field question under Other changes). A host that switches over a declaration's `type` exhaustively no longer compiles, and one that reads every declaration's `prompt` meets a declaration without one.
  - Migrate a host that switches over a declaration's `type` by handling `site_field`.
- The auth skill's signed-in-page sentence on `openPath` and its list of `check_signed_in_marker` checks are the new named sections `auth.signed-in-page` and `auth.marker-checks`, whose standalone text is unchanged.
  - Migrate a host that composes the authoring directory with its own section text by adding the two sections.
- `inputContractChangedInstruction` from `pomerado/core/mint/registry-feedback` is now a function of whether the build is a write. Its text says what a requested change may do to an input the published tool declares: add optional inputs, widen what an input accepts, add a default, or narrow it only to a limit the site itself states, such as its own maxlength. A read runs its example again and publishes that execution; a write calls `finish_build` again with the same `executionId` and never runs the write again, and its refusal is recorded with recovery `correct_source`, not `new_observation`.
  - Migrate a host that reads the constant by calling it with `false` for a read and `true` for a write. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact instruction needs recording again.
- The local host checks the sign-in a build would publish with `checkSignInVersion` and refuses `finish_build` when it fails, every time, with the new `MintFailure` reason `sign_in_version_refused` (code `PublicationUnavailable`). The minter's refusal names each finding as `signInFindings`, `{ rule, part }`, never a value, and tells it to sign in again without those parts; `MintFailure.publicationFeedback` adds `signInFindings`. A login URL, sign-in page or popup off the site's registrable domain now refuses publication (`foreign_origin`), on an origin the request declares in `authenticationOrigins` too; those origins still count while the build signs in. One over plain http refuses it too (`insecure_origin`). A one-time login URL the host already asked about still publishes when the build finishes again. The host passes no identity values or signed-out pages: the recorder already applied those guards to each screen as it recorded the sign-in.
  - Migrate a host that maps each publication refusal reason by handling `sign_in_version_refused`.
- `admitListCursor` from `pomerado/core/runtime/list-cursor` refuses every non-empty cursor its host did not sign, as `malformed`, before the run starts. A cursor whose first dot-separated part was not `pc` and a number, or that was not a string, used to count as the tool's own and reach the run unchanged, so a made-up cursor started a run. Tools never make or read a cursor of their own. An absent, empty or null cursor still starts at the first page, and a cursor in another host version's form is still refused as `version`.
  - `sealListOutput` drops a `next_cursor` that is not a runtime draft, such as a string or number a tool wrote itself, instead of passing it to the caller, since the host would refuse it when it came back. `next_cursor` becomes null and `dropped` is `not_a_draft`. `has_more` stays as the tool gave it, and when it is true, `next_cursor_unavailable` is `not_a_draft`. An output without `has_more` counts as having more: the drop adds `has_more: true`, `next_cursor_expires_at: null` and `next_cursor_unavailable: "not_a_draft"` to it. An absent or null `next_cursor` passes unchanged.
  - Migrate a tool that reads or writes its own cursor by paging with `startList`, `readList` and `finishList`, whose cursors the host signs.
- `modelFailureMetadata` from `pomerado/core/models/model-failure`, the record of a failed model call that Guardian and the minter keep, reads more of the error. An SDK error is named by its class, such as `RateLimitError`, not `Error`. `requestId` comes from the SDK's `requestID`, where it was always empty. `causeCode` is the first code among up to eight nested causes, such as a closed socket's `UND_ERR_SOCKET`. The record keeps the message after credential redaction and the SDK's two replay marks, in the new optional `message`, `unsafeToReplay` and `responseStarted`. `providerCode` is now the provider's `code` beside an HTTP status, where it was the error's `type`. A string error reads as kind `string`, with its text as the message. Names, codes and request IDs pass the same redaction and keep at most 256 characters.
  - Migrate a host that matched `providerCode` against an error `type`: `providerCode` no longer carries `type`, so a host that needs it reads it in its own `failureMetadata`.
- The records skill is no longer part of the package: the authoring directory drops `records/SKILL.md`, and the local minter's skill catalog no longer lists it. The workspace guide's sentence that sent the minter to it is the new named section `agents.user-defined-fields`, empty in the standalone text.
  - Migrate a host that composes the authoring directory with its own section text by adding `agents.user-defined-fields`, and a host that loads `records` from the package by supplying its own.
- A sign-in's code step that names only some of a screen's one-character code boxes, or names them out of order, is refused before Guardian reviews it or anyone is asked for a code, as the new `AutofillRefusal` reason `code_boxes_incomplete`, a correctable step result. Its new optional `codeBoxes` gives how many boxes the screen shows and the place of each box the step named, counts only. Such a step could never send the whole code, and Guardian denied it, which a host could end as a `policy` block. The inspection's `screen.fields` add the optional `codeBox` (`box`, from 1, and `of`) for a code field that is one of the screen's two or more one-character boxes (a visible, enabled text input whose `maxlength` is 1, in its form or document), and `codeBoxesRefusal` from `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-step` returns the refusal. The local recorder checks it after the inspection; a run's replay does not, so a published recipe replays as before. Guardian's execution policy says what `codeBox` means. The auth skill tells the minter to name every box, first to last.
  - Migrate a host that records sign-ins with its own code by calling `codeBoxesRefusal` after the inspection and before Guardian's review, and handling `code_boxes_incomplete` where it maps refusal reasons. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text or review input needs recording again.
- Guardian's execution review honours two host facts it used to misread. A deny or escalate may name the host fact it rests on in the new nullable `refusalGround` of the shared output format: `retry_permission` (the step may not run again) or `committed_write` (it would commit a listed write again). The host checks it against its own record: a retry-permission refusal when `trusted_execution_context.repeatableRead` is true, or a committed-write refusal of a step the same review labels a read, goes back to Guardian once in the same review, naming the fact. A refusal that still rests on it is kept as `correctable`, never `final`, so it cannot end the build as a `policy` block. After a committed-write refusal goes back, only an allow labelled `read` stands: any other allow keeps the original refusal, as `correctable`, so a second write and an unrelated read stay refused. The execution policy says plainly that `repeatableRead` true permits running the read example again, a corrected one included, and that reading back a confirmed write's result, such as looking up the record it created or its ID on any page of the site, stays allowed. The write session's allowed effect and the writes skill say the same: a read-back may look the new record up elsewhere on the site when the page the commit left lacks what the output needs. `GuardianDecision` is unchanged: the host drops `refusalGround` after its check. A review gets one more follow-up round (`reviewFollowUpRounds`).
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text or output format needs recording again.
- The `http-mcp` authoring skill and its references `http-version.ts` and `kernel-page-fetch.ts` are no longer part of the package. The local minter never loaded them. The HTTP version runtime in `pomerado/runtime` is unchanged. The core, search, testing and writes skills and the workspace guide hold their passages on building, testing and publishing a read's HTTP version, and their pointers to `http-mcp`, in new named sections, empty in the standalone text: `core.write-http-check`, `core.data-request-reference`, `search.http-read-back`, `testing.write-http-check`, `testing.signed-in-http`, `testing.data-request-filter`, `testing.http-cases`, `writes.http-replay`, `agents.http-input-urls`, `agents.row-query-reference`, `agents.http-cases` and `agents.write-http-check`.
  - Migrate a host that composes the authoring directory with its own section text by adding these sections, and a host that loads `http-mcp` or either reference from the package by supplying its own.
- `BuildBlocked`, `BlockedReport`, `JobBuildBlocked` and `MintStopReason` add the reason `host_refused`: a host check, not Guardian and not the site, refused a step the build needs, such as a publication, a sign-in form or a site change, and nothing left in the attempt can fix it. Such a build used to end `policy`, which told its caller that a safety review or one of their constraints ruled the request out. The harness records each host refusal itself and refuses `host_refused` without one (`host_not_refused`). `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `hostRefused`. Guardian reviews its explanation like any blocked ending. A `MintHarnessSnapshot` whose `terminal.blocked` holds it does not decode on a worker from an older release. The workspace guide and the `report_blocked` description name it.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `blocked.reason` by handling `host_refused`.
- Guardian's execution review marks each deny or escalate with `refusal`: `final` when no change to the source, input or evidence within the request's authority would make the step acceptable, `correctable` otherwise. Only a final refusal lets a later `report_blocked` use `policy`; a correctable one, such as a click to remove or a site guard to add, comes back as review feedback with its `refusal`, and the host refuses `policy` on it as `policy_not_refused`. `GuardianDecision` and `MintFailure.review` add the optional `refusal`, and the shared output format adds a nullable `refusal`. A deny or escalate without one, or with a value Guardian may not return, counts as `final`, as every execution refusal did before.
  - Migrate a host that builds `MintFailure` from Guardian's execution decision itself by passing `refusal` through. Until it does, its execution refusals keep counting as final. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text or output format needs recording again.
- A sign-in recipe version 5 step (`KeySubmitAutofillRecipe`) may also carry `refills: ["password"]`, which a version 5 host that predates it refuses as an unknown key (`invalid`), and a host that does not know version 5 as `unknown_version`. It marks a screen that asks again for the password an earlier screen took, beside a second factor (a `code` or `recovery_code` field), as a site that wants the password re-entered with a one-time code does. A step may list it only beside a second factor it takes and the password field. The local recorder writes version 5 when such a screen of a verified sign-in filled the password once more; `autofillRecipe`, `recipeStep` and `signInRecipeText` write `refills`, and `AutofillRecipeStep` gains it. A run's replay enters the password again on that screen only while the screen's second factor has not gone out in the replay and the password went out once at most; a screen that asks for it again any other way stays a rejection (`credential_already_filled`), and the password still goes out at most twice. `runReplayStep` and `refillRefused` take the recorded screen's `refills` as an optional last argument. Migrate a host that replays recipes with its own code by applying the same rule, or by refusing version 5 until it does.
- `AutofillRecipe`, `publishedAutofillRecipe`, `SignInRecipe` and `decodeSignInRecipe` now read sign-in recipe version 4 (`WorkspaceAutofillRecipe`), which a host refused before as `unknown_version`. Its steps may carry `origin: "workspace"` with a path-only `page`, a canonical pathname the URL parser keeps unchanged (no backslash, query, fragment or dot segment), and `AutofillRecipeStep` gains that optional `origin`. Migrate a host that replays recipes with its own code by applying `admitScreenOrigin` from `pomerado/core/runtime/workspace-origin` to each screen, or by refusing version 4 until it does.
- A sign-in step may submit with a key: `SignInStep`, `AutofillStep` and `AutofillStepRequest` add the optional `submitKey: "Enter"`, never with `submit` or `methods`, for a screen whose form has no submit control and submits on Enter, such as a one-time-code form of one field. The host presses Enter in the step's last field instead of clicking. It inspects that field's form as it would a clicked submit's: `targets.submit` is the field's own place and destinations, `screen.submit` describes the form (tag `form`) with its method, and the new `screen.submitKey` gives the key, the field and how many visible submit controls the form has. A field in no form is refused (`credential_target_refused`, check `submit_key`). The submission guard watches the press as it does a click, and a filled report's `submit` is `clicked` once the key was pressed.
  - Sign-in recipe version 5 (`KeySubmitAutofillRecipe`) is version 4 whose steps may carry `submitKey`. `autofillRecipe` and `signInRecipe` write it only when a step uses the key, so every other recipe keeps its version and bytes. A host that does not know version 5 refuses it as `unknown_version` and never replays such a step without submitting it. Migrate a host that replays recipes with its own code by passing `submitKey` through to its fill, or by refusing version 5 until it does.
  - Guardian's execution policy allows the key as the submit only when that form shows no submit control, and judges the form's method as a clicked submit's; an explore may read a sign-in form's submit controls and key handlers. The auth skill names the key for a code or password form with no button. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Guardian's publication policy adds one sentence for sign-in recipes with a workspace relation: the relation and a step's `origin: "workspace"` marking alone are never a customer data, private literal or tenant-specific finding, while each workspace step's path is still judged like any other published text. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Guardian receives the build's request in its instructions, as `trusted_request.intent`, in place of `trusted_authority.intent` in each review's user message. The request is the same for every review of a build, so it now stays in the cached prefix, survives a compaction and is sent once rather than once per review. It comes last, after the policy every build shares. Guardian reads it as context about the task's scope, never as instructions: an action that serves a capability the request states, such as listing the tables a table app has when the request says to name them for a table that doesn't exist, is in scope, and Guardian judges its safety under the same rules as before. A publication delta names the request `trusted_request.intent` among the inputs that changed.
  - Migrate a host whose own review policy (`specialize`) names `trusted_authority.intent` by naming `trusted_request.intent`. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact instructions or review request needs recording again.
- An unanswered question or the owner's no no longer ends a build as a `policy` block. `BuildBlocked`, `BlockedReport` and `JobBuildBlocked` add the reasons `owner_declined` (the owner answered no to a `confirm` question, or declined to sign in) and `question_unanswered` (a question the build needed came back as no answer). The host refuses each without its basis on record (`owner_not_declined`, `no_question_unanswered`), and a `policy` report whose only basis is one of them ends under that reason's name. `policy` now stands only on a Guardian refusal or on `MintDependencies.policyBlockAllowed`. A `MintHarnessSnapshot` whose `terminal.blocked` holds a new reason does not decode on a worker from an older release.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `blocked.reason` by handling the two new reasons.
- `BuildBlocked`, `BlockedReport`, `JobBuildBlocked` and `MintStopReason` add the reason `site_declined`: the site offers what the task needs but declines this example's own facts, such as an applicant it will not quote, and the explanation names the reason the site gave. Such a build used to end `site_lacks_capability`, which told its caller the site can't do the task. Like `site_lacks_capability`, it needs no basis on record, and Guardian reviews its explanation before a caller reads it. A `MintHarnessSnapshot` whose `terminal.blocked` holds it does not decode on a worker from an older release. The workspace guide and the `report_blocked` description name it.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `blocked.reason` by handling `site_declined`.
- `SignInStep`'s `rejected` may now be `{ account: "locked" | "suspended", selector }` beside `{ slot }`: the site showed, once the login was sent, that the account is locked or suspended, at the visible element `selector` names. The local host checks the element shows (`makeSignInRecorder` takes an optional `markerVisible`), then ends the sign-in with the new `SignInDiagnostic` code `AccountLocked` and its optional `account`, which leaves sign-in unavailable in the build. Migrate a host that reads `step.rejected.slot` by checking for `account` first, verifying the selector shows and failing the sign-in as `AccountLocked`.
- `TaskChange`'s `login` setting adds the change `signed_out`: once an update with it applies, the task runs signed out and the harness drops the unresolved sign-in. Migrate a host that maps each login change by handling it.
- `signInUnavailableSummary` words the owner's summary in plain language: it no longer names the root cause's code and phase or says that effects and receipts are preserved. The build's diagnostics keep the code and phase.
- A live example, a live test and a write session's first step keep the browser's cookies and site storage (local storage, IndexedDB, cache storage, service workers and the tab's session storage). The reset still closes other tabs, stops loading and returns the page to the site origin. A site that grants trust to a browser session keeps trusting it across resets. A browser where a sign-in started and none was verified still clears them, so a half-finished sign-in never reaches a step. Builds no longer save the session after sign-in or restore it before each reset.
  - `startStateFor` from `pomerado/core/runtime/start-state` takes `signInStarted` and `signInVerified` in place of `signedIn` and `sessionSaved`, and returns `keep`, `clear` or `none`. `restore` stays a `StartState`, `PageStart` and `resetPageCode` mode for a host's own resets, such as a run's retry. `shouldSaveSession` is removed.
  - `makeStartTracker` takes an optional `{ signInStartedBefore }`, for a browser that builds share, and tracks per browser whether a sign-in started or was verified. It adds `signInStarted`, `signedIn` and `browserReplaced()`, which a host calls when it replaces the browser, as for a new proxy: the new browser starts with an empty profile. `plan(step)` returns the `StartState` alone, in place of `{ save, start }`, and `save` and `saved` are removed. Migrate by calling `plan` for the reset mode and dropping the build-side session save.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1. No `./core/*` export is removed, and the reset and session-save helpers a host composes its own resets from (`resetPageCode`, `saveSessionCode`, `SavedSessionStorage`, `savedSessionStorageCapBytes`, `startPage` and `PageStart`) are unchanged. The changes inside `start-state` are to the build start rule and its tracker, which no known host imports yet; raising the protocol would make hosts that accept only protocol 1 refuse a package whose interface they use unchanged. A host that does call `startStateFor`, `makeStartTracker` or `shouldSaveSession` migrates as above.
  - The local host's `makeBuildStart` no longer exposes `saveSession`, and its signed-out sample hook runs after any reset before a sign-in started, as well as after a clearing one. `makeMarkerChecks`'s `afterClear` is now `afterSignedOutReset`. `makeLocalSessionSignIn` no longer takes `saveSession`.
  - A live test case keeps its browser's site data too. `LiveTestCaseRun` and `LiveTestRecord` in `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` add the optional `start`, `warm` or `cold` (type and schema `LiveTestStart`): `cold` for a case that ran first on a new browser with an empty profile. The `LiveTestHost` contract asks a host with more than one browser to run at least one case cold and to mark every case it runs. The local host has one browser and marks every case `warm`. Each version in `publication/tests.json` adds `coldPasses`, the cases that passed cold on the published source, and the coverage line says how many passed cold.
  - Guardian's `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` treats a version with no case that passed cold as a gap for the rationale, never a finding, and no longer lists the example's rerun on a fresh browser as a gap that gets a finding. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
  - The core, testing, http-mcp, writes and auth skills, the workspace guide and the `live_tests` tool description say that examples and tests keep the browser's cookies and storage, so a script sets every value its input controls itself, and a case that runs cold is the proof.
- A filled `AutofillStepReport`'s `submit` may now be `page`: on a step with fields and no submit, once every field was typed, the page moved on before the host clicked anything, as a code widget that sends itself when its last box is filled does. `fillAutofillStep` reports it when its submit call finds the step's fields gone or all disabled, its popup closed, the page's address on another path than when the host typed the last field, or every field of a step of several emptied. Such a fill used to report `refused`, `none`, or its last field `failed` with `not_attempted`. The host gives the page its settle wait, at most 2 s, to move on before it reports `none`. A submit call that fails in that wait, as one does when the page navigates or draws itself again while the call reads it, is followed by another look: the fill reports `page` when a later look finds the page moved on, and the failed call (`uncertain`, `fill_call_failed`) as before when it does not. A step with a submit is unchanged: the host still clicks it, waits on a submit the page disabled, or refuses as before, whatever the page does while it checks what was typed. Guardian's refusals come first as before: a control that moved off the site or changed how it submits still refuses the submit.
  - `maySend` from `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-refusal` is true for `page`, `fillFailure` in the run's sign-in replay passes it, so the sign-in's own check decides what the code did, a refused code included, and the minter reads its own notice for it.
  - Migrate a host that maps each `submit` value, such as to a notice or a failure, by handling `page` as a possible send that the sign-in's own check decides.
- A read signed out runs its live test cases as a batch on the HTTP version too, and each version keeps its own results: `live_tests` with entrypoint `src/tool-http.mjs` never replaces or stales the Playwright version's results, nor the reverse.
  - `LiveTestRecord` in `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` adds the optional `entrypoint` the case ran. The harness keys results by case id and the entrypoint's version, from the new `liveTestVersion` (`http` for a `*-http.mjs` file, else `playwright`; type `LiveTestVersion`). A `MintHarnessSnapshot` from an older release, whose results name no entrypoint, restores them as the Playwright version's. `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `liveTestsHttp`, the HTTP version's results, while `liveTests` keeps only the Playwright version's. A worker from an older release drops the HTTP results rather than restoring them as the Playwright version's.
  - `liveTestsEvidence` takes `versions`, one `LiveTestVersionResults` (`version`, optional `entrypoint`, `sourceDigest`, `records`, optional `replaced`) for the Playwright version and one for the HTTP version when the build has one, in place of `records`, `replaced` and `sourceDigest`. Migrate by passing the Playwright version's former arguments as one entry with `version: "playwright"`.
  - The publication record `publication/tests.json` lists only the versions that ship: the published entrypoint's, and the HTTP version beside a published Playwright version unless `finish_build` ruled it out with `httpVersion`. A version that does not ship is neither recorded nor judged. The record adds `versions`, each version's entrypoint, source digest and counts, and gives each case `results` with its result on each version in place of the case's own status fields. Each retired entry names its `version`. Its top-level `sourceDigest` and `counts` move into `versions`. The coverage line counts each version on its own. `liveTestsEvidenceProblem`'s record adds an empty `versions`.
  - Guardian's `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` judges each version's coverage, and the HTTP version's parity with the Playwright version, from that version's own results. A case that passes on one version and fails on the other is a finding on the failing one. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
  - The testing, http-mcp and workspace guidance, and the execute and `live_tests` tool descriptions, tell a read signed out to run its cases as a batch on `src/tool-http.mjs` as well, instead of one input at a time.
- Test design is for reads signed out only. A signed-in read proves its tool with its example, and a write with its act session; neither designs further tests, runs extra live tests or builds offline test suites, and each checks its HTTP version once, against that run (a write by one `savedHTTP` replay of its session). A read signed out tests its HTTP version on several inputs, as it tests the Playwright version.
  - The scope follows what the tool is now, not what the build started as. A read that signs in or becomes a write mid-build, a repair included, has `live_tests` refused from then on and publishes with no test record. A repair that rebuilds a signed-in tool or a write as a read signed out gets the testing skill and parallel live tests. Guardian judges the published version's kind and this build's verified sign-in, not the original request's kind. Only a sign-in on the current site counts: after an update moves the build to another site, the harness, like the local host, no longer counts the earlier site's sign-in.
  - `preflightTestInput` from `pomerado/core/mint/step-checks` refuses every agent-chosen `testInput` unless `signedIn` is `false`. A signed-in read ran up to four before; a host that does not pass `signedIn` is now treated as signed in. The four-test limit and its count are gone, so `executionHistory` is optional and ignored, kept only so existing callers compile; it will be removed. Migrate by passing `signedIn: false` for a read that has not signed in, and dropping `executionHistory`.
  - The testing skill, its catalog description and the core skill, workspace guide, execute and `live_tests` tool descriptions, `testInput` description, http-mcp, writes, search, pagination and publication skills say so. The testing skill's note on reporting sign-in replay as unverified moves to the auth skill. A signed-in read's `live_tests` call and a write's live example or test are refused with an instruction to publish from the one run, no longer to test offline.
  - Guardian's `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` judges a signed-in read or a write on its one run alone: it never returns a finding, or asks for another run or test, because a capability was not tested beyond that run, and treats the coverage text's untested items as disclosure. A read signed out keeps its coverage judgment; when its host keeps no `publication/tests.json`, the policy now says to judge its coverage by the same rules from the coverage text, the live tests the execution context lists and the captures. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A list cursor works for ten minutes after the call that returned it, not the hour 0.4.0 canaries gave it: `listCursorTtlMs` is 600,000, `sealListOutput` dates `next_cursor_expires_at` ten minutes ahead, and `admitListCursor` refuses as `expired` a cursor past its own expiry or more than ten minutes (and five seconds for clock skew) past its issue, so a cursor a canary host signed for an hour stops working after ten minutes too. Migrate a host that describes the lifetime by saying ten minutes, and a caller that holds cursors longer by calling again without `cursor`.
- `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` no longer exports `checklistOf`, `ChecklistItem`, `itemViews`, `ItemView`, `ItemStatus`, `repeatRuns` or `LiveTestSkip`, which 0.4.0 canaries shipped: the host derives no test checklist. A test case's `covers` is now `purpose`, and a cases file's `skipped` is now `notTested`. Migrate a canary host that read them by reading each case's `purpose` and the file's `notTested`.

- The local host stages beside authored source exactly the modules `pomerado/runtime` loads, plus `runtime/index.js`, and its own `node_modules/pomerado`, which exports only `./runtime`.
  - `privacy/*`, `runtime/browser-execution.js`, `runtime/capture-diagnostic.js`, `runtime/diagnostic-reasons.js`, `runtime/failure-detail.js` and `runtime/kernel-operation-validation.js` are no longer staged, and an import of the package's internal modules, such as `pomerado/core/runtime/operation`, no longer resolves for authored code. A saved integration that imported one fails to load.
  - Migrate such an integration by importing from `pomerado/runtime` or `../../runtime/index.js`.
- The `mint_update` refusal reason `maintenance` is now `maintenance_setting`, and maintenance adds `output_outside_maintenance` and `owner_unavailable`. Migrate by matching `maintenance_setting` where you matched `maintenance`.
- `ReviewTurn.missingRead` is now `ReviewTurn.followUp`, and `requiredReadRounds` is now `reviewFollowUpRounds`, three rounds: a reviewer sends the host's follow-up in the same review for an outcome the review's kind does not return, once, as well as for a skipped entrypoint read. `ReviewFailure` adds the code `InvalidOutcome` for an outcome still refused after that follow-up, which is not retried as an outage. A private host kind gets no follow-up and fails `InvalidOutcome` at once. `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `invalidOutcomes`, the attempt's count of such failures, which old and new workers each restore.
  - Migrate a host's own `Reviewer` by calling `turn.followUp` where it called `turn.missingRead`, for up to `reviewFollowUpRounds` rounds, and handle `InvalidOutcome` where it handles `InvalidDecision`.
- A review that returns an invalid outcome twice in a row in one attempt now ends the attempt as incomplete with no host failure, unless the host became unavailable, in which case the attempt ends with the host's failure instead. Diagnostics, including the `mint.execution_failed` event, now report `code: "InvalidOutcome"` for it. Migrate by not treating it as an outage: the first one comes back to the minter as the `mint_update` status `review_invalid_outcome`, now in `taskUpdateStatuses`.
- A maintenance `mint_update` output change may also `add` a field or `tighten` one (optional or nullable to required, or a narrower type), with no confirmation; a `remove` needs `reason`, or it is refused as `output_removal_reason` before review. `PendingTaskUpdate.maintenance.confirmer` may be `none`, for an update that only adds or tightens output fields. A host that compares a repair's output schema against an applied output change must ignore `add` and `tighten`, which loosen nothing.
- Guardian's execution review returns a required `action` label, `read`, `write` or `authentication`, in the shared output format. An execution allow without one fails to decode and is retried like an outage. An allowed `write` on a step without the new `PendingExecution.writeAuthority` becomes a denial.
  - Migrate recorded Guardian responses by adding `action` to every execution allow, and set `writeAuthority` on the steps that may write.
- `MintDependencies.reviewAndExecute`'s dispatch fence is a function of the allow it follows, so every dispatch carries a label: `beforeDispatch(allowed: AllowedExecution)`. Pass the review ID and Guardian's label, or `hostAuthentication` for a sign-in screen the host fills itself.
- The local host's blind-repeat guard and `replayedWriteStep`, `writeStepDigest` and `WriteStep` from `pomerado/core/mint/step-checks` are removed. The harness now refuses a step that repeats an earlier write's entrypoint until the outcome review finds that write did not happen.
- `sessionSentWrite` and `checkWriteSession` from `pomerado/core/mint/write-session` decide whether a write session sent its write from Guardian's labels and the outcome review, not from request counts or commit marks. They take `{ steps }` without `nonReadRequests`; each step carries its `action` label, `possiblySent` and its newest `assessment`. `write_not_submitted` means no step recorded a confirmation and no act step Guardian labelled a write may have reached the site, or the outcome review found each did not happen.
- The minter's prompts are one text for every host. These 33 named authoring sections are now shared text, so a host that composes its own text into the sections drops them. They are `core.example-input`, `core.schema-coverage`, `core.stale-session`, `core.testing-reference`, `core.completion`, `auth.login-url`, `auth.signed-out-flow`, `auth.partial-flow`, `auth.step-fields`, `auth.code-rejection`, `auth.next-screen`, `auth.signed-in-evidence`, `auth.sign-in-check`, `caller-input.declare`, `forms.saved-steps`, `pagination.no-recreated-writes`, `writes.session`, `writes.session-limits`, `writes.finish`, `writes.run-outcomes`, `publication.description-reader`, `publication.login-url`, `publication.confirm-steps`, `publication.input-feedback-end`, `publication.confirm-action`, `agents.workspace-map`, `agents.tools`, `agents.guardian-records`, `agents.guardian-view`, `agents.repeatable-reads`, `agents.write-builds`, `agents.tests` and `agents.capacity`. The other 69 sections stay. The new `core.standalone-completion` section ends the core skill with the local host's summary of its execution and completion, the text `core.completion` held before, so a host that composes its own text adds it. The new `writes.uncertain-status` and `writes.unverifiable-status` sections hold the status a run of a write reports when its outcome is uncertain, `may_have_applied` on the local host.
- `GuardianExecutionEnvironment` gains a required `dataVendor`. It holds the off-site rule's one exception, for the site's own third-party data vendor, and the evidence that shows it on that host. It follows the authorized-site rule in the execution policy. The local host's text says it keeps no network captures, so a request that carries the caller's input to another registrable domain escalates like any other caller data sent off-site.
  - Migrate by passing the exception your host can evidence. The exception 0.3.0 gave every host starts "The site's own third-party data vendor, such as a hosted search service, is the one exception" and relies on screened network captures.
- The package adds a `testing` skill, loaded after `auth`. Its sections `testing.offline-fixtures`, `testing.saved-targets`, `testing.capture-evidence` and `testing.later-cases` have no standalone text. A host that ships its own `testing/SKILL.md` drops it and composes its text into those sections instead.
- `saveSessionCode` from `pomerado/core/runtime/start-state` is now a function of the primary tab's target ID and the site's origin. Its browser call returns a `SavedSession`, `{ state, sessionStorage }`: the context's storage state, and the primary tab's session storage from its frames on the site's registrable domain.
  - Migrate by calling `saveSessionCode(targetId, siteOrigin)`, decoding its result with `SavedSession`, and passing that as the `session` of a `restore` `PageStart`.
- `resetPageCode` takes the primary origin, the site data, the session to restore and the tab storage to restore as separate arguments, then optional extra origins to clear, `cleanupOnly` and `keptCookiePrefixes`. Cookies whose names start with one of `keptCookiePrefixes` survive a clear. `PageStart`'s `restore` takes a `SavedSession`.
  - Migrate by calling `startPage`, which builds the call from a `PageStart`, or by passing the arguments in the new order.
- A sign-in through a security question is saved in `auth-fill.json` as recipe version 1, or version 2 with a popup or approval, with the question's selector on its field. It was version 3. This version still reads version 3 recipes, so nothing has to be built again.
  - 0.3.0 and every later version before this one, 0.4.0-canary.45 to 0.4.0-canary.47 included, refuse such a recipe. A run of an integration built this way fails there with `SignInRunFailed` (`MissingRecipe`, `invalid`) before it signs in or runs anything.
  - Migrate by running such an integration with this version or later, and not rolling back once one is built.
- A run reports a write that returned without recording its declared confirmation as possibly applied, not as a success. A write declared `unverifiable` reports the same. This holds for `run`, `pomerado run` and a served integration MCP.
