  - `run` fails with the new `RunOutcomeFailure`. Its `outcome` has the code `outcome_unknown`, the write status `may_have_applied`, `possibleCommit: true` and the retry class `never`. Its `unconfirmed.output` keeps what the script returned.
  - Migrate by catching `RunOutcomeFailure` and reading the site back before any retry.
- Every other run failure that is not a sign-in failure also fails `run` with `RunOutcomeFailure`, including a failure before sign-in, such as the site check, the URL or the start page. Its `outcome` names a finite code, the write status, whether a step may have committed and a retry class. A sign-in failure still fails with `SignInRunFailed`.
  - A write's declared commit steps that were never entered show that nothing was applied only when the site refused the input or the login, or the run couldn't sign in again. Any other failure after a browser step ran may have changed the website.
  - Migrate by reading `outcome.code` in place of the operation's own error.
- A served run's failed job carries `code`, `write_status`, `possible_commit` and `retry`. Its `error` is one sentence for its code, and only a possible commit tells the caller to read the site back before any retry. The sentence that a dispatched action may have taken effect no longer follows every run failure.
  - Migrate by reading `possible_commit` and `retry` in place of parsing `error`.
- `pomerado run` prints an unconfirmed write's output on stdout and its outcome on stderr, and exits 1.
- The minter changes its task with the new `mint_update` tool once the caller confirms the change: input values, a requirement, constraint or prerequisite, the purpose, a read becoming a write, the target site or the login. It replaces the write upgrade and dropping a prerequisite on an answer alone.
  - Removed: `AgentRequest.writeUpgrade`, `MintDependencies.upgradeToWrite`, the `writeUpgrade` option of `MintDependencies.reviewQuestion` and `PendingQuestion.writeUpgrade`, `MintHarnessSnapshot.writeUpgradeDeclined`, `intentWithApproval` from `pomerado/core/mint/review-context` and `writeUpgradeApproval` from `pomerado/core/mint/step-checks`. An older checkpoint's `writeUpgradeDeclined` is dropped as an excess field.
  - Migrate by implementing `MintDependencies.reviewTaskUpdate` and `applyTaskUpdate`. `reviewTaskUpdate` runs `makeGuardian(...).reviewTaskUpdate` against the same pending review the host builds for any other review. `applyTaskUpdate` stores the harness checkpoint it is given (`TaskUpdateApplication.harness`, with the update applied) together with its own bindings, rebinds everything bound to the site for a site change, reruns intake screening and the duplicate check, and resolves the login, all or nothing. It answers `applied` for `next` exactly as given, or `clarification_required` or `refused`. A takeover that restored the checkpoint answers the same `mint_update` again as `updated` without another review, by the update's `requestDigest`. Without both hooks the minter isn't offered `mint_update`.
  - Every review after an update must carry `PendingExecution.taskUpdates`, with `allowedOrigins` rebound to a changed site, and each recorded execution its `taskRevision`. `MintHarnessSnapshot.purposes` entries carry an optional `taskRevision` too. `screenedIntent` is the intent as the owner submitted it.
- `mint_update` takes a `recommend` field, the minter's own judgment: `update` keeps the same task and workflow, and `new_mint` ends the build blocked with its `suggestedRequest`. Besides `updated`, `clarification_required`, `reword`, `new_mint_recommended` and `review_unavailable`, a result may be `update_refused`, when this build can't take the change (a site change while a write session is open or once the build can't run another live example, any change in maintenance, a recommended new build included, or a host refusal), or `update_invalid`, for a request that doesn't decode or a `new_mint` recommendation without a suggested request. Nothing changes on either. A site, login or effect change without an answer in `confirmedBy` is answered `clarification_required` before any review. After a site change, `finish_build` publishes only an execution that ran on the new site (`not_published` with reason `example_before_site_change` otherwise).
- A read build that already ran a live read example can become a write through a confirmed `mint_update`. The write session takes its own example claim, so `claimExample` is called again when its first act step starts, and `finish_build` publishes only what the write session did (`not_published` with reason `example_before_effect_change` for an execution from before the switch).
- An option the minting agent wrote is the owner's words once they pick it or type it back. `ownerNamedOrigins` now takes off-site origins from every answer, picked option labels and notes that repeat an option included, and Guardian's policy says so. `AnsweredQuestion.typed` is removed, and `answersForReview` keeps a note even when it repeats an offered option.
  - Migrate by dropping any use of `typed`. A decoder that ignores excess properties drops it from a stored answer; one that refuses them needs the field removed first.
- Guardian's shared output format adds the outcomes `clarify` and `new_mint`, for the new `update` review kind. `GuardianDiagnostics` correlations add the `update` review kind.
- `JobBuildBlocked` and `MintOutcome.blocked` add the reason `new_mint_recommended`, with an optional `suggestedRequest`.
- New exports: `TaskChange`, `TaskUpdateDecision`, `PendingTaskUpdate`, `ReviewedTaskUpdate` and `taskUpdateForReview` from `pomerado/core/guardian/task-update`; `TaskUpdateRequest`, `taskUpdateStatuses`, `TaskUpdateStatus`, `TaskState`, `AcceptedTaskUpdate`, `TaskUpdateCandidate`, `TaskUpdateApplication` and `TaskUpdateHostResult` from `pomerado/core/mint/contracts`.
- A local build's step asks only the questions its entrypoint declares as a plain literal in its one `defineOperation` call. The host reads them from the source, not from the running script. An ask whose id, prompt or bounds differ fails as `Undeclared` before Guardian's question review, and nobody is asked. A declaration held in a variable, imported or computed declares nothing.
  - Migrate by writing `questions` as an object literal inside the entrypoint's `defineOperation` call.
- A step whose literal `questions` names an invalid id is refused before review, with each id it names.
- A run asks only the questions publication reviewed. A published artifact keeps them as `questions`, an empty object when there are none, and `pomerado.json` saves them. An artifact saved by an earlier release has no `questions`, so it asks only what its entrypoint declares as a plain literal.
- A publication finding carries a required `explanation`: what is wrong, the evidence and the fix, at most 800 characters. Guardian's output format requires it, and a decision whose finding has none fails as `InvalidDecision`. `finish_build` returns each finding's screened explanation and its workspace `file` beside Guardian's `path`, and tells the minter to fix every finding and call again.
  - Migrate by adding `explanation` to every `PublicationFinding` a host makes itself, and to a host's own publication decoder.
- `example_value` is no longer an input finding. An input narrowed to the example's value blocks publication with reason `source_correction`; a decision that returns it as `input_feedback` fails as `InvalidDecision`.
  - Migrate a host's own publication policy by listing only `account_specific_enum` and `input_option` under `input_feedback`.
- A publication finding in Guardian's shared output format carries `quote`, the exact text at fault, in place of `byteStart` and `byteEnd`. The host finds the quote's UTF-8 range in the file Guardian read, first exactly, then with any run of whitespace standing for another. A quote the file does not hold, or a file the host cannot read, puts the finding on the whole file, and the denial stands. The decoded `PublicationFinding` still carries `byteStart` and `byteEnd`, and a reviewer that returns a byte range keeps it.
  - Migrate recorded Guardian publication responses by replacing each finding's byte range with its quote. A host's own publication policy should ask for a quote; core exports its wording as `publicationDecisionPolicy`.
- Guardian's `read_source` tool takes a required `match`, null for a plain read. With a word or phrase, it returns `untrusted_source_matches`: the slices of the whole file around each case-insensitive occurrence, up to 20, each with its byte offset, and the match count. Guardian queries a capture or other large file this way instead of reading it whole. `publicationCaptureQueryPolicy` is the policy's wording for it.
  - Migrate recorded `read_source` calls by adding `"match": null`. A call without it still reads a chunk.
- The authored runtime's `browser/index.js` now imports `browser/outcome-wait.js`. Until hosts compute the SDK's file list from its imports, a host that copies a hand-kept list of the SDK's files into a minting workspace or a run's sandbox must copy it too, or every authored operation fails to load.
  - Migrate by adding `browser/outcome-wait` beside `browser/form-controls` in that list.
- Guardian's publication findings add the category `safety_default`: a safety default that an earlier copy of the source set and the current source drops or weakens. It blocks publication with reason `privacy`, and Guardian's shared output format lists it. A build's `publicationDenial` and a `MintHarnessSnapshot` can carry it, and an earlier version doesn't decode either one that does.
  - Migrate a host's own publication decoder by accepting the category, and a host's own publication policy by including `publicationSafetyDefaultPolicy` (see Other changes).
- Run outcomes add the code `input_not_applied`, retry class `same_key`, so a host's `FailureRenderer` must word it. A script throws the new `errors.InputNotApplied(message, { field, requested, applied?, step, siteMessage? })` when the page did not apply a valid value the caller supplied, such as a location, store, filter, sort or date it ignored, reset or replaced with its own. The runner code is `InputNotApplied`. The run fails rather than return results computed without the caller's value, and the outcome's details carry `field`, `requested`, `applied`, `step`, `site_message` and the script's `reason`. Only a read or a run that left the site untouched gets it: a write that may have changed the site keeps `outcome_unknown`, a confirmed write `execution_failed`, and so does a run that judged a step may have committed. A numeric part, such as a ZIP code typed as a number, is kept as its text. `RunEvidence` gains `unapplied_input`, and `pomerado/core/runtime/operation-failure` exports `boundedInputDetail`, `InputNotAppliedDetail` and `maximumSiteMessageLength`.
  - 0.4.0 canaries shipped this for locations only, as `errors.LocationNotApplied`, the code `location_not_applied`, the runner code `LocationNotApplied`, the error's and the local protocol's `location` detail, `RunEvidence.location` (`RunLocationEvidence`), `boundedLocationDetail`, `LocationNotAppliedDetail` and `maximumLocationMessageLength`. `errors.LocationNotApplied` stays as a deprecated alias of `errors.InputNotApplied`, the same class, so a published tool that throws it keeps running and now ends `input_not_applied`. The classifier still reads a record's `failure_reason` of `location_not_applied`, and its `location` detail when it has no `unapplied_input`, and reports it as `input_not_applied`; `runOutcomeCodes` no longer lists `location_not_applied`. The live tests still pass a case that expects `error` on the runner code `LocationNotApplied`, which an older runtime reports, and `finiteRunnerErrorCode` still keeps it.
  - Migrate a canary host's renderer by wording `input_not_applied` in place of `location_not_applied`, its record of a run by passing `unapplied_input` where it passed `location`, and a hosted runner by passing `InputNotApplied` and the error's `unapplied` detail through as it passes `InvalidInput` and its refusal. A host that stores the failure reason should accept `input_not_applied` before any runner reports it, and keep reading `location_not_applied` for records and runners from earlier releases. A tool minted with `InputNotApplied` fails as an ordinary script error on a runtime from before this change, so upgrade a host's runners before its minter.
- `waitForOutcome` (in `outcomeWaitCode` and `waitCode` from `pomerado/runtime`) now fails as `outcome_unknown` once the page shows none of its outcomes and no progress for `noProgressMs`, 8 s by default, where it used to wait out its whole `timeout`. A slow page with no sign the wait recognizes, or whose API is on another registrable domain, can now fail before its `timeout`.
  - Migrate code that relied on waiting out a long `timeout` by passing `noProgressMs` equal to that `timeout`, or by naming the site's loading sign as `loading` and the part of the page the answer appears in as `region`.
- A minter's question left unanswered no longer ends the build. `request_input` returns `no_answer` when nobody answers before the question expires, and the minter decides how to go on: with a sensible default it lists in its `finish_build` assumptions, a narrower tool, or `report_blocked`. No answer is never consent, so a `mint_update` whose `confirmedBy` names that question is refused as `clarification_required`. The read-or-write question, the capability question, any question in a write repair and any question asked while the build is signed in or holds a login still end the build as `no_response`, and so does an unanswered sign-in request. Each question is decided when it goes unanswered: the build is signed in or holds a login once the host has one bound (`websiteCredentialsAvailable`), `requestLogin` answered `supplied` or `held`, or a sign-in in the build sent one, so a build that starts signed out hands back its earlier questions and ends on one asked after it signs in. The checkpoint keeps an obtained login as the optional `loginHeld`; a checkpoint written before it existed doesn't record a login obtained through `requestLogin`, so a build restored from one counts that login only once a sign-in in the build sends it. A host with no caller to ask hands the question back the same way, by failing its `askInput` with `noResponse`; the local host now does so when its caller leaves the minter's question unanswered, where it used to report the question as not asked. An owner's cancel, such as Ctrl-C at the terminal, is a choice to stop: a host marks it with the new optional `MintFailure` field `ownerCancelled` beside `noResponse`, and the build ends as `no_response` without handing the question back. The local host marks the terminal's cancel this way.
  - At most two unanswered questions, the minter's own and its scripts' together, go back to the minter in one build; the next one left unanswered ends the build as `no_response`. The count is kept in the harness checkpoint as the optional `noAnswerHandBacks`.
  - Once a question went back unanswered, `report_blocked` with reason `policy` ends the build, so the minter can stop instead of going on past what it asked.
  - An example or `act` step whose script question goes unanswered no longer ends the build either: its result carries `scriptQuestion` with the new outcome `unanswered` and an instruction, outside a write repair and a build that is signed in or holds a login when the question goes unanswered. `ScriptQuestionOutcome` gains that variant, whose `requestId` is optional.
  - Migrate a host that handles `ScriptQuestionOutcome` exhaustively by adding `unanswered`, and a host that waited for `no_response` to end a build after an unanswered question by reading the build's own ending instead.
- A Guardian review no longer has a deadline, so a review that ran past it is no longer started again from scratch, repeating all its work. A slow review runs to its decision. It is bounded three ways instead, and none of them starts the review over:
  - Its turn limit, 12 turns by default and 32 for the core publication review, now ends in one more turn that offers no source read, with a note to decide from the evidence already in view. The review returns that decision. Only a review that still returns none fails `TurnLimitExceeded`.
  - Each model call has its own bound. A call with no response for five minutes is aborted and sent again with the same input, at most twice, while the review's reads and earlier calls stand. Each such retry emits `guardian.model_call_retried` with `attempt` and `timeoutMs`. A call that stalls every time fails the review with the new `ReviewFailure` code `ModelCallStalled`. The bound is `guardianModelCallBound` (`{ timeoutMs, retries }`, type `GuardianModelCallBound`) from `pomerado/core/guardian/model`, and a host can set its own through the new `modelCallBound` in `GuardianModelOptions`.
  - The outage retry still runs a new attempt after a provider error, a decision that does not decode or a required source read that failed. Its `budget` now counts from the first failed attempt, not from the start of the review, which no longer has a deadline, so a long review that then meets a provider error is still retried. `TurnLimitExceeded` and `ModelCallStalled` are no longer outages: `guardianOutageRetry` returns them at once rather than starting the review again.
  - `ReviewFailure`'s `reviewPhase` no longer has `review_deadline`, which nothing produces now. `guardianReviewTimeout` is deprecated and nothing reads it; it stays so a host that imports it still loads, and will be removed. The second argument of `makeOpenAIReviewer`, `developmentPublicRead`, chose a longer deadline and no longer has an effect; it stays so callers compile.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1: no `./core/*` export is removed. Migrate a host that matched `reviewPhase` `review_deadline` by dropping that case, a host that maps each `ReviewFailure` code by adding `ModelCallStalled` as a review that could not finish, and a host that bounded reviews with its own timer around a review by relying on the per-call bound instead. A host that wraps the reviewer with `guardianOutageRetry` needs no change.

- `errors.InvalidInput` keeps an explicitly empty list beside the field it names: `new errors.InvalidInput(message, { field, available: [] })` now carries `available: []`, which says the page offers nothing for that input, so a host that repairs can settle it as it settles a refusal with choices. A missing `available`, an empty list beside no field and a list of blank labels still carry none. `boundedRefusalDetail`, the local operation protocol and the local host's `input_rejected` outcome are unchanged: they carry only a list with at least one choice.
  - Migrate a host that reads the error's `available` by checking its length, not only that it is present: an empty array means the page offers no choice, not a list to show the caller.
- A write that recorded its confirmation and then failed could still be offered another run, or reported as possibly completed. A refused input, a rejected login or an unanswered question after the confirmation kept the retry class `fix_input` or `new_key`, which tells a caller to call again and so repeat the write. A lost worker after it reported `outcome_unknown`, and a record whose status was `outcome_unknown` reported `may_have_applied`, though the commit response or the page had confirmed the write. A recorded confirmation now makes the write a result in `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome`: `failedRunOutcome` and `classifyRun` give it the retry class `never` and `writeStatusOf` reports it `applied`, whatever ended its run, and its code still says what failed, so the caller sees that failure beside the applied write. A script that threw after `verified()` keeps `execution_failed`, and output that failed its schema keeps `invalid_output`; the local host already reported both as `applied` and never to run again. A browser call after `verified()` still reopens the write. The new `confirmedWrite` names such a run.
  - `writeStatusOf` now checks for a recorded confirmation before it checks for an unsettled run. A write record whose effect is `verified` reports `applied` even when its `status` is `outcome_unknown` or its run was lost, where it used to report `may_have_applied`; `runError` no longer gives such a record `outcome_unknown`, and a lost worker gives it `worker_lost`. Any other write record reports as before.
  - Migrate a host that classifies its own job records by reading a failed run's retry class from the outcome's `retry`, not from `runOutcomeRetry` by its code, and by treating a failed write that `confirmedWrite` names, or that `writeStatusOf` reports `applied`, as a landed write: never retried, and never sent to maintenance as a write that may have applied. A host that counted `may_have_applied` or `outcome_unknown` records to find writes to read back or repair now finds confirmed ones under `applied` instead, and should not read them back or repair them.
  - A host may rely on the runner's commit report (`commitReportOf`, the journal's `commits`): a mark that reads `confirmed` means the script recorded its declared confirmation live in that run, from the commit response or the page, and made no browser or HTTP call after it. A throw or output that failed its schema after `verified()` leaves the mark `confirmed` and the effect `verified`, with its confirmation kind. Any browser or HTTP call after `verified()` still resets the mark to `sent` and the effect to possibly dispatched, since that call may have changed the website again.
- A local build's sign-in follows the build sign-in rules `pomerado/core/mint` now exports, in the local host's own screen order.
  - A screen counts as sent once the host clicked its submit after the fill, the page sent what was typed itself, or the minter went on from it after the host's click failed, as a run's replay counts it. The host no longer reads the page's requests for the login. So a signed-in check no longer lists `untrustedSignInOrigins`, the local minter no longer asks the caller whether to trust an origin (`trust_sign_in_origin`), and a local mint's outcome, its summary and `finish_build`'s `autofill_recipe_not_verified` refusal name no origins. A sign-in form whose fields or submit go off the site and its sign-in origins is still refused before anything is typed, as `credential_target_refused`. `LocalMintOutcome` keeps its optional `untrustedSignInOrigins`, which the local host no longer sets, and a published sign-in saves only the request's https sign-in origins.
  - A check before a screen sent the login fails `credentials_not_submitted` with the shared notice. A marker a signed-out page shows, and a path alone that is one of the sign-in's pages (`marker_is_login_path`), are refused before the page is read, and the sign-in stays open. The check waits up to 10 seconds for a marker the page draws late.
  - A reported field rejection counts only where the screen's recorded `rejectedMarkers` entry for it shows on the page, or showed right after the host's submit of the latest screen, and a code's always does. One with no marker shown types nothing and asks nobody (`no_rejection_marker`). A rejected username or password is corrected in place twice per field at most, and each question says how many tries are left.
  - An account rejection takes no verdict from a message a signed-out page shows, or before a screen sent the identifier with a password or code, and says why in the shared notices.
  - A code, recovery code, date of birth, ZIP or security answer is asked in the words a run's sign-in uses.
  - A recorded selector or signed-in check holding `[private]` is still refused, as `selector_names_contact` or `indicator_holds_identity`. The check for the account's own values also counts a security answer, and no longer reads a security question's selector.
  - Migrate a caller that read `untrustedSignInOrigins` by passing each sign-in origin it trusts in `authenticationOrigins` up front.
- A write build declares the whole path its one write session takes before the session sends anything. A session used to end at the first step that recorded a confirmation, such as a saved draft, while the publication review could still ask for the steps after it, which the closed session could never run. `ExecutionRequest` adds the optional `writePath` (`WritePath` from `pomerado/core/mint/contracts`): `steps`, each step that saves or submits on the site, in order, by its `commit` mark and what it saves (`does`), at most `writePathStepLimit` (32), and `ends`, what the last step leaves the caller with. The local host refuses a write build's first act step without one before review, and a later act step's revised path that drops or reorders a mark the session entered. Guardian's execution review reads the path as `submitted_call.writePath`, judges it by the rule the publication review judges a write's outcome by, and denies a path that ends before the requested outcome or that no single session can reach. The host records the allowed path, and later reviews read it as `trusted_execution_context.writePath` (`MintReviewHost.writePath`, screened by `screenedWritePath` from `pomerado/core/mint/review-context`; `PendingExecution.writePath` carries the submitted one).
  - The harness keeps a session open past a confirmation while `ExecutionEvidence.writePathRemaining`, which the host sets, lists marks of the path not yet settled, and the act step's receipt adds `writePath`, those marks as not yet settled, with an instruction that asks for a read-back first where a receipt says so and never for a step that finished or may have again. Publishing refuses a session that has not entered every mark of its path with the new `MintFailure` reason `write_path_unfinished`, before it reads the composed contract, and a composed script that does not declare them all with `write_path_undeclared`. Both name the marks in the new `MintFailure.writePathMarks`. `checkWriteSession` takes the path as the optional `writePath`. Guardian's publication policy and the workspace's `reference/publication-review.md` add the rules for a write's outcome and its recorded path, and the writes skill tells the minter to settle the path before its first act step, or to end with `report_blocked` reason `site_lacks_capability` when no single session can reach the outcome.
  - Migrate a host that runs write builds by refusing an act step with `writePathRefusal` before review, recording the allowed path when the step dispatches, setting each act step's `writePathRemaining` from `writePathRemaining`, passing the path to `checkWriteSession`, and handling the two reasons where it maps publication refusals. A session started without a recorded path runs on without one. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact policy text, review request or instructions needs recording again.

- The host reads a phone field back before it clicks a sign-in step's submit, and sends nothing when the field kept other digits than the number it typed, as under a country picker the site preset to another country, whose mask shifts the number into that country's digits and cuts its end. The site then refused the number, and the owner was asked twice to correct a number that was right. `AutofillFieldStatus` adds `changed`: the fill typed the phone, typed nothing after it and never clicked the submit (`submit` `not_attempted`), so `maySend` is false, `recordStep` counts the screen as reaching nothing and `settleAutofillValues` counts the phone as never sent.
  - `CredentialKeyboard.insertText` takes optional `InsertionOptions` as its third argument, and may answer `changed` (`CredentialReadBack`). Given `{ phone: true }`, `makeCredentialKeyboard`'s keyboard reads the field back on the same private socket, from the same field, once the page had `phoneReadBackMs` (250 ms) to reformat it (`phoneDigitsCode`): the number goes in as an argument, as the insertion's did, and only a finite answer comes back. The field may drop up to three leading digits of the number, its calling code or trunk prefix, and may show up to three of its own before the rest of it, such as the picker's calling code; a number dialed with `00` or `011` may also drop that prefix. Anything else is `changed`. A read that fails, or finds no text value or an emptied field, answers `inserted`, as before, so the next call's empty-field check still reports an emptied field `failed`. The fill passes `{ phone: true }` for a phone field, and the local host's native worker passes it through.
  - The local host tells the agent with `changedPhoneNotice`, which names the number's country calling code when the number is written in international form (`callingCode`), else points to the site's own country, so it sets the site's picker with an explore step and sends the same step again. `changedField` finds the field.
  - Migrate a host by handling `changed` in any exhaustive check on a filled report's field `status`; a host that wraps the keyboard by passing its third argument through, or it reads nothing back; and a host with its own sign-in rules by refusing the step to the agent when a field is `changed`, with `changedPhoneNotice`. A keyboard of a host's own that reads nothing back never answers `changed`.
### Other changes

- A cart tool that fills the cart and places the order in the same run may use the site's guest checkout when the site offers one and the build holds no login, and the cart skill prefers that to creating an account. A cart a later call must find, such as a standalone add-to-cart or view-cart tool, still runs signed in. The cart skill's sign-in section and description, the writes skill and the workspace guide say so, and `publicationCartPolicy` accepts such a tool without a verified sign-in when its description says it checks out as a guest. No exported type changes. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact skill or policy text needs recording again.
- A write build that runs signed out, as a guest of a site that offers its task without an account, is told to do its write through its `act` steps and publish without signing in. The harness's instruction once a build gave its sign-in up (`sign_in_abandoned`, and each later `authenticate` or login question it refuses) and its notice once a task update with the `login` change `signed_out` applies pointed every build to an example and live tests, which a write never runs. A read's text is unchanged.
  - No exported type changes, and a host needs no change. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact instruction or notice text needs recording again.
- A run's sign-in gets through more often on a site that asks for a one-time code only some of the time.
  - When the build recorded no code screen and a run meets one whose code field sits in no form with a submit, such as a field that sends the code itself once it holds every digit, the replay's look for a code screen the recipe never recorded now takes it. It is the same field, matched by the same selector and word rules, filled with no submit, so the page's own send counts (`page`). Before, the look took only a field with its form's submit, and the run failed `sign_in_check_failed`. The new `findCodeScreen` in `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-unrecorded-code` does the look, and the replay and a hand-over replay's marker wait use it. `findCodeField` is unchanged.
  - A field the page doesn't send within the fill's wait ends the sign-in `sign_in_check_failed`, as before, after one code question.
  - When a recorded code screen never shows within its wait, with neither the marker nor a later recorded screen in its place, the replay no longer ends `not_found` at once. It runs its sign-in check, which includes the look above and the last check, the only one that may open the recipe's account page. It ends `not_found` as before only when that check doesn't sign in and sends no code. A write whose site skipped a recorded code screen and landed on a page without the marker now signs in at that last check.
  - A row of one-character code boxes that shows only at a run is taken too, when the field the look matches is the row's first box. The inspection's code box check, the same one that reads a recorded row's boxes, now also names each other box of that row by its own id, name or `aria-label` when the step names one code field. The new optional `AutofillInspection.codeBoxRow` carries those names: host-only, never in `screen`, so Guardian and the minter never see it, and never a recipe value. The replay then fills the whole row as it fills a recorded one: one code question, one character per box, and with no submit the page's own send counts. The row is inspected again first, and taken only when every box reads back in its place in a row of 4 to 8 and each passes the code field's word rules. A later box, a box nothing names alone, or a row of another size types nothing, and the run goes on to its last check as before. A code that does not fit the row's boxes is asked again as on a recorded row, and is never typed. The new `codeBoxRow` in `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-unrecorded-code` does this. `findCodeScreen` and the code field's selector are unchanged.
  - The auth skill no longer says runs never look for a screen the build did not record. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact auth skill text needs recording again. Recipes and tools need no change.
- The forms skill says that a file collected while a tool is being built has no `download_url` or `expires_at`, which the published tool's runs add, and that a tool prefers returning the file as collected and never requires those fields in code. For an emptied box the fill reported that box `failed` with `submit: "not_attempted"`, so the code never counted as submitted (`maySend` was false, and `recordStep` drops a slot with any field not filled), and the host refused the signed-in page it led to. Before, the harness rewrote every such report to `sign_in_unavailable`, so a build the host's own check stopped read as the site's sign-in being unavailable. A report that stands only on what may follow from the unconfirmed sign-in still ends as `sign_in_unavailable`, as before: a refused password refill, a publication or live tests the host refused, a sign-in step refused before a later screen sent the login, or a refusal kept without its source. A later refusal, such as a publication's, never hides an earlier one apart from the sign-in. Each entry is one of these:
    - `publication`, with the failure's code and reason;
    - `sign_in_step`, with the sign-in's code and the check that refused it;
    - `task_update`, with its reason, or `other` for one core does not name;
    - `sign_in_refill`;
    - `sign_in_destination`;
    - `live_tests_spent`;

The snapshot types the field as unknown, and the harness decodes it entry by entry on restore, so a source or reason a later release adds reads as `unrecorded` instead of failing an older worker's decode of the whole checkpoint during a rollout. A checkpoint that kept the refusal without sources reads as one `unrecorded` refusal. Its codes are closed: a publication's code is `PublicationUnavailable`, a sign-in step's is one of the three refusal codes, and a reason or check is a snake_case or PascalCase code of at most 64 characters.

A destination chosen any other way, such as by a form field, a cookie, a script or the server's state, still reaches the review. A click again (`resubmitAutofillStep`) and a card-fill step never report it.

Once no regular batch fits, the publication record says time ran out whether or not the reserve runs, and a refusal the host's cap caused, with time left past the batch's ten minutes, says the attempt has run every batch its host allows instead of that too little of the attempt is left. In a 2xx body that reads as JSON, a success field that is false or holds an error word, `ok: false`, an error field holding an error (empty values and words for none, such as "No error", are none), a status of 400 or more, or an error word in a status or message is not done. A success field holding anything else but true, a status or message that refuses a value ("required", "unable", "could not") or says declined, rejected or denied, words a write may itself have meant, a body that is neither JSON nor HTML, and one that can't be read within 1 s are unclear. A message is an alert, an assertive live region or anything styled as an error, with letters, outside links, buttons and labels. One styled as an error with no such words, one that mixes them with a success, one that says declined, rejected or denied, an alert that says neither error nor success, and a field still marked invalid as it was before the click are unclear. A 202, or a body whose status says queued, pending or processing, is done as `queued`.

Numbers must match exactly: a text whose numbers, read that way, are not the query's, the same ones in the same order, scores 0, so "125 Main St" never matches "123 Main St", 500000 never matches 300000, "Adults 2 Children 1" never matches "Adults 1 Children 2", "5 15" never matches 5, 1234567.8 never matches 12345678 and 14155551234 never matches 4155551234. Two words pair when they are equal, or when both are Latin letters only and the shorter, of five or more letters, is the whole start of the longer, such as Dentistry for Dentist or Dentist for Dentists, but not Employer for Employee, Country for County, Physics for Physician, Adams for Adam or Ukraine for UK. - The native executor attaches over the Chrome DevTools Protocol to a Chromium that is already running, for a host that owns that browser, such as a cloud browser that hands out a CDP websocket URL for one job. `makePlaywrightExecutor` takes `cdp: { url, targetId? }`, which can't be combined with `endpoint`. It drives the browser's own context, at the page `targetId` names or else its first page, and opens no context of its own. It refuses a page another client's context holds. It connects once, so a URL that works only once is enough. Closing it ends that connection and leaves the browser and its pages running; the browser disposes the contexts a script made when it sees the connection close. A browser that goes away fails the running call and every later one, and never replays it. The local host doesn't take `cdp`, and refuses it, because its runs close other tabs and reset site data, which would change a browser it doesn't own.
- `makePlaywrightExecutor` takes `library: "patchright"` to drive the browser with Patchright, a patched Playwright, instead of Playwright. Patchright is an optional peer dependency; install it to use the option.
- `pomerado/core/execution/playwright-execute` exports the native executor and `pomerado/core/runtime/kernel-compatibility` exports `makeKernelCompatibility`, so another host can run generated calls on a browser it attaches to.
- A script may ask its caller for a required field the site's form adds that no input covers, such as a year that a form asks for only after an earlier answer. `ScriptQuestionDeclaration` adds `{ type: "site_field" }`, which declares no prompt. Each ask passes the field's label from the page, `ask({ extra: { label } })` for a text box and `ask({ extra: { label, options } })` for a select, and the script input asks it as a text question or a choice whose prompt is the label, trimmed and shortened past 256 characters as an option's label is. A missing or empty label, or a label given to any other question, fails as `InvalidOptions`. `asksAsDeclared` allows a site-field ask only as a text question with no length bound or a choice without the caller's own words, whose prompt is at most 256 characters; every other question still matches its declaration exactly, and an undeclared one is still `Undeclared`. The host owns any wording around the label.
  - `formControlsCode` adds `unfilledRequiredFields(scope)`, which lists the visible, enabled, empty required fields in a form with their labels, a select's or combobox's options when it lists 1 to 50, and every other kind of field marked unsupported, and `fillRequiredField(scope, label, answer)`, which fills one of them and reads it back. A field marked `required` or `aria-required`, or flagged invalid by `aria-invalid`, the `is-invalid` class or the browser's own check, counts, and a control inside another, such as a combobox's own text box, counts once as that one, with that control's required mark, flag and value. A card, password, one-time code or identity number field is always unsupported, by its `autocomplete` token (`cc-…`, `current-password`, `new-password` or `one-time-code`) or by a label, name or id such as card number, card #, credit card, security code, card verification, CVV, CVN, CID, a card's expiry (next to "card", as cc exp, or as MM/YY or valid thru), SSN, a verification, authentication or SMS code, OTP, a 6-digit code, a PIN (not a PIN code, which can be a postcode), password, passport, driver's licence, national ID or national insurance number, a TIN or EIN, IBAN, bank account, routing number, routing transit number or sort code, or a few of their German, Spanish and French names, so the host's protected forms keep asking for those. So is a read-only box, and a select or combobox with two options of one label, which an answer can't tell apart. A failure is the fixed reason `field_missing`, `field_ambiguous` or `not_committed`, or one of `chooseOption`'s.
  - `fillDate`, `chooseOption` and `fillRequiredField` throw `not_committed` when the page refuses a fill or a typed key, as the helpers' fixed-reason rule says, instead of passing on Playwright's error, whose call log quoted the value.
  - `pomerado/core/runtime/script-input` exports `longestLabel`, the longest option label and site-field prompt the host takes.
  - A write may also ask for such a field after its step's click, once the page proves the click saved nothing. `formControlsCode` adds `visibleFieldLabels(scope)`, the fields a form shows by their labels and by each name and id they carry, read just before the click, and `blockingRequiredFields(scope, before)`: the fields that stopped the click when the form still shows, once, and every control it flagged invalid is an empty required field a site-field question can ask that was not among `before`, and `null` otherwise. A flagged field that was already showing proves nothing, since a save may have cleared it or the page flagged it before the click. It counts as already showing when its label, a name or an id it carries is among `before`, or its label begins with a label among `before`, so a field whose label the page rewrites as it flags it is still caught, and so does one whose label reads as an error message, such as "Required" or "This field is required.". Without `before` it throws the fixed reason `labels_missing`. The Kernel script context adds `notCommitted(name)`, and `EffectJournal` the optional `notCommitted`: the step's mark reads `not_sent` again, until it is entered again. A script calls it once the caller answered and the answers are filled, right before entering the step again, so a run that ends on the question, unanswered or declined, still reports the step possibly sent. A step goes back once a run, only when the run entered it from `not_sent` with no confirmation since, and, when the operation declares `write.commits`, only for a step it declares; an act step declares none. A second call for the same step, an undeclared name, or any other call throws `CommitAlreadySent` and leaves the step as it was, and so does every step on a host journal without `notCommitted`. The local operation child publishes the change. The `write-session.ts` example asks for a delivery phone that the review shows only once Place order is pressed, then fills it, checks the review and its total again, and clicks once more.
  - A host that records each entered mark as it happens, apart from the run's final report, should record a step going back the same way. One that doesn't keeps reading the step as sent.
  - The writes skill says to prefer the site-field question for such a field before the step's commit mark, and to ask it after the step's click once `blockingRequiredFields` returns it: fill the answer, check the step again, then call `notCommitted`, then `enteringCommit` again, and click once. It says to ask several such fields a step shows at once in one round, declaring one site-field question per field. The caller-input skill says such a second click is not a resubmission. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact writes or caller-input skill text needs recording again.

Once the answers are filled it reads the form again, and a required field the answers made it show or enable is asked in another round, still before the step's commit mark, or before its one more click after a click that saved nothing. For that read, `visibleFieldLabels(scope, { enabledOnly: true })` leaves out disabled fields, and `unfilledRequiredFields(scope, { before })` lists only the labelled fields none of whose label, name or id is among `before`, so a renamed field an input fills is not asked. A tool that declares one site-field question and asks it with `askOne` for each field runs as before.

It says a check run again once those fields were answered, before that step's `notCommitted`, is no finding. Its execution policy says the same second click is not submitting the task a second time. - `makeRunDialogDecision` takes an optional `judgeConfirm(event, confirm)`, a host's own judgment of a write's native confirm that matched no record, consulted before the caller is asked. It gets the popup as `observedConfirmOf` sees it, with a `template` naming where each of the run's own values in its message came from, and answers `"accept"` or `"ask"`. An accepted confirm is recorded with the new `DialogIncident` reason `dialog_judged_confirm_accepted`. A judgment that fails or dies counts as `"ask"`, and a confirm that cannot be expected, because no step is known or its page is not https, is never judged. - A host may also suspend a build its minter ends on a question that went back to it as no answer. `MintDependencies.suspendHandedBack` is offered the minter's own question, as an `UnansweredQuestion` with `step` `question`, when the minter ends the build with `report_blocked` reason `question_unanswered` after that question, with no other question asked or handed back since. A question that asks whether to end the build (`endsBuild`) is never offered. It answers as `suspendUnanswered` does, except that the call that stays unreturned is the report: a resumed attempt rejoins it through `agentRecovery.recoverTool` with the owner's answer as `input`, and the minter reads that answer as the report's result, told that the build has not ended. The hand-back itself is unchanged, so the minter may still go on with a default. Without the hook, or on any answer but `suspended`, the build ends blocked as before.
- A host may suspend a build on an unanswered question and resume it later. All three hooks are optional, and a host that supplies none keeps today's behavior.
  - `MintDependencies.suspendUnanswered` is offered each question whose silence would end the build as `no_response`, as an `UnansweredQuestion`: `step` (`question`, `sign_in` or `execution`), `requestId` and `executionId` when known, and `possibleCommit`. Never a question handed back to the minter as no answer, and never one the owner cancelled. When the host answers `suspended`, the run stops at once with no outcome: the call that asked never returns, so the minter's checkpoint keeps it started, no further model call starts, and the run ends interrupted, which `attemptFinished` reports as `interrupted`. `not_suspended` or a failure ends the build as `no_response`.
  - `agentRecovery.initial.resumeMessage`, and the same field on `MintAgentRecovery` that a model run's recovery receives, is told to the restored minter once, as a user message before its next model call. A saved model response replays first, with its tool calls rejoined through `recoverTool`, and the message follows their results. A trace record with phase `continuation` and reason `resume_message` marks where it went.
  - `GuardianSessionOptions.resumeNote` is added once to a restored Guardian conversation, as a message before its next request.
  - A `suspended` answer that arrives after the model run ended is recorded and ends the build as `no_response`, since nothing can stop the run any more. An execution recorded again, as a resumed attempt's rejoined call is, replaces its entry in the harness checkpoint, so a host that saves the harness inside its hook gets it once. Only checkpoints from the next model call on hold `resumeMessage`: a takeover that restores an earlier checkpoint passes it again, and one that restores a later checkpoint does not.

The testing skill and its catalog description, the workspace guide, the `live_tests` and `testInput` descriptions and Guardian's publication policy say so.

- `pomerado/runtime` adds `pickerCode`, page code for a Kernel call body that declares `pickValue`, `pickValues` and `pickerOptions`. `pickValue(control, wanted, options)` chooses one value in any picker: a native select, an input with a datalist, an autocomplete or typeahead, an ARIA combobox, a dropdown built from divs (a menu with no ARIA roles included) or a lookup dialog with a search box and a results table, detected from the control or set as `options.kind`. It types the value and waits for the options to settle (no loading sign, no request it started in flight, the list unchanged for `settleMs`), types again key by key when the field ignored the first typing and once more when a slower answer to an older query replaced the list, reads a list that renders or loads as it scrolls to its end before matching (narrowing a list too long for the budget with its search box), ignores repeated background requests such as pollers (requests count as the site's when they go to `options.siteDomain`, else a conservative guess from the page's host), never picks an "Add" or "Create" row, then clicks the option (or moves to it with ArrowDown and presses Enter only once its keys made it active), blurs or tabs out, and confirms the field kept the choice for `holdMs` after the page's requests end, picking again up to `refills` times when the page puts the old value back. Options match by any name they show or carry (text, first line, accessible name, `title`, a table row's cells) or their value attribute: exactly, then ignoring case and spacing, then accents and punctuation, then by whole words, and options that tie at the best rank are ambiguous. A value the picker lacks, ties or disables comes back as `{ picked: false, reason, wanted, available, matches }`; decode it with the new `PickOutcome` schema and throw `pickRefusal(outcome, field)`, an `InvalidInput` with `field`, `available` and the reason as `kind`. Page and budget problems throw a `PickerFailure` whose message is a fixed reason (`wanted_missing`, `popup_missing`, `options_loading`, `not_committed` or `timeout`). It looks through open shadow roots and works in the control's own frame. `pickValues` adds or replaces chips and tags, or options of a `select[multiple]` (`PickManyOutcome`), and `pickerOptions` lists a picker's options for a caller question. `rankChoices` and `pickerRanks` export the matching rules for options read another way, such as from a site's API.
- The forms and core skills choose picker values with `pickValue` and refuse a value the picker lacks with `pickRefusal`, instead of describing the steps for each picker. The forms skill adds the `references/picker.ts` example.
  - A recorded minter response keyed on the exact forms or core skill text needs recording again.
- `pomerado/runtime` adds a pager for list UIs. `paginationCode`, pasted after `waitCode` in a Kernel call body, declares `listPager(spec)`, which finds a list's page numbers, Next and Previous controls (one control when the site repeats them above and below the list), "Load more" control, scroll, page indicator, page-size select and results summary near the list's rows, steps through them and returns each page's rows as `waitForRows` reads them. It judges a step by what changed, the rows' keys, new rows or the site's empty message, never a sleep, and reports the summary's total only when the page agrees with it. `read`, `next`, `goToPage` and `replay` return one segment; `collect` and `find` walk the list within one call, each row once, and say why they stopped; `detect` reports the mechanism and controls without acting. Every control can be named and each judgement replaced with a hook. `pagedListReader` gives `readList` the pager's site actions, so a list tool writes only its selectors and how the list starts. The pagination skill points to it and to the new reference `pager.ts`. Also exported as `pomerado/core/browser/pagination`.
  - The pager no longer reads a page number from a `pg` query parameter.
- `waitCode` from `pomerado/runtime` races more kinds of answer after an action, and waits for a control to be ready before one:
  - `waitForOutcome` takes, beside locators, an answer that names one declared kind: `{ gone }` (a form or dialog closed), `{ invalid }` (a field marked invalid by `aria-invalid`, `is-invalid` or the browser's own check, with its label and message), `{ url }` (a navigation or route change after the action), `{ response }` (a response a function or `{ method, url, status, navigation }` accepts), `{ dialog }` (a native dialog, never handled by the wait), `{ popup }`, `{ announced }` (text a live region announced, so a toast gone before a look still counts) and `{ check }` (a condition of your own). They race in the listed order, so a request the site batches, caches or renames never hangs the wait. A look the page holds up, behind a native dialog or a pending navigation, ends once such an event answered, and every listener the losing answers used is removed before the wait returns.
  - `waitForOutcomeResult` is the same wait returning `OutcomeResult`: the winning answer, its kind, its `evidence` (a locator's visible text, the invalid fields, the URL, the response's method, URL and status, the dialog's type and message) and the Playwright `response`, `dialog` or popup `page`, kept off its JSON. `waitForOutcome` still returns the name alone.
  - `waitForReady(control, options?)` waits until the control's one visible match is enabled, not busy, still, and not covered by another element at its centre, while the overlays it names show, the page's own pending count it reads is above zero, and an `until` condition is false. After anything held it back, ready must hold for `stableMs`, so a spinner that flickers off is not taken for gone. It opens a collapsed section through the toggle you name, once, and clicks the close control of a tour, what's-new dialog or banner that opens on its own, during the wait and, through a Playwright locator handler, before later actions. It fails as a `ReadyWaitFailure` (`not_ready`, `ready_timeout` or `ready_ambiguous`) naming what held it back, and `waitReport()` lists it.
  - The outcome and readiness waits take `deadline`, a `Date.now()` time they never outlast, so an action that took long cannot push the wait past the call's budget.
  - A failed run whose Kernel call ended in `not_ready` or `ready_timeout` is a settled browser action timeout, as `outcome_unknown` and `values_loading` are.
  - The core skill points readiness at `waitForReady` and response waits at a `{ response }` answer; the writes skill's commit-response confirmation and its reference `write-readback.ts` race the commit response against the form's invalid fields in one `waitForOutcomeResult`; the forms skill names a banner's control as `dismiss`.
- The local host loads the page you gave before the build starts, once the build's read or write is known, and tells the build how it loaded. Before, the first live step that did not reset the page loaded it. The load is one browser call that waits for the document, not every resource, for up to 30 seconds; before, it waited for the full load for up to 60 seconds. A load that fails, or that the site answers with an error status, no longer fails anything: the build runs, and its first request tells it whether the page opened, its status and where it landed, in the harness's existing entry notice. After a load that fails, the host stops the tab's loading, so the browser's late error page can't cut off a step's own load. A build whose first live step resets the page now loads the page you gave too, and Guardian's `browser` state reads `active` from the first review after the build's read or write is known.
  - `pomerado/core/runtime/start-state` exports `loadEntry`, the browser call `entryNavigationCode` and its answer schema `EntryAnswer`, the outcome builders `answeredEntry` and `failedEntry`, and the types `EntryNavigationOutcome` and `EntryNavigationFailureKind`.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/contracts` exports `mintEntryNavigation`, which turns an outcome into the `MintEntryNavigation` a host passes as `MintDependencies.entryNavigation`.
  - A recorded minter or Guardian response keyed on the exact text of the build's first request, its first live step's result or a review before that step needs recording again.
- `pomerado/core/mint` exports the build sign-in rules every host's build sign-in follows, in its own screen order. They are `autofill-results` (the `AutofillHost` state the rules read and update, their results and notices), `autofill-guards`, `held-rejections`, `autofill-rejected`, `autofill-filled`, `autofill-account-rejected` and `signed-in-marker`. `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-state` adds `exhaustedSecret`. No export changed.
- After a compaction, the minter is told which skills it had read. The compaction leaves the skill pages it read out of view, and the host used to say nothing about them. The request after the compaction item now carries a note naming each catalog skill whose `.agents/<name>/SKILL.md` a successful `read_source` returned before the compaction, and inviting the minter to read any of them again when it needs an exact rule. A failed read and the model's own claim don't count, and with no skill read no note is sent. Both the idle compaction and the provider's backstop compaction get it, built once per compaction so later requests keep their cached prefix. `MintDependencies.compactionSkillsNote` and `MintTurn.compactionSkillsNote` let a host word the note itself.
  - A recorded minter response keyed on the exact input after a compaction needs recording again.
- A live test's `next_page` case and the list read's shape check read only the runtime's list cursor: the string input `cursor` and output `next_cursor`, as `listInputFields` and `listOutputFields` give them. `cursorPairOf` from `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` used to also take `page_token`, `next_page_token`, `continuation` and similar names, and numbers, so a tool could keep a continuation of its own under another name; such a tool now gets `no_cursor` for a `next_page` case. The minter's `no_cursor` instruction names the list fields.
  - A recorded minter response keyed on the exact `no_cursor` instruction needs recording again.
- The authoring guidance asks for faster tools, fewer test rounds and fewer owner questions, without weakening any check:
  - The core and search skills wait for a change only after a step that changed the page or should have, and skip a second change wait when nothing changed; the values a tool returns still get their one `waitForRows` or `waitForValues`, and a value settles on two consecutive identical reads. A choice already in the requested state gets no step, and an unset optional one is read from the page where it shows.
  - A challenge the build saw is named as one answer of the same `waitForOutcome`, never probed separately on every pass of a home-made loop or after a swallowed timeout.
  - The core skill prefers the page's own structured data to its rendered cards when it holds the same fields, reading a change's own data response after an in-page change. A list of texts read from a page drops blank entries, and a text field that can be blank on some records is nullable.
  - The workspace guide enters at the results URL, with filters, sort or location in it, when the mapping from the tool's inputs is reliable and their valid values are known and readable; otherwise it uses the page's controls. The search skill makes the fast path the first design to try: load the results URL (setting a location through the page first when needed), then read the page's own data in the same session. Its main path never opens, clicks or waits on an optional control the caller left unset, and testing covers the all-unset path.
  - The search skill offers a ZIP or store input when results depend on one and always returns the applied one. A query the site corrected or rewrote returns the site's results with `applied_query` and `query_corrected`, a fifth case beside those the workspace guide and publication skill list.
  - The testing skill and the live test instructions fix everything a batch reveals before running again, rerun on each shipping version whose source the fixes changed, and read per-step timing where a result reports it. A slow tool still publishes.
  - The workspace guide asks only what exploring cannot answer, batches foreseeable questions early, asks about optional capabilities as one `multi_choice`, and asks again at most once when an answer leaves a choice open. The caller-input skill asks each decision once per build and keeps a declared question unchanged so its earlier answer is reused, never for a secret, code or login.
- The search URL fast path above, entering at a results URL with filters, sort or a location in it, is off unless a host passes `{ searchUrlFastPath: true }` to `loadAuthoringSkills` and `loadWorkspaceGuide`. Off, the URL guidance reads as it did before the fast path, and the search skill still reads the page's own data after reaching the results through the page's controls. In both modes, the search skill prefers catching the page's own answer to a data request, and fetches it in the page to speed things up or when that answer can't be caught, after the page has reached the results.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/skills` exports the options type, `AuthoringOptions`.
- The local host's publication review sends the small files it reads first in its request, as `trusted_publication.inline`, so Guardian decides without a turn to read each: the public definition, the entrypoint, the output digest and a read's example output, each up to 16 KiB, by its path, exactly as `read_source` returns it, a JSON file parsed. A file whose read fails or takes more than one chunk is left for Guardian to read. The review's notes start with one sentence saying the inline files need no read. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact review request needs recording again.
  - `PublicationScope` adds the optional `inline`. `pomerado/core/mint/publication-review` exports `inlinePublicationPaths`, `inlineFilesNote` and `withInlineFiles` for a host that builds its own publication request.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/review-context` exports `trackedWrites`, the list of tracked writes an execution review carries, for a host that builds its own review context.
- `pomerado/core/mint/script-answer-reuse` exports `validateKeptAnswers`, which checks answers a host kept by the rules of a fresh answer.
- A read is no longer sent to publication review while its live tests mostly fail. `finish_build` is refused before review, as `PublicationUnavailable` with reason `live_tests_below_threshold` and recovery `new_observation`, when fewer than 70% of the counted cases pass on the version it publishes, the entrypoint's. Counted cases are the cases in `test/cases.json` whose latest result on that version's published source is a pass or a fail: inconclusive results, stale and not-run cases and retired cases count neither way, and a version with no counted case passes. An HTTP version beside a published Playwright version is not held to the bar: the review judges it from its own results, as before. Once the attempt ran out of time for live tests, as a batch refused as `no_time` or a case stopped by the attempt's deadline shows, or when no further batch fits in what is left, as after a host's batch cap, no case can run again, so the bar does not apply and the review decides as before. The harness reads that last condition with the same check that refuses a batch as `no_time`. The minter is told how many passed and the bar, and to fix the code where a case fails, or the case where it is wrong, then run every case again. No Guardian call is made for the refused submission. The refusal is the minter's to fix, so it grants no `host_refused` ending. The bar applies to every build of a read that runs live tests, a host's repair of a published read included. A signed-in read is held to it on the cases it ran signed in, the only ones its record holds, on a host that runs signed-in batches. A write keeps no test record, so it is unchanged.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` exports `reviewPassPercent` (70), `liveTestsShortfall` and its `LiveTestsShortfall` result.
  - The testing skill states the bar, so the minter knows it before its first `finish_build`.
- A signed-in read can test the optional inputs its example left unset, in one combined live case, on a host that opts in; before, it proved its tool with the example alone and its filters shipped untried. Nothing changes for a host that does not opt in: a signed-in read there still runs no live tests (`live_tests` answers `unavailable`), and `preflightTestInput` refuses its `testInput` as before. The local host opts in.
  - A host opts in with the new optional `LiveTestHost.signedInBatches: true`, which says it runs a batch marked `signedIn` (new optional `LiveTestBatch.signedIn`) on the build's own signed-in browser, warm, never on a new browser, which holds no session. The harness then asks such a batch for one worker.
  - On an opted-in host, the harness offers a signed-in read `live_tests`, and an `execute` test on an input it chose, on at most two distinct inputs in a build or repair (`signedInTestInputLimit` from `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests`), counted across both and across a takeover. A batch or step that would add a third input runs nothing: `live_tests` answers `status: "limit"`, and `execute` refuses the step before review. Running a counted input again, after a fix or on `src/tool-http.mjs`, is free; inputs that differ only in key order are one input (`testInputKey`). Only inputs that Guardian allowed and that ran count. A write still runs none.
  - `preflightTestInput` from `pomerado/core/mint/step-checks` takes the optional `signedInTests`. With it true it lets a signed-in read's `testInput` through to the harness's bound; without it, it refuses as before.
  - On an opted-in host, a signed-in read's publication carries the host's record of its live tests (`publication/tests.json`) once a case ran signed in, holding only results run signed in, marked `signedIn` with a note on the bound. `LiveTestRecord` adds the optional `signedIn`, and `liveTestsEvidence` takes the optional `signedIn`. A read that signs in mid-build, a repair included, is held to the bound from then on, and its cases from before the sign-in no longer count for it, in `plan` or in the record. `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `signedInTestInputs`, the inputs counted so far; a worker from an older release drops it and runs no live test for a signed-in read.
  - Guardian's `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` judges a signed-in read on its example and those cases: a failing case, or a retired one whose last result failed, is a finding as for a read signed out, while what the read did not test, its `notTested` and its cases not run stay disclosure, never a gap. A write is judged on its one run as before. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
  - To opt in, run every case of a batch marked `signedIn` on the build's own browser, set `signedInBatches: true` on the host's `LiveTestHost`, and pass `signedInTests: true` with `signedIn` to `preflightTestInput`.
- The testing skill asks every read for a few combined live cases rather than many narrow ones. Its first case sets every optional input and filter the example left unset, together, with values the site shows, such as a picker's options or values in the example's own rows, each chosen so at least one example row fails it while some rows still pass: a value every example row already satisfies would pass even when the tool ignores it. The minter names in each case's purpose the row each value excludes, and checks that every returned row satisfies each input it set and that the excluded rows are gone. The case runs page two when the tool pages and the example did not, and the minter checks that page two holds new rows. The core skill now says free text takes a `maxLength` no longer than the site's own field. A second case is added only for inputs that cannot be set together. A read signed out adds a handful more: the example's input once more, run cold where the host can, a no-results case that also carries a made-up free-text value, and other cases only where a different page structure or real variant needs one. "About 10 to 20 cases" and three live cases for every free-text input are gone: a blank or over-long value is refused by the input schema before the site is touched. The skill's catalog description, the core, search, pagination, forms, publication and http-mcp skills, the workspace guide, the `execute`, `testInput` and `live_tests` descriptions and the harness's test plan reminders say the same.
- Guardian's publication review honours a caller's confirmed decision on how the tool behaves, from the build's explicit request, an answer or a task update, as an exception to the API-design and output-contract rules, such as blank fields returned as empty strings, an output shape or an option fixed for every run. It never excuses a privacy, credential, security, correctness or write-safety rule, and on a write it may only decline or turn off a paid add-on, paid upsell, payment method or delivery address, never accept, keep or choose one for every caller (the new shared `publicationPaidChoiceLimit`). A description or note that claims a decision establishes none. `publicationOutputPolicy` now ends with the new `publicationCallerDecisionPolicy`, so hosts that compose their own policy from it get the rule; its Loosened needed value rule yields only to such a decision. The core policy no longer counts a caller's design decision copied into source as hard-coded data, or a choice the request fixes for every run as an `input_option` finding. The authoring core skill tells the minter to build the form the owner chose and say so in the field's description.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again. A host whose own policy repeats the core sentences on answers copied into source, or on settled choices as `input_option` findings, should drop them in favour of the new rule.
- A temporary, narrow exception for choices on a site's own sign-in pages, such as a location the site asks for before it signs in, which the host's sign-in keeps in its sign-in recipe and the minter cannot edit. `decodePublicationDecision` sets an `input_option` or `example_value` finding in the recipe aside, in the decision's new optional `excusedSignInChoices`, when the decision is a denial for a source correction, input feedback or a host-owned file; with nothing left it allows. Guardian's shared `publicationDecisionPolicy` adds `publicationSignInChoicePolicy`, which says such a choice is no `input_option`, `example_value` or Unapplied input finding and that it excuses nothing about the tool's own behavior, output or source. Every other finding, in the recipe or any other host-written file, blocks as before. The exception ends when sign-in page choices become declared sign-in inputs.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Site identity has one configurable module, `pomerado/core/runtime/same-site`, and every site check in the package uses it: Guardian's authorized site and owner-named origins, where a sign-in's values may be typed, the tab storage a saved session keeps, a file input's frame, the signed-in marker's frames, list cursors, workspace hosts and the script context's `siteDomain`. A host may add its own private suffixes with `configureSiteIdentity({ privateSuffixes })`; under each, every label directly below the suffix is its own site, the way each github.io page is. An entry that is empty, not a lower-case host name, or a public suffix such as `com` or `co.uk` throws `InvalidPrivateSuffix`. Guardian's `allowedSites` entry for a site with an added suffix below its domain lists it as `addedPrivateSuffixes`; the policy text is unchanged. The module adds `siteOf`, `registrableDomain`, `makeSiteIdentity`, `siteIdentity`, `separateSuffixes`, `hostOnSiteSource` and `publicSuffixListed`; `sameSite` and `siteDomain` keep their signatures. With no added suffix every result is unchanged, so a host that never configures one sees no difference. A unit test refuses a suffix-list lookup or a hand-written host suffix check outside the module.
- A tool run's sign-in replays its recipe on the sign-in engine every host shares, `replayAutofillRecipe` from `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-replay`, with its state, value asks and rejection reads in `autofill-state`, `autofill-values`, `autofill-fresh-values`, `login-field-asks`, `autofill-rejection`, `autofill-correction`, `run-login` and the `autofill-replay-*` modules under `pomerado/core/runtime/`, and `autofill-refusal-evidence` under `pomerado/core/destinations/`.
  - A host passes its page hooks (`AutofillHooks`), its asker and its masking (`SecretBroker`). What it keeps besides the login, such as its own codes or saved answers, plugs in through the optional `hosted` set (`HostedSignIn`); the local host passes none. `configureSignInTelemetry` from `pomerado/core/runtime/sign-in-telemetry` sets where the engine's reports and stage timings go; unset, they go nowhere.
  - The local host's run sign-in and the build's automatic sign-in now behave as the shared engine does. A value counts as sent once the host clicked the screen's submit after filling it, or the page sent the screen itself, whatever the page's requests carried. A login screen that comes back after its password counts as a rejection only when the site shows the recipe's recorded error marker; without one the call fails with `RecipeFailed`. A rejected username or password is asked again at most twice per field, with the tries left, counted across the run's sign-ins until one verifies. A read's sign-in whose sign-in page sends a signed-in browser on to a page that can't tell, such as an account picker, now counts as signed in there before typing anything. The code, date of birth, ZIP and security question asks use the engine's wording, a code that doesn't fit a screen's code boxes is asked again twice, and a security question shown again after its answer fails the sign-in check. A screen check whose page call failed is retried once, and the final check waits up to 10 seconds for a marker drawn late. The look for an unrecorded code screen runs only on a recorded screen's origin.
- The shared sign-in engine no longer opens the recipe's account page (`openPath`) to check a session while it waits for a screen, or before its code probes. Only the host's last check may open it, so a write whose landing page shows no marker now waits out the marker wait before that check. On a recipe with an account page, a local read that sends its login and lands on a page without the marker now counts as signed in there, as a page that can't tell, once that page stayed put 1.5 seconds. It no longer opens the account page to verify. `AutofillHooks` adds the optional `startedEmpty` and `pageState` (`PageState`), and the engine input adds the optional `readSignedOut`. `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-replay-pages` no longer exports `settledSessionLanding` or `sessionAfterMarker`.
  - A read's check without values may end on a login entry that can't tell: the entry page's shape (`pageState`, else its address) stayed unchanged for 1.5 seconds, or until the screen's wait ended, showing no screen, marker or password field. It never does in a browser that started empty (`startedEmpty`) or right after a read of the page signed out (`readSignedOut`). The replay then returns `signed_in` with `alreadySignedIn` and `cantTell`, and reports `cant_tell_entry`. The local host sets `startedEmpty` on every sign-in, so a local call never ends there.
  - The check without values holds a screen it found for 1.5 seconds before it acts on it, and the page may leave meanwhile. A fill whose screen went with nothing sent waits once more for the page, as `screen_gone`, and holds the screen the same way if it shows again.
  - Once the login went out, a screen's wait also ends on a page off the sign-in screens that can't tell, as when a site skips a recorded code screen, for a read's last check to decide. A write's sign-in still needs its marker, so on such a page it waits out the skipped screen.
  - A local run's first sign-in on a browser the host already typed a sign-in into, such as a library scope's run after its build, first checks the recipe's account page when it has one. It waits up to 10 seconds for a marker drawn late, and types nothing when the marker shows there. A new browser, as `pomerado run` and each served call use, replays at once. A recipe with a workspace relation always replays, so on such a browser a write whose login address sends it to a workspace page without the marker fails its sign-in.
- `exampleOutputRecord` in `pomerado/core/mint/publication-review` puts each field of the example output record, and each record of a top-level list in the output, on its own line, still compact JSON with no indentation. Below the levels the output digest reads lists at, each value stays whole on one line. The text without its newlines is what `JSON.stringify` writes. A later publication round then diffs a rerun of the same output in a few hundred bytes instead of sending the whole record again. A changed record's diff also carries the records beside it, so it grows with their size. The 96 KiB cut counts the newlines and still keeps as many whole records as fit.
- A local write's failed run is `not_applied` whenever its child process reported none of its declared commit steps entered, whatever failed it, unless it recorded its confirmation. Before, only a refused input or login counted. A failure the host raised without that report, such as its deadline, and an error the child sent while a host request was still out keep `may_have_applied`. A script that fails on its own unanswered question, or a run that fails after a popup's question went unanswered, reports `no_response`. The popup's case is new. Such a write is never `not_applied`, since the local host keeps no log of what the page sent: it reads `applied` once it recorded its confirmation, `not_attempted` when it sent nothing, and otherwise `may_have_applied`. `unenteredWrite` in `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome` names the rule for any host.
