  - The new minter tool `live_tests` has two actions. `plan` returns the agent's cases in `test/cases.json` and each case's latest status, result and failure, with the authored source line that threw. `run` runs the named cases, or all of them, as one batch after one Guardian review, each case on a page reset like the example's, and returns every result. A case may also run the next page through the tool's own cursor. The first passing example's receipt reminds the agent to design and run its cases once the schemas are settled.
  - Each case says in `purpose` what it establishes, and the file's optional `notTested` lists what the agent chose not to test, each with `what` and `reason`. The host derives no checklist: the testing skill guides the agent's design, with every input alone and together, smart combinations of controls, different real values unlike the example's that the site lists (another retailer, store or record with another layout), a value the site does not offer, no results, the next page, a location applied and one the site cannot apply, and one rerun of the example's input on a fresh browser. A read is a list read by its shape, an output that is an array or a results list, and `primaryListOf` names that list; a details record with image or variant arrays stays a details read, and an empty array inside it is not an empty result.
  - `MintDependencies.liveTests`, a `LiveTestHost`, runs the batches; `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` holds the cases file schema, `runLiveTestCase` and the verdicts. The local host offers it and runs a batch one case at a time on its one browser. A host may run a batch on several fresh browsers at once. `LiveTestHost.caseTimeoutMs` names how long one case may run on that host, `defaultLiveTestCaseMs` (60 s, the local host's) when absent, and the harness refuses a batch as `no_time`, running nothing, when less than that is left before the batch's deadline.
  - Guardian reviews a batch once, as `currentExecution.input` `agent_chosen_batch` with every case's id and input listed, and the cursor input of each case that also runs its next page (`batchReviewInput`), judging each case under the agent-chosen input rules. The new mark is also a history entry's `input`.
  - Results never refuse publication by themselves. At `finish_build` the harness gives `publish` its record of the cases on the source being published, as the new optional third argument `hostEvidence.liveTests`, and adds a line to coverage. The local host's publication review reads it as the host-owned file `publication/tests.json`, and Guardian judges the cases there against the tool's claims and the captures: a failing case, or a claimed capability no passing case tests while time remained, is a finding. The coverage line counts the cases as pass, fail and inconclusive. Each case's output gets the same output check as an example's: the agent reads its findings, with their meaning and a screened sample, in the case's `outputChecks`, and the record keeps each finding's path, check, count and whether it blocks. A case's findings never refuse publication; the example's still do at `finish_build`. A case that expects `error` passes only on a declared refusal, `InputNotApplied`, or `LocationNotApplied` from an older runtime; any other throw fails it. A failure stays in the record when the case later passes unchanged on the same source, marked `flaky`, and a retired failure carries `excusedBy` only when a case passes the same input with the same expectation on the published source after the source changed. The record keeps retired cases, ones the agent deleted or changed after they failed, with their last result and input. It says `outOfTime` when a batch was refused for time or a case was stopped by a batch deadline the attempt's own end set, never by a batch's ten-minute cap; then, and for gaps whose cases were only inconclusive, Guardian notes the gaps without refusing, and a repair is never refused for not repeating the example. A record the host could not build says why instead of being left out. A host with its own publication policy can include the new export `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` from `./core/guardian/publication`. `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `liveTests`, each case's latest result and the replaced failures before them, and `liveTestsOutOfTime`, which old and new workers each restore. A case's digest covers its input, expectation and next page, not its purpose, so rewording a purpose keeps its result.
  - `preflightTestInput` takes an optional `signedIn`. Only false, a read signed out, runs agent-chosen live tests, with no limit. The execute tool's description promises batches only when the host offers `live_tests`.
- A choice the request, the caller or the owner declined is never an input of the tool. The skills say so, and the publication policy makes such an input a source correction. The rules that make every control an input, in the search and publication skills and Guardian's "Unapplied input" finding, make the same exception, and Guardian's question review never allows asking about a declined choice again.
- A local operation's failure names the authored source line that threw, such as `src/tool.mjs:12`, as `LocalOperationFailure.frame`.
- List tools page. `pomerado/runtime` adds `listInputFields`, `listOutputFields`, `startList`, `readList`, `selectRows` and `finishList`, with `ListMechanism`, `ListPosition`, `ListSegment`, `ListReader`, `CursorRefusal` and the limits `listLimitDefault` (20), `listLimitMax` (50), `listCursorTtlMs` (ten minutes), `listCallBounds` and `listDepth`. The new `pomerado/core/runtime/list-cursor` gives hosts `admitListCursor`, `sealListOutput`, `listCursorKeys` and `randomListCursorKeys`; a host signs each run's next cursor and checks a returned one before the run starts, so a script never makes or reads a cursor. The local host does both for `run`, `pomerado run`, served integrations, the minter's own steps and each case of a live test batch, so a `next_page` case's page two runs from the cursor its page one returned, and `PomeradoOptions` adds `listCursors` for its keys and clock.
  - A host that adopts this release's pagination skill must check cursors with `admitListCursor` before a run and sign outputs with `sealListOutput` after it. The checked position reaches the script on a host channel beside its input, never in the input: `executeKernelOperation`'s browser binding and `runLocalOperation` take `list`, `executeOperation` takes `{ list }`, and `withListHost` (`pomerado/core/runtime/list-page`) attaches it. A run without it refuses any cursor that would continue a runtime list, and its `finishList` returns no next cursor (`next_cursor_unavailable: "unsigned_host"`).
  - A cursor is bound to the tool, the inputs other than `limit`, `cursor` and `include`, the caller the host names as `ListCursorScope.subject` (refused as `other_account` for any other) and ten minutes after the call that returned it. Its body is signed, not encrypted: it shows the site's own link, token and scope for the next page. The tool, inputs and caller appear only as digests keyed by the signing key.
  - A cursor the host did not sign, a runtime draft sent in as a cursor included, is refused as `malformed`, and a next cursor that is not a runtime draft is dropped (see Breaking changes).
  - `readList(list, reader)` reads one call's page from site actions a tool gives it: `open` reaches where the call starts, the first segment or the one the position names, `more` reads more of the open segment without navigating, and `next` moves to the next site page, API response or "Load more" step or scroll. It asks for exactly the rows the window needs, reads on while rows are lacking within `listCallBounds` and the run's time, and writes the next position: the segment that holds the last returned row (its site link and page number, the API token, or the fewest steps that load it) and the offset after that row. A call's `limit` and the site's page size need not match: with `limit` 20 over 30 rows per site page, page two returns site page one's rows 21 to 30 and site page two's rows 1 to 10. Rows inserted above the last returned row move the window down its segment, and the call still returns `limit` rows when its reads allow. Every count `readList` asks a reader for is at most 1,000, or the last segment's rows read plus `limit` when that is more; `open` asks for the position's offset plus `limit`.
  - When the segment a later page opens shows neither the last returned row nor any row the cursor remembers, `readList` looks for them within `listCallBounds`. For `pages`, `next_link` and `api` it reads up to two segments on, since rows inserted above push them on, keeping a read to start over. It also reads the previous segment by its page number, which finds them after removals above of up to about a segment's rows, and resumes there. A reader reaches a page by its number by following Next from the first page, so that read costs one read per page: when the previous segment is the list's first it is read before looking on, since a start over needs it too; an earlier one is read after looking on, only when it fits within the call's reads with one left to start over, never deep in a list. When neither direction shows them, it starts over from the list's first segment in the same call: after the last returned row or a remembered row when that segment shows one, else from the list's first row, repeating rows rather than skipping any. A step back or a start over sets `list_changed`. A start over, or a page continued from a segment's first row with neither the last returned row nor any remembered row in sight, can return rows returned before the 16 the cursor remembers, so the cursor then carries a flag and `list_changed` is true on that page and on every later page from it; callers should de-duplicate by key once `list_changed` is true. `finishList` takes `mayRepeat` for it. A call returns no rows while the list has more only when the run's time or the call's reads stopped it; reads that ran out before the start over hand back the list's first segment. `finishList` takes `resumeAfter`, the key of the row a call that returned no rows resumes after when that is not the previous page's last row, such as a row returned earlier that it read past, so the next cursor's anchor matches its position. `ListReader.open` may get a position with only a `page`: for `api` without a token, fetch the responses up to that page. A cursor remembers the last 16 rows returned across calls, not only the last call's.
  - When the run's time stops a call before it reads past its position, its next cursor holds that same position with `has_more` true; `finishList` takes `bounded` for it. A second such call in a row at that position ends the list with `no_progress`, so a caller paging until `has_more` is false still ends when every run's time runs out before it reads. Otherwise only a call that read and stalled ends with `no_progress`.
  - `list_changed` is true when anything changed at or above the last returned row: it moved from the offset the cursor saw it at (rows inserted above it, which the caller never gets, or returned rows removed), `readList` found it in the previous segment or started over, it is gone, or a row the cursor remembers came back, and on every page after a start over. Rows that change below it are returned without it. A position whose site `token` starts right after that row (offset 0) does not expect it.
  - A site link that carries a session, a credential or a signature is left out of the next position, which then reaches that page by its number: user info, a `;jsessionid=` path parameter, an ASP.NET cookieless `(S(...))` path segment, or a `sid`, `sessid`, `sessionid`, `session_id`, `PHPSESSID`, `CFID`, `CFTOKEN`, `access_token`, `id_token`, `auth_token`, `authenticity_token`, `csrf_token`, `token`, `api_key`, `password`, `sig`, `signature`, `X-Amz-Signature`, `X-Amz-Credential`, `X-Amz-Security-Token` or `X-Goog-Signature` parameter.
  - Every position a draft can hold fits in a cursor of at most 2,048 characters: a draft keeps the last 16 row-key digests and at most 1,360 bytes of JSON. When a list has more but no cursor, `next_cursor_unavailable` says why: `depth_cap`, `no_progress` (a later page that returned no rows and stayed at the position it started from, such as a list that still stalls, so a caller paging until `has_more` is false still ends), `position_too_long`, `unsigned_host`, `off_site`, `not_a_draft` or `inputs_not_json`.
  - `InputRefusalDetail` and `errors.InvalidInput`'s detail add `kind`, a short token such as `expired`, which a run's `input_rejected` outcome carries as `details.kind`, and `RunEvidence` adds `refusal_kind`.
  - The pagination skill is rewritten around this contract and its mechanisms: numbered pages and Next links by the site's own link, load more and infinite scroll by a capped replay, the page's own list API, offsets within a page, changed lists and when to stop. The search, core, testing and publication skills point to it, and `references/pagination.ts` is a paged search tool and a "Show more" list on the same contract (`readRooms`), both reading through `readList`; `readRooms` waits for each step's rows with `waitForRows` and keeps a list that stops growing while its control is still offered open, with `has_more` true and a position that continues.
- Guardian treats a signed-out session's own state as part of a read. Choosing a store or location, a delivery or pickup mode, a filter, sort, option, currency or language, or dismissing a dialog is never a write there, even through a Save button, and choosing among the page's own options for it is not an invented value. Saving a guest address signed out is part of the read too when it only sets the delivery or store location. A signed-out write includes a step that submits or sends something to a person or an organization, such as a contact form, an application, a message or review, a sign-up or newsletter subscription, or an order, booking or payment. Adding to a cart, a hold on inventory, a seat or a time slot, an upload and a draft stay writes. A signed-out quote or price form that only fetches is a read, but any signed-out step that enters a name, email, phone number or similar personal detail, an address dialog that also takes them included, is a write that needs the owner's confirmation, so Guardian escalates it without one. When Guardian can't establish that a session is signed out, it applies the signed-in rules. Signed-in sessions keep the rules they had. The authoring skills say the same.
- A script that refuses a caller's value can name the input and list the choices the page offers for it: `new errors.InvalidInput(message, { field, available })`. The run's `input_rejected` outcome carries them as `details.field` and `details.available`, and the local host's failure text lists them. A host that repairs may close such a refusal without a repair, since the caller has the page's choices, and never asks the caller for a replacement. Only a refusal without choices, of a free-form value, may still be checked and confirmed with the caller. The detail is optional, and a refusal without it is unchanged. `RunEvidence` gains `refusal_field` and `refusal_available`, and `pomerado/core/runtime/operation-failure` exports `boundedRefusalDetail`, which keeps at most 100 non-empty choices of at most 200 characters each.
  - `ExecutionEvidence` gains the trusted host marker `refusal`, `{ field?, available }`. In maintenance of a read, an example whose script refused the caller's value with the page's choices is a publication receipt: maintenance cannot change the caller's input, so that refusal shows the repaired tool behaves correctly. A first build, a write repair, or a refusal without choices still needs a completed example.
- `package.json` declares the host protocol, `"pomerado": { "hostProtocol": 1 }`. It goes up by one whenever a release removes a `./core/*` export hosts use or changes one incompatibly. A package without it is protocol 1. `pnpm test` first runs `tools/check-host-protocol.ts`, which fails when a `./core/*` export of the `canary` or `latest` version on npm is gone without a higher protocol.
- `getRuntimeSources` and `runtimeSourceEntry` from `pomerado/assets` give the modules `pomerado/runtime` loads, computed from its imports, for a host's Guardian source reads and minter workspace. The build writes the same set as data to `dist/runtime-sources.json`, exported as `pomerado/runtime-sources.json`, for a host that reads the package without running it.
- `pomerado/runtime` holds the HTTP version API and only ever adds exports. It adds `SiteHttp` (its service key stays `pomerado/SiteHttp`), `SiteHttpRequest`, `HttpFailure`, `defineHttpOperation`, `readText`, `readJson`, `requestPastChallenge`, `isBotChallenge`, `parseSavedHttp`, `SavedHttpFixtures`, `SavedCaptureEvidence` and `OfflineFixtureUnavailable`, with their types. Authored tools may import it by that package path; the local host resolves it to the modules it runs.
  - `makeSiteHttp` from the new `pomerado/core/runtime/site-http-host` builds `SiteHttp` over a host's `HttpTransport`. Its optional `isChallenge` is the host's test for a bot-protection challenge page, which marks a result `challenge: true`. `isBotChallenge` reads that mark, so without a test no answer is a challenge. The contract modules are `pomerado/core/runtime/site-http`, `pomerado/core/runtime/http-operation` and `pomerado/core/runtime/saved-http`.
  - `runLocalOperation` takes an optional `http` transport and runs an HTTP version over it. Without one, an HTTP version fails before it sends anything.
- `pomerado/runtime` adds HTML reading for HTTP versions, which import no other package. `parseHtml(text)` parses an answer into an inert tree, where nothing runs and nothing is fetched, with `select(css)`, `selectOne(css)` and `title()`, and each node's `text()` (entities decoded, whitespace collapsed), `attr(name)` (only attributes the element has), `html()` and its own `select` and `selectOne`. `embeddedJson(text, selector)` returns, as an `Either`, the JSON a page embeds in a `<script id>`, every `application/ld+json` or `application/json` script, an attribute such as `data-state`, or the object or array a script assigns to a global (`{ assignment: "__STATE__" }` reads `window.__STATE__ = {...}`, `self.__STATE__ = ...` or `JSON.parse("...")` there, with the string's escapes decoded, in classic and module scripts, the last assignment that parses winning), through comment and CDATA wrappers and a trailing `;`; its `EmbeddedJsonFailure` says whether the block is missing or not JSON and names the selector and the page's title. `readEmbeddedJson(http, request, selector, schema)` reads the page with `readText`, decodes the block with the schema, and when a safe read's live answer lacks the block reads once more over the page's fetch where the host can carry it, never from an offline replay, keeping the block's own failure if that read fails; it fails as `parsing`, naming the selector, the page's title, the URL, status and transport. The package now depends on `htmlparser2`, `css-select`, `domhandler` and `domutils`.
- The local host stages and lets Guardian and the minter read the modules `getRuntimeSources` lists, in place of its own list, which missed `runtime/files.js`. Its Guardian reads a bare `pomerado/runtime` as `browser/index.js`, and Guardian's execution policy names `pomerado/runtime` as trusted SDK beside `runtime/index.js`.
- The package ships the `http-mcp` authoring skill and its references `http-version.ts` and `kernel-page-fetch.ts` for a host that builds HTTP versions. The local host doesn't load it. Its section `http-mcp.challenges` holds the bot-challenge guidance, which a host that recognizes challenge pages supplies.
- The minter and Guardian share one navigation rule. URLs built from caller values are brittle, so the minter prefers the page's own controls. A URL the caller supplied, on the tool's site, may be opened unchanged, such as the build's start page or a product or listing page URL input. A stable identifier route the site itself uses may be opened from the caller's identifier when that is clearly better. Either way the page's identity is read back. Building or iterating on search, filter or sort parameters in place of the page's controls stays refused. This replaces the rule that refused any URL holding a caller value.
- `report_blocked` with reason `policy` needs a refusal on record in the attempt: a Guardian deny or escalation of an execution, a refusal of a publication that no correction gets past, or the owner's no to a `confirm` question. A publication refusal counts only when Guardian denies or escalates it with reason `privacy` or `authority`, or names a finding in the categories `private_literal`, `credential`, `customer_data`, `exfiltration`, `unsafe_logging` or `safety_default`; the outcome alone never decides it. A denial or escalation that names a correction, such as reason `source_correction`, `unsupported_claim`, `evidence` or `input_feedback`, or an `example_value` finding, is review feedback: the minter fixes it, or gathers the missing evidence the rationale names, and calls `finish_build` again, and the build's other limits stop a finding it cannot fix. `publicationRefusalUnfixable`, `unfixablePublicationReasons` and `unfixableFindingCategories` from `pomerado/core/guardian/review-contracts` hold the rule; `publicationRefusalUnfixable` reads only the review's `reason` and `findings`. Without a refusal the host refuses the block as `policy_not_refused` and the build goes on. A host whose own guidance names a `policy` ending, such as a repair's verdict on the caller's input, allows it with the new `MintDependencies.policyBlockAllowed`, which gets the screened explanation. `MintHarnessSnapshot` gains an optional `guardianRefused`, so a takeover keeps the record.
- A publication denial with reason `host_owned`, whose every finding is in a file the host wrote, ends the attempt at once as the host's own failure, `hostFailure: "publication_unavailable"`, with the denial in `publicationDenial`. It used to go back to the minter, which no source edit could help: the build ran on until its final-answer limit and ended incomplete with no host failure, or the minter's `policy` block was refused. This ending recurs on every build until the host fixes its own file, so a host should treat an outcome that carries `publicationDenial.reason: "host_owned"` as final: it should not requeue the build or offer a retry, as it may for other host failures.
- A `finish_build` that changed nothing since Guardian denied a publication is refused before review, as `unchanged_since_denial` with recovery `correct_source`, instead of being reviewed and denied again; the denial stays the build's `publicationDenial`. The harness compares the request, the digests of the entrypoint's and `src/tool-http.mjs`'s import closures and of each file the denial names, and the attempt's executions, live test results, task revision, answers and retained captures. It keeps the comparison in memory only, so a takeover drops it and reviews the next submission.
- The workspace guide and the `report_blocked` description name every finding category a `policy` block may stand on, and the guide tells a repair to keep every declared input and output rather than narrow the tool. The continuation prompt says a publication denial that names a correction or missing evidence is feedback to fix.
- The minter's guidance asks for broader tools and fuller tests. A read signed out runs as many live tests with an input the minter chose as it needs (`preflightTestInput` in `pomerado/core/mint/step-checks`), and the testing skill asks it to test every control it exposes.
  - The core skill adds "Configure, then read": discover a page's choice groups, set them from the input, confirm, then read the values that depend on them. A value the page does not offer throws `errors.InvalidInput(message, { field, available })` listing the page's choices. The runtime change that adds that second argument, and the host behavior that ends such a run at once with the choices, are in this release (see the `InvalidInput` entry above).
  - Tools expose every control that narrows, orders or configures their results, a location or store included on search, details and cart tools when the site lets a visitor set one. Setting a location, filter, sort or option in the run's own browser is part of a read; Guardian's execution policy says so in the same words, and saving to a signed-in account stays a write.
  - Output schemas lean toward more information: the facts a caller could reasonably use, with full displayed values and split values, such as a maker line above a name, in their own fields. A field the code never reads is a defect, and narrowing an output claim is no longer the default remedy.
  - A value the request needs is never loosened: it is required and non-null. Only a record whose own page shows no such value may return null, together with a field saying why. A value the page shows that the code failed to read throws instead of returning null.
  - Reads wait for the values they read, not their containers, clear overlays and expand sections before clicking, never swallow a failed wait, and check after extracting that the page did not re-render.
  - A repair fixes any declared input it finds broken, and never asks the caller for a replacement value.
- The core authoring skill asks for plain `locator.click()` calls, with at most a `timeout`. A hosted browser may make such a click a real pointer click, after the same checks Playwright makes. Options such as `force` and `position` keep a synthetic click.
- Operations take and return files. `FileInput` and `FileOutput` from `pomerado/runtime` type a file field, marked `format: "file"` in the published JSON Schema. A script's `files.place(reference, { field })` puts a caller's file into a file input on the tool's site, where `field` is data, `{ label, exact? }` or `{ selector }`, never page code, and `files.collect(trigger)` returns a download as `{ "$file": { id, name, media_type, size, sha256, download_url?, expires_at? } }`. Bytes never pass through page code, its results or JSON bodies.
  - The local host takes a `file:` URL in the input and returns a download's `file:` URL, which expires after 30 minutes. `PomeradoOptions.files` sets where downloads are kept and the caps, 25 MiB per file and 10 files or 50 MiB per run by default. Without a directory, downloads are kept in the process's temporary directory, removed when it exits.
  - A placed file is checked by its first bytes against its declared type and the input's `accept`. The host checks the element the field names and sets that same element in one page call, only when it is a file input in a frame on the tool's site. A refusal of the caller's file (`too_large`, `run_limit`, `type_mismatch`, `executable`, `not_accepted`) fails the run as invalid input naming the reason; a refusal before the page call reports nothing sent. A download's type comes from its first bytes, else the server's `Content-Type`, never its name. Programs are refused either way.
  - The minter sees a caller's file as a `{{file.fN}}` handle with its name, type and size, listed in the new `MintRequest.files`. A live step or publication whose source holds a handle is refused, publication with the new reason `file_handle`. One whose source reads a file's contents is refused too, and in a build with files also one that sets a file input itself, takes a download itself, reads an input's files, or routes the page's requests, publication with the new reason `file_readback`; `fileReadback` and `fileReadbackRefusal` are in the new `pomerado/core/mint/file-readback`. That check is static, and Guardian's execution policy gains a `{{file.<id>}}` rule that denies the same reads.
  - Setting a file input with `setInputFiles` from bytes in a tool's own input, such as base64, is deprecated in favor of `FileInput` and `files.place`. A build with no file handle and no `FileInput` may still do it, so repairs of tools built that way keep running.
  - `file_download` in `IntakeReasonCode` is deprecated, since a tool can return a downloaded file. It still decodes; a later release removes it once hosts stop emitting it.
  - Other hosts implement `FileHostHook` and call `makeRunFiles` from the new `pomerado/core/runtime/file-transfer`, bind the result as the runner's `files`, and pass the minter handles from `issueFileHandles` in the new `pomerado/core/mint/file-handles`. `pomerado/core/runtime/files` and `pomerado/core/runtime/file-types` hold the contracts and the type checks.
- A publication finding whose byte range runs past its file or is empty is fitted to the file instead of discarding the denial.
- The publication policy adds `publicationOutputPolicy`, `publicationCartPolicy` and `publicationFindingFeedback`, exported from `pomerado/core/guardian/publication` for hosts with their own policy. `publicationOutputPolicy` also refuses Playwright source that opens a page URL holding a caller input value.
- The execution policy settles the sign-in method in the order the caller's choice, a password, then a code, until the host picks it, denies opening account areas a task does not concern after sign-in, counts related results and listing pages as discovery, lets a step decline optional setup such as adding a passkey, denies any passkey or security-key step, refuses a page URL holding caller input in an example, test or act step, and no longer treats a read's filters as defaults. The question policy leaves the sign-in method to the host when it holds a login, still allows asking where a code is sent when nothing names it, and allows asking for the location a search's results depend on, which the owner may decline.
- `SpentSignIn` adds `sign_in_retry_spent`: a host refuses another sign-in once the attempt's one retry of a sign-in that submitted the login but never verified, and was not rejected, is spent. The agent and the build's owner read why. `relogin_spent` now reads as the attempt's one extra sign-in, which a host may also run on a recovery's new profile.
- The local minter's `AGENTS.md` starts with the features the shared prompts describe that another host supplies and the local host lacks, which the minter ignores. The local Guardian's execution policy ends with the same list, as protections it doesn't count on. `typescript/src/hosted-features.ts` holds the list, one line per entry, and a change that brings a feature to the local host deletes its entry. `GuardianExecutionEnvironment` gains an optional `absentProtections`, the policy's last line. A host that leaves it out gets the policy unchanged.
- Guardian's core publication policy, which the local host uses, no longer says the published files passed a deterministic credential precheck. It says the local precheck covered only the build's own caller-supplied values and secret handles, and asks Guardian to inspect every published file for hard-coded API keys, tokens and passwords. It also says this host has no data-vendor exception.
- The local minter reads the shared text where its own sections were empty or different, and the testing skill. That covers the write session's steps, declared questions, sign-in screen fields, the login URL rules, the workspace map, the tool list and how Guardian sees the build. Where that text describes a feature the local host lacks, the list on top of `AGENTS.md` tells the minter to ignore it.
- A script's `ensureSignedIn` now signs in again on a local run or build when the page reads signed out. Before, the local host always answered that it didn't sign in again.
  - The host checks the recorded signed-in marker on the current page without moving it. A marker that names a path reads signed out only on that path.
  - On a page that reads signed out, it first checks without typing anything. It then replays the recorded sign-in on the same browser and page, with the login it already holds, and asks only for what the screens need.
  - A run makes at most 3 such sign-ins. A build makes at most 4 per attempt, across a change of site too, and 1 per check. Past that, a build ends with the session not kept, and a run fails with `RunOutcomeFailure`.
  - A run whose page reads signed out and that can't sign in again, because its sign-ins are spent or the sign-in failed, reports `website_sign_in_unavailable` with the retry class `same_key`. That holds for a read, and for a write that sent nothing or entered none of its declared commit steps. Such a write's status is `not_applied`, or `not_attempted` when it sent nothing. A run whose journal shows a commit step entered, or commit marks it can't read, never reports it, even when its request says it reads. Any other write keeps the outcome its journal shows, `outcome_unknown` while it may have applied, so a retry can't repeat it. A run given no effect, as `pomerado run` is, follows the write's rule. A served run's failed job carries the same code and retry.
  - A username or password the site rejected during one of these sign-ins is never sent again. The next sign-in asks for a correction before it types, asks once more when the correction repeats the rejected value, and refuses after that. A rejected code, date of birth, ZIP or recovery code is asked for fresh, with the login kept.
  - The operation's deadline pauses meanwhile. No sign-in step starts after the sign-in's bound, and a question it asks waits no longer than that bound.
- A signed-in build checks its signed-in marker after each reset that restores its saved session. When the reset's load signed the site out, the build signs in again before the step runs, saves that session and resets once more.
  - A marker the site's root doesn't show can cost more at each such reset. A marker that names a path costs nothing there. One with a page to open costs a load of that page and one more reset. A marker with neither, on a site whose sign-in page shows its form to a signed-in browser, signs the build in twice at the first reset and once at each later one, and those steps start on the page the sign-in left. The fourth such reset then fails with the session not kept. Choose a marker the site shows on every signed-in page.
  - A site whose load signs out a fresh sign-in starts each later step on the page its sign-in left.
- The session a signed-in build saves now holds the tab's session storage too, when the whole save fits in the 1 MiB a browser call returns. Otherwise the build saves the stored state alone, as before. A reset that restores the session puts back what it saved.
- `pomerado/core/runtime/session-sign-in` holds the re-sign-in rules hosts share: the allowances, how a marker check reads the session, the bounds of a sign-in a script waits for, and the start check after a reset. `SessionSignInHook` is the host side of a script's `ensureSignedIn`.
- `pomerado/testing/session-sign-in-contract` holds cases that any host's re-sign-in can run against the controlled shop. The shop fixture's home now shows an account link to a signed-in session. It adds an `/orders` page, a `signOutOn` path whose next signed-in load signs the session out, and a count of sign-in page loads. A wrong code now shows the code form again under its error, and `codePosts` counts the codes posted.
- New exports let another host share these modules instead of keeping a copy.
  - `pomerado/core/guardian/publication` holds the publication decision decoder.
  - `pomerado/core/runtime/kernel-operation-run` holds `executeKernelOperation`, which runs a script under the execution context's deadline, capture, events and journal. A host with its own implementation of an operation passes it as the optional `first` runner. The local host never does.
  - `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-recipe` holds the `auth-fill.json` schemas, `recipeStep` and `autofillRecipe`.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/login-url` holds `oneTimeLoginUrlParameters` and `refuseCredentialParts`.
  - `canonicalSchema` and `withReferences` join `pomerado/core/registry/schema-references`, and `described` and `siteInput` join `pomerado/core/mcp/schema`.
- A version 1 or 2 recipe may name `questionSelector` on any secret field, as other hosts write it. A run reads it only on a security answer.
- Publication asks once about a login URL that is one authorization request, such as an identity provider's authorize URL or one carrying `state`, `nonce` or `SAMLRequest`. The first `finish_build` fails `login_url_one_time` with the URL's one-time parameters. Finishing again with the same URL publishes it.
- Publication refuses a name, description, `siteName` or `siteSummary` that holds a value the build was given, with `metadata_contains_credential`.
- A run of a built integration counts as possibly sent from its start, as the shared runner marks it, so one that fails before its first browser call reports `possible` where it reported `not_sent`. A mint step still counts as possibly sent from its first browser call, and a run with no browser still reports `not_sent`. The shared runner also races the execution deadline, but the local host's own deadline starts first and still ends a local run as before.
- A built integration's MCP server serves an input schema that recurses, with its references under `$defs` at the call schema's root. It used to fail to start with "Invalid operation schema.". The input is titled `<tool> input` and its description adds a summary of its top-level fields.
- Recoverable failures no longer end a build:
  - When Guardian asks for a reword of a `report_blocked` explanation, the minter gets the rationale and may revise or withdraw it. An unavailable review of the explanation is retried under the review outage budget. Only an allowed explanation, or a review that stays unavailable past that budget, ends the build blocked. `MintHarnessSnapshot` gains optional `blockedReviewUnavailable`, so a takeover during such an outage can still resubmit the explanation.
  - A diagnostic copy the host could not keep, such as an execution's diagnostics or a readable model transcript, is a recorded gap, however many there are. The raw record of each model call stays fail closed: a host's `retainRuntimeRecord` failure still stops the attempt before the next model call, with `hostFailure: "diagnostic_retention"`. A host without `retainRuntimeRecord` has no required trace. `MintHarnessSnapshot.diagnosticRetentionRetries` is deprecated and optional: the harness always writes 0, only so older workers can restore newer checkpoints, and ignores it on read.
  - A review whose evidence the host could not keep is an ordinary review outage under the review outage budget, with no `retriesRemaining`.
  - `CaptureUnavailable` from an execution returns a capture gap with a possible effect, and from publication a `not_published` with `retryable: true` until the review outage budget runs out. The harness no longer sets `hostFailure: "capture_unavailable"`; the value stays in `MintOutcome` for older hosts.
  - A publication screening or source read that names no file to fix (`evidence_screening`, `source_screening` or `schema_screening` without `publicationBlock`, or `source_read`) is a retryable publication outage. `path_screening` stays a refusal the minter fixes.
- `MintDependencies.publicationDecisions` is a new optional `PublicationDecisionLog` hook. The harness records each `finish_build` decision as a `PublicationDecision`, with its code, reason, execution, time, failed checks and `PublicationRecovery` path, and the tool result carries its `decisionId`. `reviewQuestion` receives the latest refusals as `publicationDecisions`, and Guardian reads them as `question_review.publicationDecisions` in a question or blocked-explanation review. A task update review gets them too, as `PendingTaskUpdate.publicationDecisions` and `update_review.publicationDecisions`. The local host keeps them in memory for the request.
- The `write_not_submitted` refusal says the requested write was not demonstrated, and tells the minter to continue the remaining authorized work or ask about revising inputs the site cannot take.
- `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome` holds the run outcome classifier other hosts share. It exports `classifyRun`, `runError`, `writeStatusOf`, `possibleCommit`, the commit evidence and confirmation checks, `runOutcomeCodes`, `runOutcomeRetry` and the `FailureRenderer` hook.
- `pomerado/core/runtime/failure-renderer-contract` exports `failureRendererIssues`. It checks that a host's renderer words every code with its own text and retry class, and that every possible commit, and every write that may have applied, carries the host's read-back advice.
- A write build refuses a step that repeats an earlier write's entrypoint or the same source under another name until the outcome review, caught up and with no readback outstanding, finds the write did not happen. Guardian's execution review sees earlier writes and their findings in `trusted_execution_context.writes` (hosts pass them through `OutcomeReviewHost.bindWrites` and `MintReviewHost.writes`) and denies committing the same change again. The harness checkpoint keeps tracked writes in `MintHarnessSnapshot.outcomeWrites`.
- A separate outcome reviewer judges each write after it runs, from the minter's whole history and the host's records, and records `done`, `not_done` or `unknown`. `MintOutcome.writes` reports every write with its newest assessment. See `MintDependencies.outcomeReview` and `pomerado/core/mint/outcome-review-contracts`.
- `MintTurn.history` gives the minter a history archive and takes a reader of its run state, so the outcome reviewer reads the minter's whole history, the items before a compaction included, after a takeover too. `OutcomeReviewHost.historyArchive` is the host's durable archive, and `MintAgentSnapshot.historyOffset` records where the run state starts in the whole history; a recovery factory stores the counters' `historyOffset` in it.
- A served write tool takes an optional `idempotency_key`. A call that repeats the key and input rejoins the first job and acts on nothing, in parallel, from a second server on the same folder, and after a restart. The same key with other input is refused with a retry-conflict message, and nothing runs. A call without a key is still a new website action.
  - `pomerado-mcp serve` keeps each keyed job's record in the integration folder's `.jobs` for a day, with the key, a digest of the input, the job ID, its status, and a failed run's outcome and commit marks, never the input or output. `get_job` finds a keyed job after a restart with its status and a failed run's outcome but not its output, and a keyed job a stopped server left running reads as failed and is never run again.
  - `.jobs` is reserved, so an artifact may not use the name. `IntegrationMcpOptions` gains `directory`, the folder those records live in. Only a write tool keeps records there, and `.jobs` is for servers on one machine.
- `pomerado/core/runtime/job-store` exports `submitJob`, the retry-key rule every host shares, with its `JobStore` hook and `RetryConflict`. `pomerado/core/runtime/fingerprint` exports the request `fingerprint`. `pomerado/testing/job-store-contract` exports `describeJobStoreContract`, which runs the shared contract on a host's own store.
- The authoring text and the write examples call `verified()` with no argument and declare `write: { confirmation: "readback" }`, whether the write read the site's confirmation or the saved state. The runtime still accepts `{ confirmation: "message" }` from operations published before.
- The minter reaches every page of a browser version and its probes through the site's own search, forms and links, never through a URL that holds the caller's input. The detail-read example `navigation.ts` types the identifier into the site's search, follows the one matching result link and checks the final path against that link's `href`. Only the site's own word that no record matches fails as `InvalidInput`. A search still loading, or one that failed, does not.
- When a supplied value is incompatible with what the site offers, such as an unavailable option, date or quantity, the minter asks the owner with `request_input` whether to revise it or stop. The question names the value and offers what the site has, and the minter never substitutes another value on its own. Work the request already covers proceeds without a question. Before calling a value unavailable, the minter checks where the site would show it for the requested scope; settled evidence for the requested option is enough.
- Guardian's question review allows the minter's question to replace an unavailable supplied value or stop when settled evidence shows the value unavailable, and no longer asks to reword it as redundant or as asking for more authority. A published tool still throws `InvalidInput` for a value the site refuses: a question review's input marks a question the operation's script asks with `question_review.scriptAsk`, and Guardian rewords such a question that asks to replace the value or stop. `questionForReview` takes the new `scriptAsk` host fact.
- Guardian's question review allows a blocked report that starts "Caller input error:" when it names the input value at fault and why the site cannot fulfil it.
- Guardian's question review leaves the sign-in method to the host only when the host holds a login for the site, as `credentialsAvailable` reports. Without one, it always allows a question asking which sign-in method to use, such as phone or email, when its choices match the page, and encourages one when the method is ambiguous. It still never allows one offering a passkey.
- Guardian's question review keeps a repair's caller-input question to whether the caller's value is invalid or real, and no longer rewords it to offer a replacement value, since a repair can't change the run's input. The rule for the minter's question to replace an unavailable supplied value or stop leaves that question out.
- Guardian's execution policy treats the SDK a source imports from `runtime/index.js`, such as `defineOperation`, `CalendarDate` and `formControlsCode`, as trusted host code. Guardian judges what the source does with it, and never escalates or denies only because it cannot read an SDK file through `read_source`.
- The core skill's input schema rules tell the minter never to derive a format from one sample, such as a zero-padded date key, and to read it off the page for the value it needs.
- The publication skill's checks before `finish_build` add **Typed output**, right after **Schemas**. It prefers parsing what the page shows into typed fields, one per fact a caller would filter, sort or compare on, with a worked example, over one text blob or summary.
- The core skill's output rules prefer parsing what the page shows into typed fields, as the publication skill's **Typed output** check does, so the minter reads them before it writes the schema and the parser. They also prefer keeping every result row the page shows, and make the facts every row has required output fields, typed as `Schema.Int` or `Schema.NonEmptyString`. A row whose fact the code could not read then fails as `InvalidOutput`, which a host that repairs sends to repair, rather than being dropped from a list that returns as success.
- The core skill's input schema rules prefer throwing a plain error for a listed option the code does not read results for yet, over returning results for another option. The run then fails as `OperationFailure`, which a host that repairs sends to repair.
- The core and writes skills no longer tell the minter that nothing repairs a tool whose run fails as `InvalidInput`. A host that repairs checks the value and asks the caller before the run ends that way.
- The core skill's input schema rules tell the minter to look a value up in the site's own search the way a person would, matching names loosely and counting the matches at the best rank. None throws `InvalidInput` at once naming the closest entries, one is used, and several ask the caller with a declared choice question, which the caller-input skill now lists. `formControlsCode`'s `chooseOption` also ignores apostrophe style, so `’`, `‘` and `ʼ` match `'`.
- The core skill's input schema rules prefer keying only on what the caller asked for. A detail that changes from one listing to the next, such as a seating label or a room name, shouldn't decide whether a tool books or refuses unless the caller chose it.
- The writes skill says that on a host that repairs, a failed write run whose marks show no commit step entered reports that it changed nothing, and that its repair fixes the code and runs the write once, without reading the site back.
- The writes skill tells the minter to mark only the step whose click saves or submits. Opening or filling an unsaved form is not a commit step, and the `commit_marks_undeclared` refusal says so too.
- The writes skill tells the minter that the first read-back that matches confirms a write, so it stops there instead of reading more pages. A match shows the written record after the write, found by its number, its own reference or the exact values entered, in one confirmation, list or detail view and never by position, with every field the caller asked for that the view shows in agreement. It reads another page only for a field that view did not show and the output promises, and other rows only where the before-and-after check or another skill compares them.
  - State from before the write never matches: a match needs the number or reference the write produced, or a value it changed, and a list that has not reloaded neither confirms the write nor shows that it did not happen.
  - Entered values find a record but never prove it new. A create matched only by them reads the list before the write too, and only a row that wasn't there before counts. Two rows that match afterwards, with no noted id to show which one is new, don't confirm it by values alone.
- A failed `act` step Guardian labelled a write that may have committed it carries `writeSession` with `verifyFirst: true` and a notice to read the site back before any further write. The local host counts a step as possibly sent when it made a browser call, entered a commit mark or lost its result.
- `finish_build` on a write first checks that the session may have sent its write, and a session that did not gets `write_not_submitted` before Guardian reviews the composed contract. It used to get a contract refusal first.
- A step that lost its result counts the commit marks it entered only once a later step confirms the write.
- `pomerado/core/mint/write-session` exports the write session checks. A host marks a step `possiblySent` when its own record can't rule out a send; the local host sets it from the effect journal. `LocalOperationFailure` gains `reported`, which is false when the host lost the child's result.
- A write's build keeps the confirm popups the owner accepted during its act steps and publishes them with the tool as `acceptedConfirms`, in `pomerado.json`. Each is a SHA-256 digest of the popup's normalized message, page origin and step, never the page's text. Only a confirm on an https page, raised under a step name, whose message holds no secret the caller supplied, is kept, up to 32.
- A run accepts a write's recorded confirm without asking when the same message shows on the same page origin at the same step, once per record. Every other popup still asks the caller. A read tool accepts nothing from the record.
- `finish_build` on a write refuses a composed script whose files never name a step the owner accepted a confirm popup at, with `confirm_action_unmatched` and the steps in `confirmActionIds`. A run could never match that confirm. `checkWriteSession` takes those steps and the entrypoint as an optional `confirms`.
- A run dismisses a popup whose question's window ends unanswered, and goes on. It used to fail the step. A cancel at the terminal, or a question that can't be asked or answered, still fails the step.
- The new `pomerado/core/browser/dialogs/expected` export holds the confirm digest, matcher, recording, the `confirm_action_unmatched` check and the run's popup decision. The new `pomerado/core/runtime/incidents` export holds `IncidentStore`, the hook a host uses to record the popups it settled, and the local `noIncidents`. The new `pomerado/testing/confirm-popups-contract` export holds a fixture page and a check that any host's popup handling can run. `pomerado/core/browser/dialogs/contracts` adds `DialogFacts`.
- `mint_update` gains an `output` change, `{ setting: "output", field, change, text }`, which loosens one output field of a published tool's contract: optional, nullable, removed or widened. Only maintenance accepts it, together with `requirement` and `purpose` changes. Maintenance refuses input, effect, site and login changes, and a recommended new build, before review. The new optional `taskUpdateConfirmer` hook on `MintDependencies` says whether the person answering a repair's questions owns the tool. Without it, or when it answers `none`, a maintenance update is refused as `owner_unavailable`, so the local host still refuses every maintenance update. Guardian reviews an owner's update with the host fact `maintenance: { confirmer: "owner" }`.
- `MintFailure` gains the publication reason `output_obligation_weakened` with `weakenedOutputs`, each `{ field, change }` with `change` one of `removed`, `optional`, `nullable` or `widened`. `finish_build` hands both to the minter, not as a review or an outage. After an extraction fix a read repair runs the example again and publishes that execution, and a write repair publishes the same execution without running the write again; after an applied output change either publishes the same execution again. In maintenance, `contract_output_mismatch` now tells the minter to keep a registered field and fix the extraction, or to propose the output change, not to make the field optional.
- A host that publishes repairs adopts this by implementing `taskUpdateConfirmer`, keeping applied output changes with the rest of the task state, and comparing the registered output schema with the repair's at publication. It refuses a loosening as `output_obligation_weakened`, exempting only the field and change an applied output change names, and never through a change to a field an earlier applied update added. Core ships no comparison, because the local host publishes no repairs.
- A misplaced secret handle's refusal now tells the minter to write the handle as the whole string literal in the Playwright code of a `kernel.browsers.playwright.execute` call.
- `exampleInputRefusal` takes an optional `maintenance` flag. A host sets it while it repairs a published tool, which runs its failing case's own input, so `exampleInput` is refused. The local host never sets it.
- New exports let other hosts drop their copies of these checks.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/step-checks` exports `testInputNotJson`.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/publication-review` exports `unlistedExampleInputKeys` and `exampleInputFeedback`.
  - `pomerado/core/mint/draft-questions` exports `draftQuestionDeclarations` and `draftQuestionDeclarationFailure`.
  - `pomerado/core/execution/declared-questions` exports `asksAsDeclared`.
- `pomerado/testing/shop-fixture` adds a storefront for evaluating search and cart tools. `/catalog` is a search whose size, color and fit filters sit in a panel behind an "All filters" button, offers only the choices the query's results have, shows each applied filter as a removable chip, and says so when nothing matches. `/product` asks for a product's options before adding it. The guest cart and the account cart are separate, and the account's cart starts with one line. Checkout needs the account, holds back an order under `orderMinimumMinor`, and asks for a shipping address when the account has none saved. `storeProducts` lists the catalog, and the shop's state holds `guestCart`, `accountCart`, `savedAddress` and `placedOrders`.
- The package adds a `search` skill, loaded after `core`, and a `cart` skill, loaded after `writes`. Neither has sections.
  - `search` is for a search or listing tool. It takes the site's own filters, sort and location as inputs, sets each through the site's control and reads it back, and returns an empty list for a search the site answered with no results. A filter that did not apply fails the run, and a value the page does not offer throws `InvalidInput` naming the choices it does offer.
  - `cart` is for any tool that reads, adds to, changes or checks out a cart. It signs in first, reads the cart before and after a change, states whether quantity adds or sets, and throws `InvalidInput` with the site's reason when the site refuses the order as asked, such as an order under its minimum.
  - `AGENTS.md`, the writes skill and the core skill's `core.references` text point to them. A host that composes its own `core.references` text names them there.
- The core skill states the output rules in one place. A value the request needs is required and non-null. Every output is read from the page or response on every run, never a literal or a constant. `null` or an empty list means the page lacks the value, and a value the code cannot read throws `OperationFailure`. The input schema rules add that the schema never promises an input the code accepts and then ignores.
- `AGENTS.md` asks the minter to read back every input before returning, applied filters, sort and selected options included, and to fail the run when one did not apply. It no longer offers flagging a mismatch instead. A link the site offers is opened by its exact `href`, never trimmed or rebuilt.
- The auth skill never lists or picks a passkey, even when the caller asks for one. It declines optional setup offered during sign-in, such as creating a passkey, as its own fieldless step, opens only the pages the task needs once signed in, picks a sign-in method in a fixed order, and records the site's own sign-in link by its exact `href`.
- The shared skills give one rule for when to ask the caller, the one in `AGENTS.md`, and the forms and writes skills point to it and to the core skill's input schema rules instead of restating them. Tool descriptions briefly state their design decisions, limits and interpretations.
- The minter's `execute` tool description and its credentials notice no longer mention Kernel Managed Auth, which no host offers.
- A signed-in check whose sign-in sent the login only to origins off the site and its sign-in origins still fails `credentials_not_submitted`, and still counts nothing sent there. Its result now lists, in `untrustedSignInOrigins`, the origin of each `fetch` or XHR request other than a GET whose address or body carried every value the screen filled, as an exact origin with no path. An origin it already lists also counts a later screen's values its own requests carry, as an identifier-first sign-in sends them one screen at a time. It lists an origin only while a value that origin heard doesn't count as sent yet, and when some of those received the password or a code, only those. With none to list, it fails as it did before.
- A local mint that doesn't publish lists those origins in its outcome's `untrustedSignInOrigins`, even when no check ran, and so does the output of an incomplete `mint` job. `finish_build` names them too when it refuses as `autofill_recipe_not_verified`, and so does the outcome's summary. Add one you trust to `authenticationOrigins` and mint again. The new `LocalMintOutcome` type from `pomerado` is what `mint` returns, `MintOutcome` with that field.
- The local minter asks the caller once whether to trust the origins a signed-in check found the login sent to, when there are one to three and each is an https origin off the site it hasn't asked about. The question is the host's own `trust_sign_in_origin` confirm, which Guardian doesn't review. The local minter no longer asks it, and the points below describe the canary versions that did (see Breaking changes).
  - Until the sign-in has sent a password or a code anywhere, the site included, it asks only when there's one. A check made before the password screen, when the email went to an identity service and to an analytics script, asks nothing. The check after the password screen asks as before, and once the password reached the identity service it names only that one. One origin that heard only the identifier is still asked about, as a sign-in by approval or email link sends nothing else.
  - A yes trusts them for sign-in only, counts what the sign-in sent there and checks again without typing anything. Guardian's allowed origins stay the request's.
  - A trusted origin stays in `untrustedSignInOrigins` until a sign-in verifies, since nothing saved it, and the summary then says to pass it up front.
  - A sign-in that sends the identifier to such an origin and then the password to the site in a script's request without the identifier verifies only when that origin is passed in `authenticationOrigins` up front. One whose password screen posts its own form to the site verifies after a yes.
  - A no ends sign-in in the build as `sign_in_unavailable`. `SpentSignIn` adds `sign_in_origin_untrusted` for it. An unanswered question ends the build unanswered and trusts nothing.
  - A published sign-in saves the build's https sign-in origins in `pomerado.json` as `signIn.authenticationOrigins`, the ones passed in `authenticationOrigins` first and then the trusted ones. A build with no sign-in origins saves the same bytes as before. `deployment.json` adds them to the request's `authenticationOrigins`, and a run signs in through both. `MintArtifact.signIn` gains the optional `authenticationOrigins`.
  - An earlier version ignores the origins `pomerado.json` saved. Its served calls still sign in through `deployment.json`, and its `pomerado run` of such an integration fails to sign in.
- `pomerado/testing/shop-fixture` adds `identityHost` and a sign-in at `/identity-login`. Its script sends the login to an identity service on that host, then trades the token for the shop's session. `/identity-steps` is an identifier-first sign-in on one page. Its first screen sends the identifier to that service, and its second sends the password there, or to the shop with `?password=site`. With `?analytics=on` its first screen also sends the email to an analytics service on `analyticsHost`, and the shop's state counts those posts in `analyticsPosts`. `/account/note` saves a note for the signed-in account. The shop's state counts `identityPosts` and keeps `accountNotes`.
- A Guardian review's 120-second deadline (600 for a development public read) no longer counts the time the provider spends compacting the conversation. Each model call whose response compacts it moves the deadline later by its duration. A compacting call still running at the deadline is cut off with the review.
- Guardian's publication policy gives a loosened needed value its own rule. A needed value is an output fact the request names, the record's identifier or context those depend on. Its field must be required and non-null, whatever its description says; it may be null only where that record's page does not show it, with a required field beside it saying why. Source that turns a failed read into null is the same finding.
- The policy adds a lost-information rule: a fact each record or result shows that the output drops, or a value it trims or splits and drops part of. An input may be left out only when the site has no control for it; a description naming it as left out no longer excuses it. A field the code reads from each record's own element is supported even where the example's record lacked the fact, and narrowing a claim is never the fix for an output the page shows.
- `pomerado/runtime` adds `outcomeWaitCode`, page code for a Kernel call body like `formControlsCode`. Its `waitForOutcome(outcomes, { action, timeout, unchangedMs })` waits for whichever of the page's possible answers shows after a step such as a search, a filter, a date pick or a submit, named one locator each (`{ failed, unavailable, empty, results }`), and returns that key. It only observes, apart from running `action` once.
  - The first listed outcome wins when several show, so a site's error, a refusal or a greyed-out choice listed first wins over results beside it. It answers once two looks in a row agree.
  - `action` is the step itself. What an outcome showed before it, the same element with the same text, counts only after staying so for `unchangedMs` (2 s by default), so results a filter has not yet re-rendered are not read, and a filter that leaves the same results still resolves. A new or changed element counts at once.
  - It throws an `Error` named `OutcomeWaitFailure` with `reason` and per-outcome `observations` (`OutcomeObservation`, a count of matches, of visible ones and, after an action, of changed ones), and a message naming both: `outcome_ambiguous` when the winning locator matches more than one element, which it never picks among, and `outcome_timeout` when nothing showed within `timeout`, 30 s by default.
  - The core, forms and search skills tell the minter to name every way a step's page can answer and wait with it, rather than only for the happy result. The navigation reference waits for its search's answer, its record's page and its verification page this way.
  - Other hosts get it from `pomerado/core/browser/outcome-wait`.
  - A run whose `waitForOutcome` saw no outcome in time fails as `BrowserActionTimeout`, as a native Playwright wait's timeout does, so a host keeps the browser and reports it as a browser action timeout. `outcome_ambiguous` stays the tool's own failure.
- A script's context adds `askOne(id, question)`, a thin wrapper over `ask` that asks one declared question and returns that answer itself. `ask` with a list or an object returns one answer per id, such as `{ seat: "12A" }`, so the caller-input skill now says to read `answer.<id>` and names `askOne` for one question. `pomerado/runtime` exports its type as `ScriptAskOne`.
- The core skill tells the minter to prefer waiting, after a search or a filter, for the site's own sign that this load finished, such as a spinner that showed and went, or a result count that updated, before reading results. A list that fills in as it loads, or an old list that stays up before a reload, can pass the outcome wait's unchanged check.
- A write can confirm from its own commit request. The writes, forms and core skills tell the minter to keep the click on the final commit control in its own step and, in the same call, wait for the site's response to the request that click sends, matched by its method and path and by its body where the body shows the caller's values. The write went through when that response is 2xx or 3xx, a body the call can read shows no error, and the page shows no error or validation message after it. A 200 whose body reports an error, as GraphQL can, did not go through, so the write isn't confirmed. A 202, or a 2xx whose body says the work is queued, counts too, and the output carries the site's own status word for it. The step then reads what the output promises from that response or the page the commit left, calls `verified()` and still declares `readback`, and opens no other page only to prove the write.
  - It reads the site back only when the request can't settle the write: no such request went out, it failed, got no answer or returned an error status, its body shows an error or can't be read on an endpoint that reports errors with a 200, the page shows an error, the commit went over a websocket or a GET link, or a later stage the site can still refuse sent no request of its own. A generic toast alone, or a 200 from another request, confirms nothing.
  - A failed step may confirm from the page as it stands, after checking it for an error, when `stateChangingRequests` shows its commit request went through. Showing that a write did not happen still takes a read-back.
  - The outcome reviewer counts that commit request, listed in the step's `stateChangingRequests` with a 2xx or 3xx status and no error after it, as evidence the write is `done`. A 202, or a 2xx whose body says the work is queued, counts too, and a 200 whose body reports an error, as GraphQL can, did not go through. A commit step with no commit request recorded is an example of `unknown`. Guardian's publication policy accepts a composed script that confirms this way, and makes a script that accepts any request or status, takes an unchecked 200 from an endpoint that reports errors in its body, or skips the page's error check a confirmation finding. Guardian's execution policy says that when a write's step shows its commit request went through, with no error after it, reading the page as it stands is the read-back it asks for, and another page is not needed.
  - The core skill describes each `stateChangingRequests` entry's `statuses`, its requests' final response statuses in order, and `unanswered`, the count with no answer when the step ended. A host that lists state-changing requests must record the commit request's response status there. Without it the minter reads the site back as before. The local host keeps no such list, so only the script's own check applies there.
- The core skill tells the minter to prefer waiting up to 15 s after applying a filter for the results to filter, and to return once most of the rows pass. After 15 s the tool returns what is there: it doesn't fail and drops no rows. The search skill's rule that a filter that did not apply throws `OperationFailure` stays. It checks the control's own read-back, and this line checks the rows after one.
- The core skill tells the minter to prefer typing into the search box again when its suggestions don't appear after a step that reloads the page, such as choosing a city.
- The core skill tells a tool to start an obvious refusal's message with "Caller input error:", so a host that ends such a refusal at once can give the caller the answer without a repair. A refusal is obvious when the input alone breaks a rule of the task, checked before any site action, such as a departure date before yesterday in UTC, a return before its departure or a count below one, or when the page refuses the value in its own words, which the message quotes. It is never obvious when the tool's own read found nothing, such as an empty suggestion list, a control that had not loaded or a day a write finds disabled without the page saying why. A read returns a greyed-out day as unavailable data.
  - The forms skill points to it and counts a date as past only before yesterday in UTC. The caller-input skill names a near-miss name the site offers alternatives for as ambiguous, and prefers not starting its refusal with it. A greyed-out day or option is never ambiguous: it follows the core skill's unavailable-option rule. The `InvalidInput` doc comment says which refusals a host may end at once.
  - The local host gives the start no special meaning; it is part of the failure's message.
- Guardian and the minter turn safety defaults off. A safety default is a preselected option whose only effect is sharing the caller's data with another company, such as a partner comparison that opens the search on another site, opting into tracking beyond what the site needs, or signing the caller up for marketing email.
  - Guardian's execution policy says turning one off invents no choice and needs no caller input or answer. Keeping one on is what needs a reason: Guardian denies source that submits with one on unless trusted intent, an answer or the input asks to keep it, or the site refuses to submit with it off. It never covers an option that changes what the caller gets, such as which results come back, a seat, a room or a price. The write-options rule, which settles an add-on, a pre-selected paid option or an unexpected commitment on a write's path only from the input or an answer, excepts a safety default.
  - `pomerado/core/guardian/publication` exports `publicationSafetyDefaultPolicy`, and the core publication policy includes it. Such source is part of a good tool and never an input, example-value, schema or claim finding. A current source that drops or weakens one an earlier copy set is a `safety_default` finding. A write's options must be inputs, except a safety default.
  - The core skill tells the minter to turn each one off on every run before the submit it affects, read back that it is off, leave it on and say so in the description when the site won't submit with it off, and name the ones it turns off in general words. A repair keeps each one. The minter finds them by listing every checkbox, switch and toggle near the form by role and by `input[type=checkbox]`, unlabeled ones included, and checks after a probe's first submit whether a new tab opened or the page left the site.
  - The search, writes and workspace guidance never asks about one or makes it an input. The publication skill's rule against setting a consent, marketing, tracking or privacy flag now says to turn safety defaults off and never turn one on. The forms skill prefers a cookie banner's control that declines optional cookies.
- Guardian's execution policy treats accepting the terms a site requires to finish the requested action, such as a required "I agree" box at checkout or a notice that signing in accepts the site's terms, as part of that action, with no other authority. Terms the action doesn't require, such as a partner's terms or a marketing consent, are not. Terms on a sign-in screen no longer need authority in trusted intent; lasting account preferences still need it or the owner's confirmation (see Fixes).
- The forms skill tells the minter to prefer matching an existing record by a fuzzy match on its name, as the core skill says, and to prefer never picking an "Add new" row when an existing record matches, so a picker doesn't create a near-duplicate of a record whose name differs from the caller's only in case or in a usual form of the same name.
- Guardian's publication policy asks the review to list every finding it sees in the bundle, not only the first, so the minter can fix them together. A publication review now has 300 seconds, up from 120 (earlier 0.4.0 canaries gave it 240); other reviews keep 120, and a development public read keeps 600.
- `SiteHttp` names the rule a request broke when it refuses it before sending. The failure's new optional `refusal` holds `rule`, one of `HttpRequestRule` (`request_invalid`, `url_not_absolute`, `url_has_credentials`, `url_has_fragment`, `method_unsupported`, `body_on_get_or_head`, `header_name_invalid`, `header_value_not_text`, `header_value_newline`, `timeout_invalid`, `max_response_bytes_out_of_range` or `capability_unsupported`), and the header or the capability and transport it was about. Its message says "refused by the request check, nothing was sent", the rule and how to fix it, and leaves the URL's user:password, query and fragment, and any header name or capability that is not a plain identifier, out of the message. `pomerado/runtime` exports the `HttpRequestRule` and `HttpRequestRefusal` types. The codes stay `invalid_request` and `unsupported_capability`, with dispatch `not_sent`, except that an unknown capability string now fails `unsupported_capability` where it failed `invalid_request`.
- `http.request_started` names `page-fetch`, with reason `page_environment_required`, for a request that requires `page-environment` or `service-worker` on a live transport. It named the host transport, such as `kernel-curl`, for every request.
- The http-mcp skill opens with the request rules, and no longer tells the minter to raise `maxResponseBytes` for a large body or to declare `buffered-response-v1` with it.
- `finish_build` takes an optional `httpVersion`, `{ outcome: "ruled_out", signal, requestId?, note }`, for a read published without an HTTP version: the signal is one of `HttpVersionSignal` (`per_request_hash_or_page_id`, `bot_wall_on_page_fetch`, `unobtainable_session_token`, `streaming_response`, `location_not_applicable` or `site_refused_on_both_transports`), `requestId` the captured request it rests on and `note` what showed it. The harness screens the note like `coverage` and passes the decision to the host's `publish` as `PublicationRequest.httpVersion`; the host decides publication from its own records. `HttpVersionDecision` and `HttpVersionSignal` are exported from `pomerado/core/mint/contracts`. The harness refuses it on a write build as `http_version_on_write`, before publication. The `http_implementation_untested` instruction asks for this field instead of a deleted file, has no limit on HTTP tests, and says a request refused before sending never counts.
- `preflightTestInput` treats a live test of a `*-http.mjs` entrypoint like one of the Playwright version: an HTTP version is fixed and tested again until it matches. The local host refuses a Kernel script in a `*-http.mjs` file before it runs, as hosted hosts do.
- The http-mcp skill is rewritten for reads.
- The HTTP version reference shows a site's refusal of a caller's value failing as `operationErrors.InvalidInput` with the input field and the site's choices.
- Guardian's execution policy counts a read's HTTP version that sends the page's own signed-out search-state save, a PUT or POST included, before its query as part of the read.
- The writes skill and the workspace guide say a refused publication keeps live execution on the same browser, still signed in, so the minter reads `page.url()` first and prefers not to sign in again. Only a publication whose capture failed leaves a fresh browser on a new, empty profile. The `http-mcp` skill says a refused publication does not skip the HTTP-version ask, and the ask's own message no longer says it comes only while live capture is open.
- The core skill and Guardian's execution and publication policies say a tool matches the caller's proper nouns, such as places, venues and company names, with a fuzzy match that accepts the usual forms of the same name, never an exact one, and lists the options for the caller instead of picking one when more than one matches. Another usual form of the same proper noun is not a near match, the workspace guide counts matching one as work the request already covers, and a read-back compares a proper noun with the option it matched.
- The search skill says that when the page's list of choices does not hold a caller's value but a broader choice such as "Show all" covers it, the tool chooses the broader choice and returns the rows the site gives, saying so in the description; otherwise it refuses with `InvalidInput`. The core skill allows that broader choice on a search, and the workspace guide's read-back rule returns its rows with what applied. A filter the site has a control for is set through that control, never by filtering the returned rows, and Guardian's "Applied without readback" publication finding covers only inputs the site has a control for.
- Guardian's execution policy counts searching with the site's broader setting or its default as a routine implementation choice when the site's form has no setting for a request constraint, such as a cabin class with only "Show all".
- The auth skill says that on a one-time-code screen the minter prefers one read that finds the code fields, the submit and any remember-this-device box, then sends the code step at once, before any other probing, since codes expire in minutes.
