- The core skill tells the minter never to search the open web for the page a task needs, but to make its URL an input and ask the caller for it.
- The writes skill tells the minter to prefer marking the commit at the step that submits the booking or order. A hold that expires on its own, such as a reserved table or a cart timer, is not the commit, and its step is not marked.
- Guardian's publication policy says a repair's review prefers denying only for lines the repair changed, or for unchanged lines that a failing run or example shows broken, and notes other doubts about unchanged lines without denying, except a privacy or credential finding. It is part of `publicationLiveTestsPolicy`, so a host with its own publication policy that includes it gets the rule too.
- Guardian's task update policy says the host, not the agent, sets `update_review.effect`: for a write build no change needs to make it a write, and the confirmation need not cover the write. It also says a task update review's empty `trusted_authority.allowedEffects`, like the read-only `allowedEffects` of earlier exploration reviews, says nothing about the build's effect.
- Guardian's execution policy, beside its sign-in screen rules, and `publicationSafetyDefaultPolicy` say to prefer leaving a site's own "Remember me" or "Keep me signed in" choice at its default on the tool's own sign-in: it keeps the account owner's session, which later runs rely on, it is not a safety default, and neither review denies the host's own sign-in step over it.
- Guardian's task update policy, its execution policy, the `mint_update` tool description and the workspace guide say the caller's confirmed answer settles a Guardian decision that held a step for their input, authority or confirmation, a scope decision included, but never one made because a step weakens security or safety (see Fixes).
- The caller-input skill says `ask` returns one answer per question id only with an array or an object of questions. `ask("id")` with one id returns that answer itself, as the line before it says.
- The writes and forms skills tell the minter to prefer confirming a write from what the page shows after the commit click, such as an order or reference number, or a success message or toast that appears after the click with no error on the page, and to open the saved state, such as an orders page or a booking list, only when the page shows no confirmation. A commit step that returned without calling `verified()` while its `stateChangingRequests` list the commit request with a 2xx or 3xx status prefers confirming it in the next step from the page as it stands. A composed script prefers confirming from that commit request in the commit call over opening the page the session confirmed on. The local host's standalone write completion text says to read back committed state only when neither the commit request's response nor the page confirms the write, and its Guardian publication note says `unverifiable` is valid only when the site offered no commit response the script can check, no confirmation and no read-back.
- The `write-readback.ts` reference confirms its write from the create request's 201 and its Location, calls `verified()` at once, and returns only the new task's id, taken from the Location. It no longer opens or reads the page that follows, so a slow, missing or stale detail page no longer fails a write the 201 confirmed. The Location may be a path or an absolute URL on the response's own origin, and its path must be `/tasks/` followed by one id. Any other Location, such as one on another origin or with a trailing slash, fails as `not_saved`. The output schema of the `pomerado/authoring/write-readback` export is now `id` alone, without `title` and `assignee`.
- A 201 whose Location names the new record confirms a write as soon as it arrives. The writes skill says such a write went through with no wait for the page, the forms skill says it needs no page error check before `verified()`, and the publication review's write rule says it needs no page check, so the `write-readback.ts` reference confirms without one. A recorded publication review keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- The workspace guide says a write build reads back whether its earlier commit took effect, after a refused publication, only when that commit recorded no confirmation. A commit that recorded its confirmation stays done.
- `chooseOption` from `formControlsCode` matches an option's label or value ignoring case, spacing and accents, and apostrophes and the punctuation `.`, `,`, hyphens and dashes except next to a digit, so "Abaca" chooses "Abacá", "St Louis" chooses "St. Louis" and "Saint Denis" chooses "Saint-Denis". Its last rank now picks a label that holds the wanted name as whole words, so "Acme insurance" chooses "Acme insurance Customer". Two options that then match at the same rank, such as "Abaca" and "Abacá", or two labels that both hold the name, are refused as `option_ambiguous`. Other symbols, such as `+`, `#` and `&`, still count, so "C++" never chooses "C", and a number keeps its sign and separators, so "-5" never chooses "5", "5'10" never chooses "510" and "1.5" never chooses "1-5". A value of punctuation alone, such as "-", is compared as it is, so it never chooses an option with an empty label.
- The core, writes and forms skills and the workspace guide say name matching and read-back comparisons ignore accents too: typeahead lookups ignore accents and spacing, the usual forms of a proper noun include it with or without its accents, the Confirm step and the workspace read-back rule ignore accents and punctuation such as periods and hyphens, the pre-commit check on a write compares values the same way, and the forms skill says what `chooseOption` folds.
- `pomerado/runtime` exports `sameText(shown, wanted)`, which says whether the text a page shows names what the wanted text names, as a person reads them. It ignores case, accents, spacing, apostrophes, and periods, commas and hyphens between letters. A word the shown text writes short, such as St for Saint, matches the full word in the wanted text when it starts with the same letter, keeps its letters in order and is at most half as long, never the other way round. This holds only for words of Latin letters, never inside an email, domain or file name. Words with digits or other symbols must be equal, and both texts need the same number of words in the same order. The core and writes skills tell tools to confirm a read-back with it, against the option the tool picked when it picked one, and the writes skill prefers running that check before the step's commit mark. The core skill says not to choose from a list with it, since a short label can match a longer name it isn't, such as UK for Ukraine.
- `sameText` from `pomerado/runtime` reads a space, hyphen, dash, slash or parentheses between digits as one separator, whichever it is, so 29 59, 29-59 and 29/59 match, and so do (317) 555-1234 and 317-555-1234. The groups it separates still count, so 1/12/2025 never matches 11/2/2025, 1 234 never matches 12 34 and 2959 never matches 29 59, and so does a period or comma between digits, so 10.0 never matches 100. Words compare as before. Its new option, `sameText(shown, typed, { rewrite: true })`, says whether a site's own version of a value the tool typed, such as an address the site standardized as it was entered, kept everything the caller gave. It only tells a reformatted value from a changed one and never decides whether a write goes on. Each word and number of the typed value must appear in the site's version, in order. A word may be written short or in full either way, with the same first letter, its letters in order and a short form of two letters or more, at most three fifths as long as the full word, and of two letters when it is the full word's start, so Main never matches Martin, Mainland or M, Larkspur never matches Lark and Oakwood never matches Oak. A number keeps its groups, though the site may pad a group with zeros, as 01/12 for 1/12, add thousands commas to a bare number, as 1,420 for 1420, or split a phone number with periods, as 555.123.4567. A bare number of seven digits or more may come back grouped, as a phone number does, but a shorter one never does, so 1420 is changed as 14-20. What the site adds is a change, a word holding a digit, a unit or a place alike, except a word of one or two Latin letters between two of the caller's, as N in 100 N Main St, a country code before a number, as +1, and one more group after a postal code of five digits or more that ends the typed value, as 46204-1234. So 12 Elm Parkway is kept as 12 ELM PKWY, Suite 4 as STE 4, 46204 as 46204-1234 and 5551234567 as (555) 123-4567, while 12 Elm Pkwy is changed as 13 Elm Pkwy, 12 as 123, 29-59 as 2959, Apt 4 as Apt 4B and 1420 Larkspur Rd as 1420-2 Larkspur Rd or 1420 Larkspur Rd Apt 2. An email, domain or file name compares as it does without the option.
  - The writes skill reads each typed value back once its field settles, before the step's commit. When the site rewrote it as it was typed or on leaving the field, or offered exactly one suggestion for a free-text value the caller typed, the write takes the site's version as it would a picked option: later read-backs compare against it, and the output returns it. A picker that selects a record, such as a person, customer, store or account, keeps its rules. It reports the rewrite in an optional `<input>_note` output field with what the caller sent and what the site kept, and the rewrite check only decides whether the note says the site reformatted the value or changed it. The write goes on either way and never throws `InputNotApplied` for such a rewrite. A value cut short by the field's length limit, digits an input mask dropped, or a field that cleared is a failed fill, not a rewrite. A value the site replaces only when a step saves follows the read-back before committing, as before. The core skill's Confirm step and picker guidance, the forms skill's picker guidance, and the caller-input skill's list of what is not `InputNotApplied` say the same.
  - Guardian's publication policy, under "Unapplied supplied input", and the publication skill say that a write's `<input>_note` reporting a value the site rewrote as it was typed, on leaving the field, or through its one suggestion is not unapplied input, since the tool read the site's version back and returns it. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A choice question's own text that repeats an offered option now picks it apart from accents and inner spacing as well as case and surrounding spacing, so a typed "Abaca" picks the offered "Abacá" instead of counting as the owner's own answer. A bare option id still never counts.
- The output check flags the same item twice in one list and fields no tested run filled, both advisory. A record ID, SKU or link field (such as `id`, `sku`, `url` or `product_id`) that repeats within one list of two or more records is reported as `duplicate_entries`, such as a sponsored copy of an organic result; no new check name is added, so every release decodes the findings a live test record keeps. `lintOutput`'s new option `alsoTested` takes the `fieldFill` of the tool's other tested runs on the same source, paths and counts with no value (`fieldFill` from `pomerado/core/runtime/output-lint`): a field empty in every record of the run and of those runs, at least one of which holds it, is flagged `empty_field` outside a list even in one record, as a detail field read from a collapsed section or a guessed key, and in a list once the runs hold three of its records. With no other tested run, only the per-list check runs, so a single build or hosted run is never flagged for a null detail field. The list paging fields are never flagged empty.
  - The harness passes each live test case the other cases' fills on the same source, and gives `publish` every case's fill as the new optional `hostEvidence.testedFields`, which no checkpoint holds. `outputNotes` takes it as `alsoTested`; the local host passes it, so `publication/output-notes.json` flags an example's field no tested run filled.
- The pagination, search and core skills return each item once. A row's key is the item's own stable ID, never its link text or position, and the skills no longer give a sponsored copy its own key so both stay. When the site lists the same item twice, such as a sponsored copy of an organic result, the tool returns the organic row at its organic position and drops the copy. A promoted row whose item the page lists only as promoted stays in place with `sponsored: true`, and a promoted carousel or banner with its own heading is not a result. The search skill asks the minter to list a typical query's item IDs before the first example and check that none repeats, to read the applied query from the page's embedded state, to fetch a per-result extra in one batched request, and to test a broad query and a fallback or "no exact matches" query whose expectation it writes after seeing the site's answer.
  - `ListReader` and `selectRows` take an optional `promoted`, whether the site marks a row sponsored. `readList` and `selectRows` then drop a promoted copy of an item the rows read show unpromoted, or an earlier page returned, without setting `list_changed`, and remember a returned promoted row apart from the item's organic row, so the organic row a later page shows is returned rather than counted as a change: the one repeat left is a sponsored copy returned before its organic row was read. A promoted row that ended the previous page anchors the next as any row does, and `finishList` takes `resumeAfterPromoted` for a call that returned no rows and resumes after one. Without `promoted` they behave as before. The pagination reference example keys rows by ID and passes `promoted`.
- The core skill says descriptions, specs, ingredients and variants often sit in collapsed sections that read empty while hidden: prefer the page's embedded record data, or open the section and read it back, and never guess a data key with a null fallback. Before the first example, the minter sees each nullable field filled on a record that should have it, bases an absence reason on evidence that the record lacks the field, and names in `finish_build` coverage a tested record where each nullable field was filled. A tool sets up its own start state from a cold page, such as a guest session, a chosen location or a consent banner.
- The testing skill plans the cases builds most often add late before the first batch (no results, a broad query, page two, and one case per selectable variant such as a second store), runs no batch before the example passes, observes a no-results query before writing its expectation, and after an edit reruns only the cases the edit can affect.
- `waitForOutcome` from `waitCode` and `outcomeWaitCode` no longer counts an answer that was on the page before its `action` while a request the action started is still in flight, as well as for the first `unchangedMs`. A filter whose answer came after the 2 s window used to return the list from before it; the wait now returns the redrawn list. A step that starts no request, or whose request ends within the window, ends as before. A request the action started holds the earlier answer back for at most `lateFillCap` (15 s), and never past `timeout` less `unchangedMs`, so a request that never ends, such as a long-poll or a stream, delays an answer the wait used to return but never turns it into `outcome_timeout`.
- The core skill asks for `waitForOutcome` after a step that redraws part of the page, such as a pick from a list or adding a table row, as well as after one whose answer can vary, naming the element the next step uses beside the step's other results. It prefers no fixed pauses unless necessary, and asks the minter to check that the tool handles a lookup or filter that matches nothing. The writes skill says the composed script runs a session's steps back to back, without the pauses taken between them, so a step that redraws the page, such as a filter, a pick or a new table row, should end with `waitForOutcome` naming the element the next step uses.
- `waitForOutcome` from `waitCode` and `outcomeWaitCode` answers at the first look that shows an outcome, where it used to wait for a second look, taken at once, to agree. A page already showing its answer now costs one look instead of two, and its `waitReport()` summary says "1 look". An answer that was on the page before the `action` is still held back for `unchangedMs` and while a request the action started is in flight, a quiet page still needs two looks to fail as `outcome_unknown`, and a winning outcome that matches more than one element now fails as `outcome_ambiguous` at the look that sees it. A look that began while such a request was in flight holds the earlier answer back even when the request ends during that look, so a slow answer that redraws the list just after it ends returns the redrawn list. A look that answers takes no progress sample, so the summary's progress may end one sample earlier. Each look reads the outcomes in reverse of their listed order, so the highest-priority outcome is read last: an error the page draws in the same render as partial results wins over them, even when that render lands between two reads of the look.
- The core skill prefers no result wait after a step that changed nothing, such as closing a panel without picking anything, and no extra wait for the same answer after `waitForOutcome`, which already waited for it.
- The core, caller-input and forms skills count a date as past when it is before today in the site's time zone, in place of before yesterday in UTC. A site with places in several time zones uses the place's zone, such as a restaurant's or a departure airport's, when the tool knows it before any site action, and when it doesn't know the zone, it refuses only a date that is past in every time zone, such as one before yesterday in UTC. The core skill notes that UTC's date can be a day ahead or behind. A tool that compared with today's date in UTC refused a date that was still today where the site is, for several hours each evening in the Americas.
  - Guardian's `publicationOutputPolicy` adds the "Past date in UTC" rule, a source-correction finding: source that refuses a date as past by comparing it with today's date in UTC, rather than today in the site's time zone or the place's, unless the site's own dates are in UTC. Refusing only a date before yesterday in UTC, which is past in every time zone, is not this finding. The publication skill's edge-case checks name it. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- The testing skill gives every free-text input of a read signed out three cases of its own in a build: a blank value, an over-long value and a made-up value the site has no match for. A repair adds only the made-up value, so it never tightens a published input's bounds to pass the others. A blank value expects `invalid_input` when the input is required, and an optional input may treat it as left out; the over-long value expects `invalid_input` from the input schema; the made-up value expects `invalid_input` naming the field, `empty` for a search query, or a labeled `result` for a location the site won't apply, and none may end in another throw. An input that takes a person's, account's or record's name, number or code skips the made-up value, and the other cases still use real values the site offers. A blank or over-long value the input schema refuses may expect `invalid_input` beside the inputs the site has no results for, does not offer or cannot apply.
- The core skill's refusal recipes name the input: `new errors.InvalidInput(message, { field })` for a value the site refuses, and `field` beside the page's own words for a not-found record and in the waits paragraph. The `navigation.ts` reference passes `field` on each refusal.
- Guardian's execution policy and action label treat a filter or sort in a signed-in session as part of a read when the site does not save it, or when the step reverts it to its prior value before the step ends. One the site saves as part of a saved view or setting, such as a table app that saves view filters with the view, and that the step leaves in place, stays a write. Guardian tells them apart from whether the source restores the prior value and whether the page's own requests for the change send a save, and a denial for an unauthorized saved filter names both fixes: revert it before the step ends, or read the rows through the page's own query request. The workspace guide tells a signed-in read to prefer the page's own row query from the captures over a filter control the site saves, and to revert the filter or sort in the same step when it must use the control. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A Guardian denial that finds a capability incomplete names the implementable change first when the captures or the site's own requests show a reasonable way to complete it, such as resolving a name the caller gives to the identifier the site stores, or returning names where the source returns identifiers. It offers narrowing the tool only when the site does not support the capability or completing it would need substantial new live work the session cannot verify. The rule is part of the outcome policy every execution and publication review reads. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A list tool whose site rarely shows an empty state can publish without a no-results case. Guardian's `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` no longer counts a list's untested empty result as a gap; a list's next page still is one. The testing skill prefers a no-results case when the build finds one, and without one it needs no `notTested` entry and no question. The publication skill no longer names a query with no results as a typical untested item. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- The minter reads the publication review's rules for live tests and output before it publishes. `loadWorkspaceGuide` installs `reference/publication-review.md`, built from Guardian's own `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` and `publicationOutputPolicy`, so a rule change reaches the minter in the same release, and the workspace `AGENTS.md` tells the minter to read it while it explores and gather the evidence it asks for before its first `finish_build`, such as a list tool's second representative record. A build used to learn these requirements only from a publication denial, which cost a review round and the code and test rounds after it. A host that lists the workspace guide's files exactly sees one more.
- A build answer about how the tool behaves, such as a sort order, which kind of record a write creates or whether a save runs the site's automation, is now the tool's default in the authoring guidance and Guardian's publication policy: an optional input whose schema default is the answered value when a caller could want another value, fixed only where varying it is unsafe or meaningless, never a required input or a question at run time. A choice that changes what the caller buys or pays, or whether the action can be cancelled or refunded, such as a paid add-on, a rate or plan tier, shipping speed, the payment method or the delivery address, stays a required input or a declared question, and may default only to declining, off or the option that adds no cost; a default that accepts or chooses one is an `input_option` finding. The input schema review, the Unapplied input rule, the live-tests policy, the authoring skills and the minter's input feedback define `input_option` the same way, by that effect, through the shared `publicationCostlyChoices` list, through `publicationCostlyChoice` and `publicationCostlyChoiceDefault` (what such a choice's default or a caller's decision about it may be: declining it, turning it off or the no-cost option), which every rule and the input feedback are built from, and so does `publicationPaidChoiceLimit`, the limit on a caller-confirmed decision, which now covers a rate, fare or plan tier, a refundable option, shipping speed and how much of a balance to pay as well: a write's other options are inputs or settled defaults, never `input_option` findings, and its schema review no longer asks that every option the site requires be a required input. The runtime adds `defaultedInput(schema, value)`, an optional input field whose run gets `value` when the caller leaves it out and whose published schema shows it as `default`, which `Schema.optionalWith`'s own default does not. `publicationOutputPolicy` adds `publicationSettledChoicePolicy`: a required input, a run-time question or a missing or different schema default for a settled choice is a `schema_mismatch` finding. The authoring guidance asks a write to put its questions before its first save wherever the page lets it, so an unanswered question never leaves a partial save; nothing refuses a later question. `draftQuestionDeclarationFailure` now names a draft question declaration that does not decode before a step runs, where the run used to declare none of the draft's questions. Item 8 of the workspace guidance no longer offers to leave out a field, input or output the request names.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Publication checks the names a build's request declares in structure: a top-level key of the caller's own input that the input schema doesn't list comes back as `example_input` input feedback, and an output field a task update declared that the output schema lacks as a `schema_mismatch` source correction. `PublicationCandidate` adds the optional `outputSchema` and `requested`. `missingRequestedNames`, `requestedOutputFields` and `requestedNamesFeedback` give a host that runs its own review the same check; `exampleInputFeedback` is unchanged.
- The output check adds two advisory checks, `format_mismatch` (a `format: "date"` field not `YYYY-MM-DD`, or a `format: "date-time"` field not ISO 8601 with an offset) and `case_variant` (one value in two letter cases across a list). The authoring skills now share one output notation: the request's stated notation wins; otherwise enum values, statuses included, are lowercase snake_case with the site's wording in a separate `*_label` field, and an amount that can go either way is unsigned with a direction field.
- The page's controls read after a sign-in step's clicked submit add the page's visible alert and error text, as `alerts` in `PageControls` from `pomerado/core/destinations/page-controls`, at most `pageAlertsLimit` (10): an alert or live region, the error message a field names as its own, or an element whose class or id says error, alert or invalid, never one that holds a field, each element's rendered text once, outermost first in page order. Only text a person sees counts: never an element a pixel wide or tall, as a screen reader's text is, a transparent or hidden one, one wholly off the page, or one styled as a success or a notice, such as `alert-success` or `toast-info`. A site's message that refuses a submit may be gone by the time the agent looks. The local host saves them in `captures/after-submit/<step>.json`, screened with the control names before `presentControls` cuts each to 200 characters, and only when the page showed one. A host that passes the new optional `countNewAlerts` to `fillAutofillStep` has the fill read the alerts just before its click too, waiting at most 500 ms, and its report's controls add the host-only `newAlerts`: how many of `alerts` the page did not show then. A read that runs out counts nothing, and a fill that does not ask, as a run's replay and the local host's, never reads before its click. `presentControls` never passes the count on. A reader that predates `alerts` or `newAlerts` drops them.
- A sign-in step reads the state of its fields and its submit in one pass in each frame, so a form that locks or unlocks them while the host reads it is no longer refused as a form that keeps changing, and each step call makes fewer browser calls.
  - `isolatedLocatorPage` from `pomerado/testing/isolated-locator-page` now accepts element handles in an isolated locator evaluation's argument, as Patchright does.
- `RunEvidence` from `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome` adds the optional `unanswered_question`: the kind of question a run's caller left unanswered, as the host names it, such as `sign_in`, `login` or `other`. `runError` returns it as `question` in a `no_response` failure's details, after `possible_commit`, so a host can tell its caller which question went unanswered and how to avoid it. A host that records none gets the same failure as before.

### Fixes

- `sameText`, `rankByWords` and `readBackMatches` from `pomerado/runtime` read one period or comma between a number's digits as its decimal mark, whichever it is, and drop the zeros after it, so a site's 1,5, 28,0 or 8,20 confirms a caller's 1.5, 28 or 8.2, and 10.0 confirms 10. Before, a write that saved a measurement as asked could refuse its own save because the site showed it with a decimal comma or an extra zero, and a refusal after the commit left the run's outcome unknown. Values still compare exactly, as strings and never as floats: 1,5 never matches 15, 28,1 never matches 28, 1,25 never matches 1.2 and 10.0 never matches 100. Three digits after a lone mark may be thousands and keep their reading, so `rankByWords` reads 1,250 as 1250, `sameText` keeps 1,250 and 1.250 as written, and neither ever matches 1.25. A number right after a letter is a code and keeps its reading too, so v2.10 never matches v2.1, and Indian grouping such as ₹1,25,000 never reads as 1.25. Digit groups split by a space, hyphen, dash, slash or parentheses read as before, so 29 59, 29-59 and 29/59 match each other, 2959 never matches 29 59 and 1/12 never matches 11/2. A number with both marks, such as 1.234,5, reads as before.
  - `saveAndReadBack` and `sameText(shown, typed, { rewrite: true })` read numbers the same way. `rankByWords` reads 2.50 as 2.5, where it dropped only a trailing .0 or .00 before. A list of digits joined by a bare comma, such as 1,2, now reads as one decimal, so `readBackMatches` no longer confirms 1, 2 or 1 2 for it.
  - The writes skill prefers `readBackMatches` for a number the caller gave, such as a measurement, an amount or a quantity, in the read-back after the commit, and the core skill's Confirm step prefers it for a number the caller gave and keeps preferring comparing dates as values. No exported type changes, and a host needs no change. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact core or writes skill text needs recording again.
- The writes skill says that after a commit the site may open a dialog or cover the page, such as an added-to-cart panel, and prefers confirming the commit from its own response, and closing such a dialog with its own control, as the core skill says of dialogs, or going to the address in the site's own link, before clicking anything behind it. Before, a tool that checked a cart by clicking the header's cart link behind such a panel timed out after its add went through, and the run's outcome was unknown. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact writes skill text needs recording again.
- The core skill's cookie banner section says that a notice the site shows every visitor before it lets them use the page, such as a demo or test-environment warning, an age gate or a region notice, is page setup on a read. It prefers accepting it with its own control the way the tool dismisses a cookie banner, waiting until the page beneath it is usable, with no commit mark and without making the tool a write for it, even when accepting it sends a request, and prefers its close control when it has one. When the request is about that notice itself, accepting it is the write. The cookie banner rule's exclusion of terms the site asks you to accept names this notice as the exception. Before, a lookup that put such a notice behind a commit mark became a write, a slow answer to the notice ended its run with the outcome unknown before the lookup started, and its repair could not run past the notice to test the lookup. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact core skill text needs recording again.
- A write whose quote expired before its commit, such as a fare, rate or cart price read minutes earlier, now refreshes it and commits once more, and a commit the site refused with nothing created ends not applied. Before, a quote read before a slow execution review could expire by the time its step ran: the site refused the commit and created nothing, but a script that did not wait for that refusal timed out with its mark sent, the outcome reviewer could not tell, and the build ended with the write possibly applied, blamed on the caller's input.
  - The writes skill says a commit step prefers reloading or re-selecting a quote right before its mark, checks the fresh one against the caller's limits there, and waits for the site's refusal in the commit call. When the site's own answer proves the click created nothing, by a response, read whole, that names the site's reason for refusing, such as an expired quote, a changed price or sold out, and returns no record, the script calls `notCommitted` for the step and ends with the site's words, preferably as a declared refusal, so the run reports the write not applied. For an expired quote or a changed price, it refreshes, checks again, enters the step again and clicks once more, at most once. The Kernel script context's `notCommitted` documents the case.
  - Guardian's execution policy says that second click is no second submission, and its publication policy says such a `notCommitted`, followed by a refusal with the site's words or by one checked retry, and a check or refusal after it before the step's next `enteringCommit`, are no finding.
  - The outcome reviewer's `not_done` includes such a commit response, in the step's result or a capture. A 5xx, a timeout, a conflict or duplicate answer, or an error inside a returned record is not that proof, there or in the skill.
  - `report_blocked` with reason `caller_input`, in its tool description and the workspace guide, does not fit a stop the site caused on its own, such as an expired quote, a block, an outage or a site error, even when the request says to stop on it: prefer `site_declined` or `site_unavailable`. A caller's limit the site's answer shows met still is `caller_input`.
  - No exported type changes, and a host needs no change. A host with its own copy of Guardian's publication policy adds the two sentences. A recorded Guardian, outcome reviewer or minter response keyed on the exact policy, instruction, skill or tool text needs recording again.
- A later list page whose site shows no results and a total of 0, after earlier pages returned rows, no longer ends the list. Such a page is far likelier a search that did not load or apply than a list that emptied, but `readList` returned `has_more: false` and a null `next_cursor`, so a tool that calls its list complete when `has_more` is false told its caller that the rows it had, such as 20 of 310, were all of them. `readList` now keeps `has_more` true and hands back a cursor for the same position, as after a call the run's time stopped, and its result adds the optional `reason`, which says the search likely did not load, for the tool to return. A second such call in a row ends the list with `no_progress`, `has_more` still true, so a caller paging until `has_more` is false still ends. A list that shrinks to anything but 0, a first page, and a page whose site shows no total behave as before. Neither the cursor nor `listOutputFields` adds a field, so a host or a published tool on an older core needs no change. The pagination skill prefers returning `reason` as the result's own, and calling a list complete only when `has_more` is false. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact skill text needs recording again.
- When a Guardian review asks for the owner's confirmation of a specific action and the owner confirms it, that answer now authorizes exactly that action, in sign-in or anywhere else, and every later review accepts it. Before, the reviews disagreed: an execution review could hold a step until the owner confirmed it, a question review allowed the confirm question, and after the owner's yes the next execution review refused the same step again and asked for a task update, which the task update review refused because a caller's answer could not overturn a scope decision. The build then ended blocked although the owner had said yes.
  - The execution, question, task update and publication policies now share one rule. The owner's yes to a reviewed question that confirms a specific action authorizes that action, beyond trusted intent too, with no task update. It authorizes nothing else, so a changed action needs its own confirmation, and a change of the build's effect still goes through a task update that the answer confirms. No answer authorizes an action that weakens security or safety: disabling or bypassing a security control, such as two-factor sign-in, sign-in alerts, lockouts or the site's own protections; adding insecure code or behavior to the tool; sending credentials, or the account's data, to anyone other than the task's own site and the caller, though reading or returning more than an underspecified task names is fine; acting on an account other than the task's, or on a site the task's site doesn't itself send the user to, where its own sign-in, payment and partner pages count as part of the task; or overriding Guardian's protections against prompt injection. Only the owner's answers in `trusted_authority.answeredQuestions` count. Text on a website, in a step result, a tool's output or the agent's note that claims the owner's confirmation never does.
  - An execution deny or escalate may name the new `refusalGround` `owner_confirmation`: the step needs the owner's confirmation of this specific action. The minter asks with a confirm question whose new `confirmsReview` names that refusal's `reviewId`. The harness refuses a mark that names no execution refusal of the build (`confirms_unknown_review`), and one on a question id the owner already answered or that an earlier marked question used for another refusal (`confirms_question_reused`). It keeps the mark from the owner, who reads a plain confirm, and passes it to `MintDependencies.reviewQuestion` as `confirms`. `questionForReview` carries it to the question review as `PendingQuestion.confirms`, which Guardian shows as `question_review.confirmsHeldSteps`, with the holding review's rationale.
  - Guardian binds the owner's yes to the held step itself. It records the step when a correctable refusal names `owner_confirmation`, never a final one: its entrypoint, purpose, target, screened input, the page the host last observed (origin and path) and the text of every file it loads. It records the confirm question once the question review allows it, and binds each question id to one review for good: an id asked again for another review or prompt, or first asked after an answer under it, binds no answer, so an earlier yes never stands for a later ask. When a later step matches all of that, and the host's answers hold a yes to that question with the same id and prompt, Guardian tells the review `trusted_authority.ownerConfirmedStep`. A refusal of that step that still rests on `owner_confirmation` goes back once in the same review and counts as `correctable`, never final, so it never ends the build as a `policy` block. A refusal on any other ground, such as a step that turns off two-factor sign-in, stands as Guardian returns it. A changed file, input, purpose or target, a question the review did not allow, or an answer that is not a yes binds nothing.
  - The sign-in screen rule keeps its remember-device case. A build may select a remember-device, trust-this-device or keep-me-signed-in control on the sign-in screen when trusted intent or the owner's answer authorizes it, or when the evidence shows the site will not finish the sign-in without it. The build's browser profile is the service's own, not the owner's device. Otherwise the review escalates with `owner_confirmation`, so the minter asks the owner.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1. A host that does not pass `confirms` on to `questionForReview` still gets the shared rule, but not the binding or the pushback. Guardian keeps its records in memory for the job, so a Guardian restored from a session after a restart starts with none. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact policy, tool description or decision format text needs recording again.
- A sign-in replay on a site that takes the email first now waits for the password screen while the site looks the account up. The screen's wait checked on every poll whether the screen it had just submitted still showed, and on the first poll, before the site answered, read that as the site's answer (`credential_screen_returned`), a possible silent refusal. Now the wait goes on at once when the next screen shows and still stops at once on a recorded error marker, and reads the submitted screen still showing as the site's answer only at the screen's existing deadline. A real silent refusal ends the same way, at that deadline instead of the first poll. No wait is added and no outcome changes name.
  - `makeReplayScreenWait` passes its optional `rejection` whether the wait's deadline passed. A host that passes a `rejection` taking no argument keeps its behavior. Recipes keep their shape, and `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1.
- A sign-in step whose submit stays disabled after the host typed its fields gets every field typed again key by key, then the same wait for the submit. The host typed each value with one native insertion, which fires an input event but no key events, so a page that enables its submit only on key presses, such as a code box with a keyup handler, kept it disabled, the code never reached the site, and the fill reported `stayed_disabled`. `fillAutofillStep` now types each field again through the new `InsertionOptions.keys`: in the same guarded call on the same private DevTools channel, with the same binding, origin and focus checks, each character goes in between a keydown, keypress and keyup on the field, which carry the legacy key codes (`keyCode`, `which`, `charCode`), and the checks hold again before the host inserts each character, since the page's key handlers run between them. A character whose keydown the page cancels is the page's to insert, as with a person typing. The host still never clicks a disabled submit, and the report keeps its shape: `stayed_disabled` now means the submit stayed disabled after both typings. A submit the page enables after the first typing is clicked as before, with no second typing. Run replays get the same retry through the shared fill.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and recipes are unchanged. The key events are dispatched by script, so a page that accepts only trusted key events (`isTrusted`) still keeps the submit disabled. A host with its own `CredentialKeyboard` that ignores `keys` inserts natively again. A host that counts its keyboard's insertions per field sees each field of such a step typed twice.

- A sign-in screen that asks for a company or organization ID beside the username can now be filled. The auth skill and Guardian's execution policy named `account_number` only for an account, member, customer or rewards number field, so such a field had no identifier kind a sign-in step could name, and the build could not sign in. Both now name a company or organization ID field `account_number`, which the login holds or the host asks the owner for, as it does any account number.
  - Guidance only: recipes keep their shape, and `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact execution policy or auth skill text needs recording again.
- A live test that expects `result` now passes a list read's record whose results list is null, when the tool's output schema allows null there and another of the record's declared values is filled, such as a reservation lookup whose place takes no online bookings returning its status and address with no times. `judgeCase` judges such a record by its other values, as it judges a details read, and the record still passes a case that expects `empty`, as before. Before, it failed as `no <field> list`, so a correct tool could fall under the pass bar and not publish. `primaryListOf` picks the same field as before, and its `PrimaryList` adds the optional `nullable` and `others`, the output's other declared fields. A null list the schema does not allow, or one beside no other filled value, still fails a `result` case, now with the detail "The tool returned a record whose <field> is null. If the site lists no <field> for this input, expect empty for this case, or choose an input the site lists <field> for." An empty list still reads `empty`.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1. A host that builds its own `PrimaryList` without `nullable` judges a null list as before, with the new failure detail. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact failure text needs recording again.

- A sign-in recipe's approval step, where the owner approves the sign-in on another device or by an emailed link, now replays on a new browser. The replay admitted the approval only on its exact recorded page. A site's approval page often holds a code of its own in its path, and a recording may have saved the signed-in page the site showed once the owner approved, so a new browser showed neither and every replay of such a recipe ended `screen_changed`. Now, once the screen before it was submitted, any page on the recorded page's origin is the approval's screen too, unless it is one of the recorded screens or the entry page, shows a password field or the credential screen the replay just submitted, or already shows the signed-in marker. The replay then asks the owner to approve once, as before, and the sign-in's own marker check decides. A page that already shows the marker signed in with no approval: the replay asks nothing there and goes on to that check (`skipped`, `signed_in_indicator`). A page on any other origin is never admitted, and a login form shown again ends as before (`screen_changed`).
  - Recipes keep their shape, and `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1.
- A publication review can now read each skill's reference files, the examples its skill page points to, at the path the minter reads them, `.agents/<name>/references/<file>`, beside the skill's `SKILL.md`. Before, the review served only each `SKILL.md`, so checking a finding against an example the minter had followed failed as an unavailable source, and the review could rest a finding on a reading the example contradicts. `skillReferenceFiles` in `pomerado/core/mint/skills` also lists each skill's reference files whose content is inline UTF-8 text, and leaves out any other entry. Like the skills, they are listed apart from the publication index's `files` by byte length and read on demand, so no finding names one and no file's text is added to the request. No policy or skill text changes.
  - A host that serves `skillReferenceFiles` to its reviews serves the new paths with no change. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact `trusted_publication.references` list needs recording again.
- Guardian's execution policy says an email, username or phone-only first step in the site's own sign-in dialog is a sign-in screen even when the same dialog also invites new customers to join. It judges the request by the evidence of what the submit sends: a form's native method is not evidence of the request when a captured script handles the submit. It also prefers weighing new evidence the agent cites over repeating an earlier denial. Before, Guardian could deny such a dialog's identifier step as not clearly a sign-in form, and for its form's GET method, until three denials in a row of the same step ended the build's sign-in. A registration step is still denied, and three correctable denials in a row of one unchanged sign-in step still end sign-in.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact execution policy text needs recording again.
- A takeover whose host finds the minter's started `request_input` unanswered when it rejoins it, such as a question that expired while no worker held the build, goes on as the live question would have. When the host's `agentRecovery.recoverTool` fails that call with a `MintFailure` carrying `noResponse`, the harness hands the question back to the minter as no answer where it would hand back a live one, and otherwise offers it to `MintDependencies.suspendUnanswered` (`step` `question`) and ends the build as `no_response` unless the host suspends it. Before, the failure escaped the replayed call, the model run failed and the build ended with no recorded cause. The harness never learned the replayed question's id, so the offer carries no `requestId`: a host matches it to the question it rejoined. An owner's cancel still never goes back to the minter or to the hook.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes. A host without the hook ends such a build as `no_response`.
- A download that `files.collect` takes over the host's size caps, one file over the per-file cap (`too_large`) or over what the run's caps leave (`run_limit`), now fails the script with an `OperationFailure` that names the cap and may have sent something (`dispatch` `unknown`), instead of `InvalidInput`. The page made the file, so the limit is the host's and the caller has no other input to send, yet a host read such a run as the caller's bad input and told its caller to correct a request that was fine. `files.place` still throws `InvalidInput` for a caller's file over the caps, and every other refusal keeps its error.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes. A tool built on an earlier release still throws `InvalidInput` for such a download, so a host that serves older tools and wants to tell its caller the same can note which file call the host refused, `place` or `collect`, on its own side of the file channel.
- A sign-in or card-fill step's inspection, and each call of its fill, skips a frame the page takes away while the call searches the page's frames for the step's controls, such as an ad slot or a widget that reloads itself, and reads the rest. The search read each frame with no regard for one going away, so Playwright's `Frame was detached` failed the whole call: the inspection came back `page_unavailable` even when the step's fields showed in the page itself, and a fill call failed the same way. Now a frame whose read fails once it is detached counts for nothing, along with any match the search read in it before, so a field only in that frame is `not_found`, as when the page has not drawn it yet, and a field in the page is still its one visible match. The inspection's read of the screen's buttons skips such a frame too. A read that fails in a frame still attached fails the call as before.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes.

- A sign-in or card-fill step whose form has an image submit (`<input type="image">`) with a `formaction` off the site and its sign-in origins is now refused before anything is typed (`credential_target_refused`, part `form_action`), whether it is the step's submit or another button of the form. The check before typing read each `formaction` and `formmethod` from the form's `elements`, which leave image submits out, so such a button's own destination was never judged, and a click on it, or Enter, could send the typed values there. It now reads the image submits the form owns as well, inside it or tied to it with `form=`. An image submit on the site still passes, and no exported type changes.
- The build harness reads the owner's no to a question that asks whether to end the build as keep going, never as a declined step. A minter's `request_input` confirm question may set the new optional `endsBuild` (`ProposedConfirmQuestion` in `AgentRequest`): its yes stands for `owner_ended`, and its no stands for neither `owner_declined` nor `owner_ended`, which the host refuses as `owner_kept_going`. While Guardian's denial of the latest publication stands, the host also refuses a `policy` report that stood only on that no, so a minter that then stops ends with the denial. Before, any confirm answered no let a `policy` report end as `owner_declined`, so a build whose owner asked it to keep going read as one whose owner declined a step it needed.
  - The host keeps the mark to itself: Guardian's question review and the host's `askInput` get a plain confirm, so hosts need no change, and a question without the mark works as before. A worker that takes over a build reads answers from before the takeover as plain confirms. A question the build was paused on keeps its mark when its answer arrives, since the host reads it back from the minter's own call.
- The build harness shows the minter the host's entry page notice again when only the host's own `instruction` changed, such as a host explaining that a replaced browser kept its session on the same page as an earlier replacement. It compared only the notice's state, outcome, reason and page, so a changed instruction never reached the minter, and an outdated one could stay the latest it saw. `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, no exported type changes, and a host that never sets `instruction` sees no change.
- A repair that nobody can confirm a contract change for may leave out the part of its request that would loosen or remove an output, and publish the rest. `mint_update` now lets such a repair change or remove an output field an earlier accepted update of the same build added, since the registered contract never had it. Before, the host refused that as `owner_unavailable`, so a build that added a field for a part it could not finish had to keep returning the field or end blocked.
  - The `owner_unavailable` instruction prefers leaving out the part of the request that needs the change: remove any code or output field added only for it, publish the rest, name the left-out part in coverage, and end with `report_blocked` only when no part can be published. Guardian's update policy says a field this build added is outside the registered contract and needs no confirmation or site evidence. Both add text only.
  - A host that exempts applied output changes from its output-obligation check exempts none that changes a field an earlier applied update added. Such a field owes nothing when the registered contract lacks it, and keeps the owner's confirmation when the contract has it. The harness has no registered output schema, so a build can "add" a registered field and then remove it unconfirmed, and only that host check stops it.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact policy or refusal text needs recording again.
- The controls the host saves after a sign-in step's submit, in `captures/after-submit/<step>.json`, a failed step's `lastScreen` and a filled report's `controls`, are now named by their accessible name, the name Playwright's `role=` selector matches, so a selector written from a saved name finds its control. Before, a name was its label's raw text, with any words the page hides from assistive technology, such as a required mark in an `aria-hidden` span. A password field saved as `Password *` then matched no `role=textbox[name="Password *"]`, since the selector compares the whole name, and the sign-in step was refused as `not_found`.
  - `pageControlsCode` reads each name with the new page code `accessibleNames`. It leaves out words hidden by `aria-hidden`, `display: none` or `visibility: hidden`, unless the label or referenced element that holds them is hidden itself. It adds CSS generated text, such as a required mark drawn with `::after`, spaces block-level words apart, and reads an image's alt text and an input button's value or default name. A control the page hides is named as an `include-hidden` selector names it.
  - It keeps what was typed out of a name as far as page code can tell. A field, an editable element or a control that holds a value adds nothing to a name it sits in, and a placeholder never names a field. An editable control, such as an editable `div` with a text box, button or option role, is never named by its own words; its `aria-label` or label still names it. CSS generated text adds nothing when it holds, at two characters or more, one of its element's attribute values other than its class, id or style, as `attr()` reads them, or a value a text field or editable element holds now in the same document or its open shadow roots, since a page may draw what was typed from an attribute, a custom property or a rule its script adds. Text a page draws from a value no such field holds any more, or one in another frame, is not caught, and the host still screens names for typed values.
  - It reads names in the same in-page pass, with no extra call per control. A name that cannot be read is null, and the frame's other controls and alerts are still saved. `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes.
  - `accessibleNames` is adapted from Playwright under the Apache License 2.0. The package ships Playwright's license and notice in `third-party/playwright/`.

- A sign-in step's submit whose click outlasts its timeout while the next page loads now counts as clicked once a request for the page's main frame went out, as a form's submission sends. Playwright's click waits, inside its 5 s timeout, for the navigation it starts to commit. So a form the site answered at once with a redirect, but whose chain of redirects reached the next page only after 5 s, as a slow network or proxy makes it, came back `submit: "failed"`. A run's sign-in then failed as `submit_failed` although the site had signed the browser in, the host counted what the step filled as never sent, and the minter heard it could click that submit itself, which sends the form again. The submit call now notes each request for a navigation of the main frame from its click on. When the click, or a submit key's press, throws after such a request went out, whatever the site answered, the fill reports `clicked`. It gives that navigation the usual settle wait to commit and load, then reads the page as after any click, so the sign-in's own check decides what the submit did. A click that throws with no such request is `failed`, and one another element caught is `blocked`, as before. A submit the site answers within the timeout takes the same path as before.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes. The controlled shop from `pomerado/testing/shop-fixture` adds `sessionHops` and `sessionHopMs`, which send its two-screen sign-in's session post through slow redirects; both are 0 by default.

- A sign-in step's `dismiss` can now clear a covering notice whose own control sits in the sign-in form or would submit a form, such as a button with no type inside a form that wraps the whole page. Before, the host refused it as `dismiss_refused` (`sign_in_form` or `submits_form`), Guardian denied it, and the notice stayed over the sign-in, so the sign-in could not finish on such a page, and a fresh browser met the same notice every time. The host now clicks such a control only when it sits in a notice that covers one of the step's controls, and the submitting controls a click of it reaches all sit in that notice too. It first empties the step's fields, so nothing typed sits in the form while the page's own code runs, and refuses to click when a field stays filled. For that click alone, capture listeners on the notice's window cancel the default action of each click that reaches the notice and every submit event, so the page's own handler runs and neither the click nor the handler's own `requestSubmit` submits a form. The listeners come off once the tasks the click queued ran. What the page's own code still sends while the fields are empty, by a form's `submit()`, `fetch` or XHR, carries nothing typed. A submission the page delays until after the host types again sends the values typed again, once, to the form's judged action. When the page's own code starts a navigation, the dismiss waits for the new document, and the next call finds the step's controls again before anything is typed. A fill whose notice caught its submit's click types the fields again after such a dismiss and submits once; a click again (`resubmitAutofillStep`) finds the fields emptied and clicks nothing (`submit: "not_attempted"`), so the recorder fills the screen again as a refill, within the password's two fills. The step's submit, its fields, links to another page, a control outside the notice and a notice that covers none of the step's controls stay refused, and every other dismiss works as before.
  - Guardian's execution policy allows such a covering banner's own control as the banner's own control, since it sends nothing typed: the host empties the step's fields and cancels that click's own submission. Its other denials still apply, such as the step's submit, a field or a link to another page. The auth skill says the same, and prefers not to name a link to another page as `dismiss`. The minter's `dismiss_refused` notice names the narrower refusal.
  - `pomerado.hostProtocol` stays 1, and no exported type changes. A recorded Guardian or minter response keyed on the exact policy, skill or notice text needs recording again.

- `pickValue` and `pickValues` type a value once more, key by key, when the list it answered ends empty or holds no row the author's `option` selector keeps, instead of refusing it at once with an empty `available`. A page still starting up can drop the first typing and ask its site for nothing, or draw a slower answer to an empty term, such as its default list for a new visitor, over the typed term's. They typed again only when the list held some row. The picker first waits, up to 2 s, until none of the page's own requests, whatever started them, polls aside, is in flight and none started or ended for `settleMs` from when it began waiting (a request the page started before the call shows only by its end), then clears the field and types the value with keys, never pressing Enter or a popup's Search button. It does so once a call, after the last spelling of the value, and only with more than a third of the call's `timeout` left; never with `create` or the author's `type`. Typing again that fails, such as one that runs out of time, refuses as the empty list did, so a call that refused before still refuses, now listing what the site offered once it answered. A pick from a list that answered the first time takes the same path and time as before; a value the site lacks whose list ends empty takes up to the wait and one more settle longer to refuse.
- Guardian's question review sees a multiple choice's `minSelections` and `maxSelections`. `questionForReview` left them out, so the review could not tell that a question allowed no picks, and kept rewording the workspace guide's capability question, which asks with `minSelections` 0, to set the field it already had. A multiple choice's projection now carries both, and the question policy names them among what the request shows. A single choice and every other question keep their shape. A host that builds its `PendingQuestion` with `questionForReview` gets them by updating. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact question policy text or review request needs recording again.
- `declaredRefusalReasons` from `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome` reads two more output shapes, so a write's declared refusal in either one completes its run. A branch that is only a union, with nothing beside its `anyOf` or `oneOf` but annotations, now counts as its own branches, up to eight unions deep: Effect writes `Schema.Union(Placed, Schema.Union(RefusedA, RefusedB))` as an `anyOf` inside an `anyOf`, which used to read as one branch that could match any `status`, so the schema declared no refusal. And a branch whose `status` is a string with a `pattern` that `refused` does not match, read with the `u` flag, such as `Schema.String.pipe(Schema.pattern(/^(?!refused$).+$/))`, no longer counts as one that could be refused. A free-text `status`, a pattern that matches `refused`, an invalid pattern, and a nested union holding such a branch still declare none. Every schema that declared a refusal before declares the same reasons, so a host reads the new shapes by updating, with nothing for a tool to publish again.
- A minter run with a recovery boundary now saves its run state after a reply that splits its cache writes by lifetime. Since the static prefix ends at a 12-hour cache breakpoint, the provider reports a call's cache writes with a nested `cache_write_tokens_details` (`ttl_30m_tokens`, `ttl_12h_tokens`), which the SDK copies into its usage as given. Its saved run state accepts only numeric token details, so the save before the next model call failed and the build ended unavailable after its first call. The minter now returns each reply with numeric usage details only, after reporting the call's counts, so the response a recovery boundary saves also decodes when a resumed attempt replays it: a nested count stays as `<key>.<inner key>`, such as `cache_write_tokens_details.ttl_12h_tokens`, and the usage counts read either form, so the per-lifetime counts a call reports are unchanged.
- A `host_refused` report now stands on a sign-in screen the host refused at its destination check, so it ends the build as the host's refusal, or as sign-in unavailable while a sign-in the build sent is unconfirmed. The host types nothing into a screen whose field, form or submit would send the login somewhere credentials may not go, and returns that inspection's `credential_target_refused` as the step's result rather than a failure, so the harness had no host refusal on record and refused the report as `host_not_refused`, while the minter changed selectors that could never pass the check and could end the build under a reason that told its caller to change the request. The new optional `MintDependencies.signInDestinationRefused` says whether the host refused such a screen with no later screen that takes a password or code passing that check and no sign-in verified since. A screen with no such field, such as an identifier screen, says nothing of where the password would go, so its passing leaves the refusal standing. The harness reads it after each authenticate, a `host_refused` report stands on its latest answer as on any host refusal of a sign-in, and `MintHarnessSnapshot` keeps that answer as the optional `signInDestinationRefused`, so a successor keeps it. A refusal the minter can correct, such as a selector that found nothing, still leaves the report refused. The local host's recorder answers the dependency. A host without the dependency is unchanged.
- A page wait's look at the page's generic loading signs costs one page call, however many elements carry a hint class. `waitCode`'s waits counted the busy regions and progress bars, then the skeleton, shimmer, spinner and loader classes, with two visible-filtered locator counts, and a locator count on Patchright costs time for each element it matches: a page whose image wrappers all name a loader, a few hundred of them, took several seconds a look, before the action and at every look after it. One page evaluation now counts both, through open shadow roots, as a visible locator match counts them, a `display: contents` sign by its child elements and text. A sign inside a closed shadow root, which a Patchright locator could count, is out of page code's reach and no longer counts. A sign element in content the browser skips rendering (`content-visibility`) does not show, and is not laid out to check, since reading its box laid that content out one element at a time. A `display: contents` sign is still judged by its children there, its text by a range's box, as the visible check judges it. A look whose count runs past 250 ms in the page leaves the class hints uncounted and takes their last count as unchanged. What counts as progress is otherwise unchanged: the author's `loading`, busy regions, progress bars, class hints new since the action, region changes, requests and reads.
- `pickValue`, `pickValues` and `pickerOptions` say why a list still counted as loading when they fail as `options_loading`. The message stays the fixed reason. The failure's new `loading`, and its stack's first line, which a host logs with a failed call, name what still showed at the last look, as kinds and counts, never page text: each new loading sign's kind, tag and place, loading rows, a busy list, the requests the picker's action started still in flight with their types and the oldest one's age, whether `answered` was still false, and how many options it read when the list was not read to its end.
- A publication denial no correction gets past whose every finding is in a bundle file the host wrote now ends as the host's failure. `decodePublicationDecision` sets the reason `host_owned` itself on a denial that `publicationRefusalUnfixable` counts as unfixable, one for privacy or authority or with a finding such as `private_literal`, when every one of its findings is in an `operation/` file that the review's index marks `owner: host`, as a host does through `publicationScope`'s `hostWritten`, such as for its sign-in recipe. The decision's rationale starts with a note that the host set the reason and names the reason the review gave. Before, such a denial kept whatever reason the review chose, such as `source_correction` for a `private_literal` in the recipe, and let the minter end the build as its own `policy` block instead of the host's failure. Every other denial keeps the review's reason, so a fix the minter can make still goes back to it: much of the recipe records the minter's own sign-in choices, such as a signed-in marker that also shows signed out, which it fixes by signing in again. So do a denial with a finding in a file the minter wrote, in `publication/definition.json` or in `publication/tests.json`, input feedback, an escalation and a denial with no finding. A host that marks no bundle file as its own, as the local host, sees no change. Guardian's publication decision policy says the host sets this reason, so a finding the minter's source causes also goes on that source.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- An attempt that a second `InvalidOutcome` review ended, in which Guardian again returned no outcome its review allows, now has a typed ending: `MintOutcome.stopReason` is `host_unavailable`, since the host and not the task ended it, and the attempt's diagnostics add `{ phase: "review", reason: "review_unavailable", code: "InvalidOutcome", review }`, naming the review kind. It still has no host failure, so a host requeues nothing for it. Such a build used to end with no recorded cause, which a host could only read as a missing publication, even after one of its writes had applied.
  - The follow-up that tells Guardian, once in the same review, that its outcome is not one the review returns now names the outcome it refused beside the ones the review returns, and, for a built-in kind, which of those holds a change it wants first: a deny or an escalate, as its policy says, for an execution review, a deny for a recovery or publication review, a reword for a question review, and, for a task update review, a clarify when the update needs the caller's confirmation and lacks it or gives it ambiguously, or a reword when it is wrong as written. A reviewer that meant the agent should change or ask something first answered with another kind's outcome again. The `InvalidOutcome` failure's message names the refused outcome too, when it is one of the shared output format's, and never a private host kind's.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact follow-up text needs recording again.
- A sign-in replay matches its recorded screens with a trailing slash on their paths dropped, except on the root `/`. `offScreens` and `leftSignInScreens` from `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-replay-pages`, the default screens of `makeReplayScreenWait` from `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-replay-screens` and a replay session's own screens compared raw addresses, so a recorded login screen such as `/login` reached at `/login/`, or recorded at `/login/` and reached at `/login`, read as a page off the recorded screens. A sign-in whose check failed on its own login page with such a difference read that page as one past the sign-in that can't tell, and reported `outcome: "signed_in"` with `cantTell: true`. A page now matches a screen when its origin and its path are the screen's, a trailing slash aside, and a workspace screen's path compares the same way on the workspace origin. The default screens are exported as `exactScreens`, which matches each screen only on its recorded origin, so a workspace screen, which records a path alone, matches no page there. Every replay session passes its own screens, which put a workspace screen on the workspace origin the replay knows, so the default applies only to a caller of `makeReplayScreenWait` that passes none. `pathOf` and `placeOf` from `pomerado/core/runtime/session-sign-in` are exported too: the path and the page, origin and path, as the screens compare them. `placeOf` also takes `undefined`, and returns `undefined` for it or for an address that is not absolute. Reported and fixed by an outside contributor (https://github.com/Pomerado/pomerado/issues/391).
- Autofill types into sign-in fields inside open, nested or closed shadow roots. Its two focus checks, after focusing a field and just before the native insertion, compared the field with its document's `activeElement`, which for a field in a shadow root is the outermost shadow host, so such a field was refused as `credential_target_refused` (`not_focused`). Both checks now read the field's own root, whose `activeElement` is the field only when the field itself holds the focus, so a sibling in the same shadow root that takes the focus is still refused. A `not_focused` refusal's `activeTag` names the deepest focused element it can read through open shadow roots, and a closed root's host.
- `publicDefinition` from `pomerado/core/mint/publication-review`, the definition a publication review reads at `publication/definition.json`, shows each reference in the input and output schemas that has a constraint beside it inside `allOf`, beside that constraint, through the new `withLoneReferences` from `pomerado/core/registry/schema-references`. Effect writes a refinement of a named schema, such as `Schema.NonEmptyString.pipe(Schema.maxLength(4096))`, as `$ref` with `maxLength` beside it, and the published text declares Draft-07, which ignores every keyword beside `$ref`; a review that read that text to the letter judged the bound missing and refused the schema as not matching the tool. The view keeps every keyword and value of the published schema, `$schema`, `$id` and every definition included, and leaves data such as defaults, examples, constants and enums as they are, as well as a reference beside an `allOf` that is not a list, so the review still reads all the text that ships. The published contract (`contractJsonSchema`) is unchanged, and so is every other use of the schemas.
  - A host that builds its own publication review from the published schemas can apply `withLoneReferences` to show the same view.
- `canonicalSchema` and `inlineLocalRefs` no longer drop a constraint when they merge a reference, or a schema that is `allOf` one schema, with the keywords beside it. When both sides set a validation keyword to different values, such as a named trimmed string's `pattern` and a refinement's own `pattern`, the keyword beside the reference used to replace the referenced one, so the inlined schema accepted values the published one refused; the referenced value now stays in `allOf`. The same holds for Effect's two refinements on one keyword, such as `Named.pipe(Schema.pattern(a), Schema.pattern(b))`, which Effect writes as `{ $ref, pattern: b, allOf: [{ pattern: a }] }`: the inlined schema used to keep only `b`, and now keeps `a`, `b` and the named schema's own pattern. When the keywords beside a reference change ones the referenced schema's keywords depend on, such as `properties` beside a referenced `additionalProperties: false`, `prefixItems` or `items` beside a referenced `items`, `maxContains` beside a referenced `contains`, or `else` beside a referenced `if`, the whole referenced schema stays in `allOf`. Annotations such as `description` beside a reference still replace the referenced schema's. The canonical form changes only for schemas with such a conflict; every other schema inlines as before.
- The refusal of a step that repeats a write no longer tells the minter to wait for an outcome review when the host wires none (`MintDependencies.outcomeReview` absent). It used to say the write stays refused until its outcome review finds it did not happen, a review that never comes there, which led a minter to send the write again through a copy of the step. Without an outcome reviewer, the refusal now says the step stays refused for the rest of the attempt, never to run a copy of it to send the write again, and to read back what happened in a new file that changes nothing. With one, its note on reusing the write's file says the step stays refused only until the outcome review finds the earlier write did not happen, and then to run that step itself again, not a copy.
  - A recorded minter response keyed on the exact refusal text needs recording again.
- Guardian's execution review treats a sign-in field as a valid code slot when its label, name, id or placeholder asks for a one-time, verification or authenticator code, or when it is the screen's only text input and the screen's own prompt asks for that code, even when it is a plain text box without `autocomplete="one-time-code"` or a numeric `type` or `inputmode`. A username, email, password, search, recovery or backup code, card security code, security question answer, captcha, voucher or promo field never takes a code, nor does a field whose own label, name or id names such a value. The policy used to call the slot a "one-time-code field", which reviews could read as requiring that autocomplete token, so the same field was allowed in one review and refused as final in another.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A `host_refused` report now ends a build as sign-in unavailable once the host refused a sign-in screen because the login's password fills are spent. The host fills a password at most twice in one sign-in and refuses every later screen that needs it as `credential_already_filled`, a step result rather than a failure, so the harness had no host refusal on record and refused the report as `host_not_refused`, while the minter tried recoveries that could not sign in.
