The new optional `MintDependencies.signInRefillRefused` says whether the host refused such a screen and the password is still spent, with no sign-in verified and no corrected login since, and a `host_refused` report stands on it as on any host refusal of a sign-in; with the sign-in unconfirmed, the build ends as sign-in unavailable. Spent fills with no screen refused yet, and a fill that remains, leave the report refused as before. `passwordFillsSpent` from `pomerado/core/runtime/autofill-state` reads the spent state from a host's sign-in state, and the local host's recorder answers the dependency. A host without the dependency is unchanged.
- A sign-in's code screen whose one-character boxes the page limits by its own script, with no `maxlength`, now gives each box its `codeBox` in the inspection's `screen.fields`, so a step that names every box can be allowed and one that names only some of them is refused as `code_boxes_incomplete`. Such a box is a visible, enabled text input with no `maxlength` limit whose parent element has two to twelve such inputs as children, all of one type, when the step names two or more code fields; its place counts among them. When the step's boxes found so disagree on how many there are, or two share a place, as boxes split between two containers do, none of them is a box, as before. Boxes whose `maxlength` is 1 count as before. The step format, the recipe version and the fill are unchanged.
- A sign-in's code screen whose script-limited boxes each sit alone in their own wrapper element, side by side in one container, now gives each box its `codeBox`, as boxes side by side in one parent element already did. A box that is the only such input in its parent counts among the inputs one level up when every child of the parent's parent holds one such input as its only element; the same type, `maxlength`, count and agreement checks apply. Elements that show no box, such as a hidden input, a style element or a message not shown, count at neither level. Boxes wrapped more deeply, a container that also shows a bare box or another element, such as a separator, and a wrapper that shows anything beside its box, such as a label, give no `codeBox`, as before. Boxes side by side in one parent element and boxes whose `maxlength` is 1 count as before. The step format, the recipe version and the fill are unchanged.
- A `host_refused` report no longer ends a build on a host refusal a later `finish_build` got past. The harness kept one flag per attempt that any host refusal set and nothing cleared, so after the minter fixed a refusal that named its fix, such as `variants_unsupported`, and Guardian's review then denied the build with a correction, `host_refused` was still accepted and the build ended as if nothing left in the attempt could fix it. A `finish_build` that gets past the host's checks to Guardian's review now clears the refusal, and a `host_refused` report with no host refusal since is refused as the new reason `host_refusal_passed`, with an instruction to fix the findings the latest refusal names and call `finish_build` again. A host refusal of that same `finish_build`, or a later one, stands as before, and `host_not_refused` is unchanged. `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `hostRefusalPassed`, so a takeover keeps the refusal cleared; an older worker ignores it. A passed host refusal still keeps a report from ending as sign-in unavailable, as it did before it was cleared.
- A `host_refused` report no longer ends a build right after a host refusal whose own instruction names the step that fixes it. A minter often reported the build blocked within minutes of such a refusal, such as `variants_unsupported`, instead of taking the step. When the latest host refusal is a publication refusal of that kind, the first `host_refused` report on its reason is now refused as the new reason `host_refusal_names_step`, and the build goes on; a second report is taken, so a minter that still cannot get past it can stop. Those are core's refusals whose instruction names such a step, and a host's `no_match_case_untested` when it carries a review rationale, which names its step there. The reply carries `refusedReason`, core's screened instruction as `refusalInstruction` for core's own refusals, and the screened rationale as `refusalRationale` when the refusal carried one. A sign-in screen the host refused because the password's fills are spent, or at its destination check, standing beside it takes the report at once, as before. Which reasons were offered, and the instructions and rationales, are kept in memory only, so a takeover may refuse one report again, or take one on a host's refusal at once; it reads the latest refusal from `hostRefusalSources`, and a checkpoint from an older worker decodes as before.
  - A host refusal of a submission the host already refused, sent again with nothing changed after a later `finish_build` got past that refusal to Guardian's review, now records no new refusal. A minter could otherwise send such a submission to make a basis for `host_refused` after its report was refused as `host_refusal_passed`. The report right after it is refused as the new reason `host_refusal_repeated`. A changed submission's refusal stands as before.

- The local minter retries a model call whose connection was lost with the same request, as it already did for a provider error: a reset, broken pipe, timeout or failed lookup, found on the error or a cause beneath it, such as the bare `TypeError: terminated` the HTTP client throws when a response stream is cut after its headers. It sends the whole call again, never resuming a stream, within the existing delays, time budget and attempt deadline. Before, only SDK errors named for a connection failure, a rate limit or a server error were retried, so a cut stream ended the build.

- A write's run now accepts, without asking, a confirm its build accepted whose message names the record, such as "Finalize <name>?", when the run's confirm names the run's own record. The record of an accepted confirm was a digest of its exact message, so it never matched once a run's record differed, and every such run asked its caller. Before taking the digest, `observedConfirmOf` replaces each whole-token occurrence of a value of the execution's input (a string or number, named by JSON pointer) or of an identifier in the page's URL (a path segment or query value holding a digit, other than an amount such as `50.00`) with the place it came from. A value counts from two characters when it holds a digit and from three otherwise, and only one of four or more characters matches in any letter case, so a code such as `OR` or `CAN` never turns the site's own words into a placeholder. A run matches such a record only when the same wording names its own values from the same places, so other wording, a record the run was not asked about, or two values in swapped roles still ask. A confirm that names no value keeps its exact digest, byte for byte, and a run still matches records published before.
  - `observedConfirmOf` and `makeExpectedConfirms` take the input as an optional last argument, `keepAcceptedConfirm` reads an optional `input` beside the decision, and `makeRunDialogDecision` an optional `input`. `ObservedConfirm` adds the optional host-only `template`, of the new exported type `ConfirmTemplate`. Migrate a host that decides a run's dialogs by passing the run's input, and a host that records accepted confirms by passing the input each live execution ran with. Wire the run side first or in the same release: a templated record matches only a run that passes its input, so a run that does not would ask about a confirm it used to accept.
  - A record kept from a templated confirm holds a new digest form that a host from an older release never matches, so such a run there asks its caller, as before.
- `providerKeyPattern` keeps a key whose body holds a separator followed by another key's shape as one key, so error messages and the local publication precheck mask and refuse it whole. A body that may hold `-`, `+`, `/` or `=` used to end at any such separator followed by a listed key's shape, so a key such as `sk-proj-…-bl_…_…` left its tail readable, and a short `sk-…` body followed by an identifier was not found at all. A separator now ends a body only before a key whose prefix the body cannot read, as in `whsec_…/ghp_…`. A key written into a body that cannot read it whole is read whole by its own rule: an `xox…-` body reads an `sk-…` key at its start or after a hyphen, and an `sk-` body, which is never cut, reads a `whsec_…` key with its `+`, `/` and `=`. `isProviderKey` accepts an `sk-` match that holds a key such as `xoxb-…` after one of its hyphens. The pattern also starts with `\b`, which is the same word start as before for every listed prefix.
- A sign-in step whose one-time code spans more than six one-character boxes now decodes. `SignInStep` and every sign-in recipe version refused a step of more than six fields, so a code of seven or more boxes, one `code` field per box, could not be expressed at all. A step's code fields now have their own bound, up to 12 (`maximumCodeBoxes`), the longest common one-time code, and `maximumStepFields`, still 6, bounds only its other fields, so a step with seven fields besides its code fields is still refused. `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-contracts` adds `maximumCodeBoxes`, `stepFieldsFit` and `stepFieldsMessage`. A host on an earlier version refuses a recipe whose step has more than six fields as `invalid`, rather than replaying part of it.
- A retired live-test failure that no case could ever excuse no longer holds publication review in a loop. Such a failure, a case whose input the tool now refuses on purpose, kept counting in `publication/tests.json` with nothing to clear it, so the review asked again and again for a record the minter cannot correct, and each rebuilt case added another retired failure. Each new way out rests on a fact the host checks or on the site's own answer in the captures, never on a relabelled case or the minter's account.
  - A retired failure whose input supplies a value the version's published input schema now bounds is marked `outsideSchema`, with the JSON pointer `path` and the JSON Schema `keyword` it breaks, such as `/query` and `maxLength`, or a property the schema no longer takes (`additionalProperties`), and stops counting. A field the schema newly requires, an error at the input's root and one under a conditional subschema such as `anyOf` never mark it, since the schema still accepts every value the input sent; every error is read, not only the first. The host checks it with the classic (draft-07) Ajv engine the MCP SDK ships, formats unchecked. `LiveTestVersionResults` in `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` adds the optional `inputSchema`, the input schema that version publishes. The review then judges only the bound: it is a finding when the captures show the site accepts values past it or it excludes a value the site offers.
  - `excusedBy` also names a case that passes the same input after the source changed with a refusal the retired case's own expectation accepts under `judgeCase`, such as `InvalidInput` for a case that expected `error`. A refusal that passes on the same source as the failure, or an `InputNotApplied` pass for a case that expected `invalid_input`, excuses nothing.
  - Each retired failure that still counts carries `clearedBy`: `ways`, the ways open to it (`changedExpectationBy`, `excusedBy`, and `outsideSchema` only when the host read the version's input schema); `siteAnswer`, when it has a `changedExpectationBy`, the site's own answer for that input a capture must show (`results`, `no_results` or `refusal`), with `refusalStatuses` for a refusal; and a `note`. The record's coverage line says how many retired failures still count.
  - Guardian's publication policy accepts, as the evidence a `changedExpectationBy` lead needs, a capture of the site's own controls answering that exact input: its no-results state for `empty`, its own results for `result`, such as near matches for a query nothing matches exactly, and for `invalid_input` or `error` its own validation message on its own form or, where the captures hold the site's responses, a 400, 413, 414 or 422 response to the request its own form sent. The request a run of the tool made never counts, since the tool built it, and neither does a 401, 403, 404, 405, 429, 5xx, timeout or challenge. A case that expects `error`, `empty` or `invalid_input` for an input the tool claims to handle is held to the same test. A finding about `publication/tests.json` names the case, input or schema change that clears it, never a correction of the record, and is reason `evidence`, naming the missing evidence, when nothing the minter can change clears it.
  - A failure whose rerun on the same source and case is inconclusive, such as a challenge or a site block, stays the case's result and keeps counting. Before, any rerun that did not fail marked it flaky, a retired failure the review read as a race nothing could clear, so a site that often challenges the browser held publication every round. Only a rerun that passes marks a failure flaky now.
  - A finding about `publication/tests.json` is reason `evidence` only when the minter can gather what clears it, such as the capture a `changedExpectationBy` lead needs. A failure that a source fix and a passing case of its input would clear stays a finding even when the attempt ran out of time for live tests; the review notes a retired failure in its rationale only when no case, source, schema or capture change could ever clear it. The minter's instruction after an `evidence` denial adds exploring the site's controls for a named capture, and says that running the same cases again on the same source clears no retired failure.
  - The testing skill no longer has an over-long value the schema refuses expect `invalid_input` in a case, lets the schema carry a bound the site itself puts on a value, lists the site answers that let a case change its expectation, says that an inconclusive rerun leaves a failure standing, and says how a retired failure is cleared.
  - Migrate a host that builds its own live-test record with `liveTestsEvidence` by passing each version's published `inputSchema`. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A maintenance update that loosens a published tool's contract is refused as `owner_unavailable` when the host's `taskUpdateConfirmer` returns anything but `owner` or `owner_request`, such as a misspelled word from a JavaScript host, and Guardian's review fact carries only a known value. The host is asked once per update, not once at the gate and again for the review.
- An Effect operation, such as an HTTP version, that calls `startList` with a cursor it cannot use now fails with the typed input refusal, `InvalidInput` with `field: "cursor"` and the refusal's `kind` and message, as a Kernel script does. `startList` throws its refusal, and `executeOperation` used to report that throw as a defect, which a host that calls `executeOperation` itself showed as a generic failure; the local host's child process already reported it as the refusal. `executeOperation` now fails the run with the input refusal (`errors.InvalidInput` from `pomerado/runtime`) when the operation's `run` throws it outside an Effect and it is the run's only error. It matches the refusal by shape, so a refusal from another copy of the runtime, such as one a tool bundles, counts too. Any other defect, the runner's own schema `InvalidInput` thrown as one included, and a cause that holds another error beside the refusal, such as a finalizer's, stay as they were. Its error type includes the refusal.
  - `_tag` no longer tells `executeOperation`'s two `InvalidInput` failures apart: both are tagged `InvalidInput`. The schema failure, `InvalidInput` from `pomerado/core/runtime/errors`, carries `operation` and may carry `issues`; the input refusal carries neither, and may carry `field`, `available` and `kind`. Migrate a host that narrows by `_tag === "InvalidInput"` to read `issues` by checking `instanceof InvalidInput` from `pomerado/core/runtime/errors`, or that `operation` is present.
- A code split across one-character boxes that the page posts as one field per box, rather than joined, now counts as sent. The seen-sent rule also reads each stretch of consecutive one-character values exactly as long as the pending code, in a request's query, form-encoded body or JSON arrays and objects, joined in order, so a one-character value beside the boxes, such as a remember-me flag or a step number, stays out of the code. Before, such a request never carried the whole code, so a build's sign-in verified on its password alone and every run of the published tool failed its sign-in as `sign_in_check_failed`.
- A sign-in step whose code is split across several code fields is submitted only once each box holds exactly one character. A widget that emptied a box as the next was typed used to have the host click the submit with part of the code, which the site rejected as a wrong code; now that box fails and the submit is not attempted.
- A live test case that expects results and runs page two now fails when every row page two returned was already on page one, as when the tool ignores its cursor and reads page one again. `judgeCase` from `pomerado/core/mint/live-tests` compares the two pages' rows of a list read; a page two with one new row still passes.
- A sign-in step with a submit whose form submits itself on its last input, as some one-time code screens do, is reported sent by the page (`submit: "page"`) rather than refused. The host typed every field, the page loaded its answer as a new document before the host clicked, and the click then found the screen gone, so `fillAutofillStep` reported `refused` and a replay failed the screen as `submit_refused`, though the sign-in had gone through. `fillAutofillStep` now reports `page` on such a step when its submit call finds a field gone and the primary page's document is no longer the one the host typed the last field in. When the fields are gone but the document is the same, as on a form that hides itself while its script sends what was typed and loads the answer after, the host looks again without clicking for the step's settle wait, at most 2 s, and reports `page` if a new document replaces it. The host never clicked, so nothing went out twice, and the sign-in's next check decides what the page sent, as for a step with no submit. A form that takes its fields away, draws itself again or changes its address with pushState loaded no new document, and is still refused.
- The writes skill prefers placing the first commit mark at the first step that saves on the site. A write that marked its opening steps, before anything was saved, made every later failure read as possibly sent, so the host could not retry it and a repair had to prove nothing was created.
- The writes skill tells a write build to end with `report_blocked` when the owner stops it or its session can't reach the task's end, with `site_declined` when the site refused the example, and says a build whose session never reached the end publishes nothing. A write session that met the site's refusal partway through could go on and publish a tool its example never finished.
- Guardian's question review prefers keeping a write's question about a blank field the form shows when the capture can't tell whether the field is required, since a wrong guess can fail the save. It used to strike such a question as an optional field left at its default when the capture showed no required marker, and the save then failed on the field the site required. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- `redactDiagnosticText` from `pomerado/core/runtime/failure-detail` now redacts credentials in text starting with `//`. Leading double slashes were treated as a route, so a credential written directly after them was left readable.
- A write step the host stopped before anything reached the site, such as when the job sandbox's copy of its source did not match the reviewed source (`execution.dispatch` `not_sent`), was tracked as a write whose result was lost. Its resubmission, which the host's own notice asked for, was refused as a repeat of a write that may have applied, and the build could end with that write reported as possibly applied. A failure whose `execution.dispatch` is `not_sent` now records the step as `not_sent`, so it is neither tracked as a write nor fenced, and the same step may run again.
- A sign-in check now normalizes non-root trailing slashes on recorded sign-in screens and marker paths. `sessionOnPage` and `markerCantTell` from `pomerado/core/runtime/session-sign-in` compared raw pathnames from `URL.parse`, so a recorded login screen such as `/login` reached at `/login/`, or an account path such as `/account` reached at `/account/`, failed to match. A failed sign-in that ended on its recorded login screen with a trailing slash could read as `markerCantTell` and count as signed in, and an expired session on `/account/` read as `unknown` instead of `signed_out` and skipped signing in again. Non-root paths now strip a trailing slash for comparison, matching `pathOf` in `signed-in-marker.ts`, while the root path `/` and distinct origins remain exact. Resolves https://github.com/Pomerado/pomerado/issues/259.
- Guardian's conversation compaction reads the prompt cache. The provider's compaction inside the request that crossed the threshold processed the whole conversation, about 240,000 tokens, uncached. A Guardian session, the outcome reviewer's included, now compacts before such a request by sending the last request again, unchanged, with a compaction trigger after its input, as the minter's idle compaction does, and continues from the returned compaction item. The threshold stays 240,000 tokens, and the provider's compaction stays as the backstop. The compaction call counts in the review's `guardian.usage` and as a compaction during the review.
- The signed-in marker's signed-out check read a role name whose `i` or `s` flag follows its quoted value after a space, such as `role=link[name="account" i]`, as text to find and reported the marker absent from a signed-out page that showed it. It now reads the flag as Playwright does, after a space or not and in either case.
- A script's question accepts long option values and shortens long labels instead of failing. `ScriptOption` from `pomerado/runtime` no longer limits `value` to 256 characters, since the value never leaves the run. A `label` over 256 characters reaches the caller as its first 255 characters and "…", cut so it never splits a surrogate pair. Such an option used to fail the whole question as `InvalidOptions`. Empty values and labels, repeated values and fields an option doesn't take still fail as before. Two labels that read the same once shortened stay separate options, each returning its own value. The host's `InputOption` label keeps its 256-character limit.
- A script's question that fails as `InvalidOptions` over one option now says which question and which option, so a run's error is no longer Effect's bare "An error has occurred". `ScriptInputFailure` from `pomerado/runtime` adds an optional `message`, such as "Script question seat could not be asked because option 2 has an empty value.", with options numbered from 1. The reason is one of four fixed phrases: "has an empty value", "has an empty label", "repeats another option's value" or "has a field options do not take". It names the first option that fails, and never an option's value or label. Every other failure keeps only its code, as before: an option that is not an object, a value or label that is missing or not text, an `accountSpecific` that is not a boolean, a `maskedLabel` that is empty, longer than 64 characters or not text, a choice given no options or options that are not a list, options given to another type, an empty list or more than 50 options, selection bounds the options can't meet, and more than eight questions in one `ask`.
  - The text is the failure's own `message`. A host that already reports a script failure's message, such as the local host's child process, shows it with no change, and the failure's `code` is unchanged. A host that constructs `ScriptInputFailure` itself needs no change either.
- A build's read-or-write question no longer dead-ends when Guardian's question review rejects it. That turn offers no other tool, so a minter that withdrew the question, as the host's instruction allowed, ended the build with no tool. For that question, `question_rejected` now tells the minter to reword it and call `request_input` again, and says the host sets its two answer labels, so only the prompt changes. Every other question keeps the reword-or-withdraw instruction.
  - Guardian's rule for the read-or-write question says the host requires it and sets its answer labels, so the review judges only the prompt and prefers allow when the prompt plainly says what the tool would do, even when trusted intent already leans one way. A prompt may say that a write build does the requested task once, for real, while it builds.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Guardian's question review now knows the build's effect, so it no longer rejects the true statement that a write build publishes only after it does the requested task once, for real. Its `allowedEffects` stays empty on purpose.
  - The harness passes the decided effect to `MintDependencies.reviewQuestion` as the new optional `buildEffect`, `read` or `write`, when it reviews the minter's questions, its blocked report and its stop text. It is absent on the read-or-write question's turn.
  - `questionForReview` and `PendingQuestion` in `pomerado/core/guardian/question` take the optional `buildEffect`, and the review reads it as `question_review.buildEffect`. A host that builds its own facts for `questionForReview` forwards it; one that doesn't leaves it out, as before. The local host forwards it.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Guardian's `new_mint` on a `mint_update` the minter recommended as `update` no longer ends the build. The host returns it as Guardian's `clarification_required`, with the rationale, and the minter asks the caller to confirm the change. Such an ending used to tell the caller to start a new build for the task they asked for, often with no suggested request to send. Only the minter's own `new_mint` recommendation, which needs a `suggestedRequest`, now ends a build as `new_mint_recommended`.
- Guardian's `new_mint` on a `mint_update` the minter recommended as `update` sends the minter to ask the caller once per change. When Guardian finds another same-task update a new build before an update applies, whatever the retry's wording, the result is `reword` with Guardian's rationale, not another `clarification_required`, so the minter revises the change instead of asking the caller again. A build used to ask the caller after each of Guardian's new-build calls, even after they confirmed it was the same task, until it ran out of turns. Guardian still reviews every update. A takeover forgets the earlier question, so the caller may be asked once more after one.
- A `mint_update` recommending a new build whose `suggestedRequest` screening made longer than 500 characters, such as by replacing a private value with a longer marker, is now refused before review as `update_invalid` with the new `reason` `suggested_request_too_long`, and the minter shortens it. The build used to end `new_mint_recommended` without the suggestion, so its caller had nothing to send. Each such call is refused until the suggestion fits, and the attempt's turn budget bounds a minter that never shortens it.
- The workspace guide says a build can't ask for a file. When the task needs one the request doesn't carry, the minter says so in `report_blocked`, naming the file the owner should attach when they build again. A composing host keeps the line, since it sits outside every host section.
- The publication record `publication/tests.json` no longer lists an HTTP version whose `src/tool-http.mjs` was deleted after its `live_tests` batch ran. It took the HTTP version's entrypoint from that batch's results whether or not the file still existed, so a deleted version that does not ship still went to the review, which could judge its untested capabilities as gaps no further test could clear. The record now lists the HTTP version by `src/tool-http.mjs` while the host's `pathExists` finds that file, and leaves it out when it doesn't, so a scratch `*-http.mjs` the latest batch ran, deleted or kept, no longer stands in for the version that ships. A host without `pathExists`, or whose check throws, keeps the file the latest results ran, as before.
- An honest read whose site shows no results for a tested input can now pass publication review. Its minter may change such a case to expect `empty`, and review no longer counts that as hiding a failure, nor as a case that tests nothing, when the captures show the site's own answer for that exact input, such as its no-results page.
  - Each retired entry in `publication/tests.json` adds the optional `changedExpectationBy`: a case that passes the same input with another expectation on that version's published source. The host excuses nothing by it; Guardian's `publicationLiveTestsPolicy` excuses the failure only when a capture shows the site giving the new answer for that input, on a page reached through the site's own controls or a URL they produce that reads the input back as the site applied it, never a challenge, error, sign-in, blocked or loading page, and the case's purpose or the coverage text names that capture.
  - A case that expects `empty` where the captures show the site's own empty state for that exact input tests the empty result. One that expects `empty`, `error` or `invalid_input` for an input the captures show results for still tests nothing.
  - Guardian's "Unapplied supplied input" rule says that with no results a sort has nothing to apply: the tool returns the verified empty result, never `InputNotApplied` for it.
  - A finding about `publication/tests.json` keeps its ordinary reason, such as `source_correction` or `evidence`, and goes back to the minter: a publication decision with reason `host_owned` and a finding in that file fails to decode as `InvalidDecision`, as one in `publication/definition.json` does. A `host_owned` denial in any other host file still ends the attempt as the host's failure.
  - The publication skill adds an edge-case check before `finish_build`, naming the review rule each miss comes back under. The testing skill says when a case's expectation may change, and the core skill says to read each page's fields in one call, since each call on a hosted browser is a remote round trip, and to prefer list data or the site's own data request to opening every record's page.
  - A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Login parameters in hash-routed callback URLs are now detected correctly. `oneTimeLoginUrlParameters` from `pomerado/core/mint/login-url` parsed the fragment as a whole, treating a route path before its query, such as `/#/oauth/callback?code_challenge=…`, as part of the first parameter name instead of reading the query following `?`. A fragment of parameters alone is still read as a whole, so a `?` inside one of its values, as in `#SAMLRequest=…?…`, keeps that parameter.
- A URL now ends where a credential or a structural closer begins, so a credential written right after it is screened like any other text. `urlSpans` from `pomerado/core/privacy/url-spans` ran a URL to the next space, quote or angle bracket, so a field glued to it by `,`, `;`, `|` or `)`, as in a CSV row, a pipe table, a Markdown link or a `url=…;password=…` list, counted as part of the URL, and `redactDiagnosticText` from `pomerado/core/runtime/failure-detail`, which leaves URLs byte-exact, kept a credential there readable: `see https://portal.example/a,password=Synthetic1 now` kept the password. A URL now also ends at an unbalanced `)`, `]` or `}`; a backslash escape other than `\/`, `\u` or `\x`; a character reference to a double quote or an angle bracket, such as `&lt;`, `&quot;` or `&#x22;`, or one escaped again for markup, such as `&amp;lt;` (an apostrophe is a URL character, so `&#39;` doesn't end one); and a `,`, `;` or `|` whose next field starts with a credential-named `key=` or `key:`, a bearer or basic credential, an AWS access key ID, a provider API key or a JSON Web Token, in its path, query or fragment alike. A `;` that closes any other character reference, such as `&amp;`, `&nbsp;` or the chained `&amp;amp;` or `&amp;#x26;` of markup escaped twice, is no delimiter. A real URL stays whole, including lists of dates, numbers, addresses or names in its path or query (`?dates=2026-01-31,2026-02-07&otp=…`, `?ids=1,2,3`, `/flights/SFO-JFK/2026-01-31,2026-02-07`), a matrix parameter (`;v=1`), a `|` anywhere but before a credential, a URL in a CSV row, balanced parentheses (`/wiki/Foo_(bar)`, which used to lose its `)`), percent-encoding, JSON-escaped slashes and nested redirects. A scheme-relative candidate needs a host both as written and within the URL it ends as, so text past its end (`//token.`) can't make it a URL. The scan stays linear. Spans are now disjoint: a URL found inside another never extends it.
  - 0.4.0 canaries from `0.4.0-canary.154` also ended a URL at a `,`, `;` or `|` whose next field held an `@`, a long number, a date or a capitalized name, at a `,` outside the query that started any `key=`, at a `|` outside the query, and at the `,` or `;` written just before the URL. Those rules ended real URLs mid-query, leaving a later parameter of the URL, such as a one-time code or an API key, to a host's own screening outside URLs, so they are gone. They also ended a URL before some credential forms, a `password:value` field and a bearer value followed by punctuation, which the credential rules above now catch. A bare CSV or pipe field after a URL, which core does not screen, is inside the URL again, as before those canaries.
  - The new `urlSpanAt` ends a URL or path that starts at a given offset by the same rules. `redactDiagnosticText` uses it for a route at the start of a message, which used to run to the first space: `/cb,password=Synthetic1 failed` kept the password.
  - `redactDiagnosticText` also redacts a credential-named `key=` value written after `|`, `)` or `]`, as it did after `,`, `;`, a space, a quote or an opening bracket: `a|api_key=… b` kept the key.
  - The new `urlReaches` returns how far each URL reached before, to the next space or quote. It is no screening exemption. The failure detail schema measures its text bounds against it as well, so a stored detail an older release bounded still decodes, and truncation charges a URL's characters past that reach, such as a balanced closing parenthesis, to the budget outside URLs, so an older release also decodes a detail this one wrote.
  - `redactDiagnosticText` and failure detail truncation no longer slow down quadratically on text with many URLs.
- The core skill no longer gives two conflicting starts for an input description. A description that records a limited use starts with "By design:", then states how many values the input takes, such as "By design: one postal code, used only to find nearby stores", so a host that checks the marker at the start of the description still finds it.
- A read build whose signed-out step entered a name, email or phone number the owner had confirmed, only to set the session's own state such as the store or location the read applies, was denied as out of authority: Guardian's execution policy called every such step a write, confirmed or not. Once confirmed, such a step is now part of the read. It still needs the owner's confirmation and is escalated without it, and a confirmed step that submits or sends something to a person or an organization, or creates an account or a lasting record on the site, stays a write. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A sign-in through code boxes that send the code themselves, with no submit for the host to click, could hang a fill call for as long as the browser's default timeout allows, or fail with the last box reported unfilled, when the page loaded its next screen while one of the host's looks for the page's own send read the boxes. A look's read of a box it had just found waited for that box to come back, and the check of the box typed last counted a box it could not read as empty. Now a step call's read of a control it already found waits at most 5 seconds, except in the fill of a date of birth, and in such a look a box the host cannot read fails the look, which the host takes again, so the fill reports that the page sent the code.
  - `isolatedLocatorPage` from `pomerado/testing/isolated-locator-page` now honors an isolated evaluation's `timeout`, as Patchright's own evaluation does.
- A sign-in whose code screen is one-character boxes, such as six boxes for a six-digit code, asked the owner for the code once per box and typed the whole code into each box. The site got a wrong code, and a request carrying the whole code never counted as sent. A screen's code is now asked once and typed one character into each box, without the spaces and hyphens it is often written with. A code that then leaves other than one character per box cannot be the screen's code, so nothing is typed and the sign-in stops for want of a value, as for any value the owner did not give. The sign-in counts the boxes' characters, joined in order, as the code a request carries, records and rules out after a refusal. A run's sign-in reads a code screen that came back after its page may have sent the code itself (`page`) as a refusal only once a request carried that code, so a page that only seemed to send it never gets a new code asked as if the site refused the last.
- A tool whose source imports the host runtime through a relative path, such as `../../runtime/index.js` or `../../browser/index.js` from `src/` as the workspace README says, could not be live tested: every `live_tests` run answered `source_unreadable` and ran nothing. The harness's digest of the tool's source followed that import out of the workspace, which refuses such a path, and the failure was dropped silently. The digest now follows imports only into `src/`, `explore/`, `test/` and `scratch/`, as the published bundle does, since the SDK is the host's and fixed within a job, and a digest the host cannot read is reported through `MintDependencies.reporting`. The write-repeat guard reads the same digest to refuse a copy of a write's step under another name. It was undefined for such a tool, so a repeat was matched by entrypoint alone; a copy is now refused, and a changed source still runs. A read build that published on its example alone, because its cases could not run, now runs them and sees their results.
- A local build's `check_signed_in_marker` read a page it loaded, the marker's `openPath` or the site's root and the other signed-in page, as lacking the marker when the page drew it more than about 2.5 seconds after its load, as a client-rendered page may. The host now checks each page it loaded again every half second until the page shows the marker, the check fails for another reason, or 10 seconds passed since its first check on that page. Only the first check loads the page; each later one reads it as it is. What counts as showing the marker is unchanged.
- A request with `maxResponseBytes` but without `buffered-response-v1` in `requires` failed `invalid_request` before sending, with no reason given. `maxResponseBytes` alone now sets the response limit; `buffered-response-v1` alone still selects the 8 MiB default.
- A publication review whose outcome Guardian kept refusing, `InvalidOutcome`, was recorded as a `PublicationDecision` with the recovery `retry`, and later question and task update reviews read that as trusted host evidence while the minter had been told to revise. It now has the recovery `correct_source`, or `ended` when it ended the attempt, and lists `InvalidOutcome` in `failedChecks`. Each review's diagnostics name such a failure `code: "InvalidOutcome"` instead of `ReviewUnavailable`. The first `review_invalid_outcome` answer no longer carries `retryable: true`, which tells the minter to submit the same call again; after live execution has closed, an execution's answer points to the retained receipt instead of a revised execution; and a host that becomes unavailable during the review ends the attempt with its own cause instead of no host failure. A host that matched diagnostics by `code: "ReviewUnavailable"` with `reviewFailure: "InvalidOutcome"` matches `code: "InvalidOutcome"` instead.
- A Guardian question or task update review was asked for the execution outcomes allow, deny or escalate before its own, so it could return `allow` where a question review accepts only `allow_business`, `authentication` or `reword`. The host then retried that review as an outage with backoff, from the same input. Each review's request now lists its own outcomes in `trusted_review.outcomes`, only execution, recovery and publication reviews get the execution outcome policy, and a refused outcome gets one correction in the same review before it fails as `InvalidOutcome`. The harness no longer treats `InvalidOutcome` as an outage: the first in an attempt comes back as `review_invalid_outcome` for the minter to revise or withdraw, and the second ends the attempt incomplete with no host failure, since reviews completing in between kept restarting the outage budget.
- A local build no longer publishes a tool that rests on a sign-in it never verified. A write build could sign in again in the middle of its session, write, and publish with no sign-in, so every later run started signed out. `finish_build` now refuses as `autofill_recipe_not_verified` when a step the publication rests on ran after a sign-in sent the login and before any check verified it, and the build has no verified sign-in to publish. A write rests on every act step of its session, and a read on the example or explore it publishes. A read is refused even when its example ran signed out.
- Guardian's source reads on the local host now find the SDK under `operation/`, where authored source imports it as `../../runtime/index.js` from `src/`. A read of `operation/runtime/index.js` or `operation/browser/form-controls.js` returns the trusted SDK file. It used to fail as unavailable. An authored file at the same path still comes first.
- The local host's SDK files for the minter's workspace and Guardian's source reads now include `runtime/files.js`, which `runtime/index.js` and `runtime/kernel-operation.js` import. A read of it used to fail as unavailable.
- A host can require a site name and summary on a site's first publication, even for a tool that never opens the site, such as one that only transforms its input. Guardian's publication review could read that naming as a claim that the tool uses the site and deny it as an unsupported claim, and the host would not publish without the naming. `publicationDecisionPolicy`, which the core publication policy includes, now says host-required site naming only identifies the site the tool is filed under and is no unsupported claim on its own. The tool's own name, description or a declared variant that promises site behavior the source lacks is still one. A host with its own publication policy gets this by including `publicationDecisionPolicy`.
- A browser's refusal of a DevTools command while the host typed a sign-in field, before the one call that carries the value, made the fill `uncertain`, and the minter read that the fields and the submit may have reached the site, so it did not sign in again. The host now skips a session whose document it cannot read or walk, such as a large third-party frame's, and refuses as `binding_unresolved` when a host's `bindingWorld` fails or the browser does not resolve the node, so such a failure refuses the field as typed nothing, `binding_not_found` when a skipped session held it. A failure of the call that carries the value stays `uncertain`. Its diagnostics now keep the error's `reason` and `method` beside its `code`, each only when no value of the step appears in it. The minter then reads that the field may hold its value and that the host never clicked the submit, and `lostTyping` from `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-refusal` names such a report for a host. A lost answer of the submit's call keeps its message.
- A write that read back its confirmation and then failed for another reason, such as an unanswered question, a refused input or a rejected login, reported `possible_commit` false beside `write_status` `applied`. A caller that read only `possible_commit` could run it again and repeat the write. `possibleCommit` from `pomerado/core/runtime/run-outcome` now counts a write's verified effect, so such a failure carries `possible_commit` true. The local host's words for it say the website confirmed the action, so running the tool again would repeat it, and to read the site back before any retry; they never say to run it again. A read's verified effect still counts as no commit.
- A confirm question the minter asked beside a notice, such as approval of a site's terms with a note that nothing was submitted yet, was left out of the answers later Guardian reviews trust. A step that relied on the owner's yes was then escalated, and the owner was asked the same question again. `answersForReview` from `pomerado/core/guardian/question` now lists every answer except secrets and logins, whether or not the request carries a notice.
- The local host asked the owner a script's question again whenever a later step of the same build asked it, as when a step that failed before its commit ran again. A script's ask now reuses the owner's answer whenever the same question comes back in the build: however long ago the owner gave it, whether or not an earlier step entered a commit or may have, and as often as any step asks it, including the step that got it. The question must match by id, type, screened prompt, and screened options or follow-up, and the kept answer must still be valid for it. A code, secret or login question is always asked again, and so is a question after a task update or after the minter asked the owner a question with the same prompt. A newer script answer replaces the kept one. Guardian still reviews each ask, the script receives the reused answer in the same shape as a fresh one, and later reviews trust it as they trust a fresh answer.
- A write build whose script named a commit step with an underscore or a capital letter, such as `place_order` or `placeOrder`, could change the website during the build and then never publish. The live run entered the mark, but publication refused the declared name as `commit_marks_undeclared`, and the host had dropped the runner's whole commit report, so a renamed declaration was refused as `commit_marks_unentered`. `commitMarkPattern` from `pomerado/core/runtime/operation` now accepts any short identifier: a letter, then letters, digits, hyphens or underscores, still up to 48 characters. A name with a space or other punctuation, or a longer one, is still refused. A host that checks mark names with its own copy of the pattern should import this one.
- A sign-in step that splits a one-time code into one-character boxes, one field per character, was refused as it submitted when one of the code's digits stood alone in the form action's path or query, such as inside a flow ID, once the host had typed into the page earlier in the sign-in. The submission guard found each box's character as a code of its own, and missed the whole code in an action, since each digit adjoins the next. It now finds a step's code fields that each hold one character as one code, their values joined in field order, by the same rule as a code in one field. A code field that holds more than one character, and a password or other secret, is guarded on its own as before.
- Guardian could refuse a host sign-in step with a `code`, `recovery_code` or `private_answer` slot because no code or answer had been supplied yet, and tell the minter to ask for it first, which the auth skill forbids. The host asks the owner for that value only while the step runs, so the build could not go on. The execution policy's host sign-in rule now says the host fills such a slot only after Guardian allows the step, from a saved value or by asking the owner then, so Guardian allows the step without an earlier code or answer. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- Guardian could still refuse a host sign-in step with a `code`, `recovery_code` or `private_answer` slot under its general rule that a live step never enters a value nobody supplied. Its review of the build's blocked report then told the minter again and again to ask for the code with `request_input`, which the auth skill forbids, so the build could not go on. The execution policy's general rule now exempts those slots, since the host fills them while the step runs, from a saved value or the owner's answer. The question review says the host asks for a host sign-in step's code while the step runs, so it is not requested with a separate question, and prefers telling the minter to send that step again. A recorded Guardian response keyed on the exact policy text needs recording again.
- A tool input that takes one of several object shapes named a field every shape refuses once per shape, so an unknown argument came back as the same issue repeated. `valueFreeIssues` from `pomerado/core/runtime/schema-issues`, which the MCP input schemas from `pomerado/core/mcp/schema` report through, now gives each distinct issue once, in the order the shapes report them.
- A publication finding whose path carried a fragment, such as a JSON pointer in `publication/definition.json#/outputSchema/properties/price`, or missed or added a leading directory, failed the whole decision as `InvalidDecision`. The host then ran the review again from the start, up to five times with backoff, with nothing telling Guardian what was wrong, so it repeated the same answer each time. `anchorQuotedFindings` from `pomerado/core/guardian/publication` now puts a quoted finding on the indexed file its path names exactly, an empty file's included, then on the file its part before a `#` names exactly, and otherwise on the one indexed file that shares the most trailing path segments with it, its file name at least, a current file before a historical one. A path that shares no file name, or that ties between files, still fails to decode.
  - A decision that does not decode gets one follow-up in the same review that names the decode error and, in a publication review, lists the indexed paths. If its answer does not decode either, the review fails `InvalidDecision` and is not reviewed again: the model already saw the error, so a fresh review from the same input mostly repeats the answer and costs a whole review. `reviewFollowUpRounds` is now four. `decodePublicationDecision`'s error names a finding path outside the index, or a reason its outcome and findings do not fit. An execution allow without an action label gets the same follow-up.
  - A review that could get no follow-up, a private host kind's or one whose host `Reviewer` never sends `turn.followUp`, is reviewed once more after `InvalidDecision`, in place of up to five times: a fresh sample may decode, as after a truncated output, but repeated ones mostly repeat the same answer. A host that counted on `guardianOutageRetry` to retry decode failures further gets one retry.
  - A host's own `decodePublication` must be pure: the review also runs it, with no source reads, to decide whether an output needs the follow-up. Only an `InvalidDecision` it returns at once gets the follow-up; another code, or a decode that needs asynchronous work, gets none.
- `chooseOption` and `fillDate` from `formControlsCode` pass their `timeout` to every read of the control and its list. A control or list that vanished while they read it, such as a dropdown the page drew again, made a read wait for the page's default timeout, 30 s in Playwright, however short the picker's own. Such a read now fails with Playwright's `TimeoutError` within the picker's timeout of when it started.
- A run's sign-in failed when the site asked for a one-time code the build's recorded sign-in never saw, and a read's could end on that code page as one that can't tell. After the recorded screens, when the account does not show and no code has gone out, the replay now looks once for a one-time-code field and its form's submit, on a recorded screen's origin or a configured sign-in origin off the site. It never looks while a field of the screen it last submitted still shows, such as a password screen shown again or a refused recovery code. It gets the code as a recorded code screen does, from a saved value or the same question to the owner, then submits it and checks the sign-in again. A field marked `autocomplete="one-time-code"` always counts, and a page with one counts only it. On a page with none, a text, number or phone input counts when its name or id holds otp, totp, 2fa or mfa, or holds code or verification on a short numeric box (`inputmode="numeric"`, or a number or phone input, with a `maxlength` of 4 to 8). Such a field never counts when its name, id or `autocomplete` holds password, email, username, search, zip, postal, post, phone, promo, coupon, discount, gift, voucher, referral, recovery, backup, captcha, security, confirmation, access, company, country, card, cvv, cvc, csc, pincode, redeem, area or year, holds pin as a word of its own, as in `pinCode` or `card_pin` but not `otpInput`, or its `autocomplete` starts with `tel` or `cc-`. A refused code is asked again only while the same screen shows. A fill that typed nothing leaves the sign-in to the usual check, and a code typed but never submitted fails the sign-in. Recipes and tools need no change.
- A blocked report while the build's latest sign-in never confirmed ends the build as sign-in unavailable (`recoveryReason: sign_in_unavailable`, stop reason `sign_in_failed`), never as `site_declined`, `site_lacks_capability`, `policy` or `host_refused`: a site that kept showing its sign-in form never got as far as judging the request, and its caller was told to change it. Only an ending the report's usual checks accept changes: a report they refuse, such as a `policy` report with no refusal on record, is refused as before and the build goes on. `owner_declined` and `question_unanswered`, a `policy` report renamed to one of them included, keep their endings. The harness counts a sign-in unconfirmed once an `authenticate` that may have sent the login has no later one that says `authenticated`; a step that sent nothing, including a check that found the site signed out and a step stopped past its dispatch fence before sending, leaves it as it was. The harness checkpoint keeps it as the optional `MintHarnessSnapshot.signInUnconfirmed`, and a task update that moves the build to another site clears it. A failed sign-in that is still pending keeps its `sign_in_unresolved` refusal, and a declined or abandoned sign-in is unchanged. The summary is `signInUnavailableSummary`'s: its session wording when the new optional `MintDependencies.signInSessionLoss` says `session_not_kept` (the site accepted the login, but the session did not survive the page load after it), its plain wording otherwise. The answer is the one a report gets once sign-in is unavailable.
- A blocked report whose only standing refusal is Guardian's final refusal of an `authenticate` step ends the build as sign-in unavailable (`recoveryReason: sign_in_unavailable`, stop reason `sign_in_failed`), as one whose sign-in never confirmed does, never as `policy`, `site_lacks_capability` or `site_declined`: the build never got past signing in. A correctable refusal still grants no `policy` ending, and a final refusal of any other step, a publication refusal no correction gets past, a host check's refusal or a later confirmed sign-in keeps the report's own ending, as do `owner_declined` and `question_unanswered`. `MintHarnessSnapshot` adds the optional `signInRefusalOnly`; a checkpoint without it keeps the report's own ending.
- A sign-in form whose own action is a script that does nothing (`javascript:`, `javascript:void(0)`, `javascript:void 0` or `javascript:;`, the scheme in any case and whitespace only between the script's words and signs, never inside `void`, which script reads in lowercase only), as many sign-in pages write it, is no longer refused before anything is typed. Such an action goes to no origin, so the destination check refused it as `credential_target_refused` (check `destination`, part `form_action`) even on the site's own page, and the sign-in could never go ahead. A sign-in step's control whose form's own action is such a script is now judged by its frame, its document and its other destinations: navigating to it sends nothing and runs nothing, and the page's own code, which can read the field anyway, does the sending. Any other script action is still refused, since it runs whatever its address says, which markup put into the page could choose. The submission guard likewise lets a sign-in's submission to a script that does nothing fire by GET, since that puts nothing in an address; any other script action is refused by GET as before, and an action that carries a filled secret is still refused. A card-fill step keeps the old rule.
  - `Targets` gains the optional `scriptAction`, read with each control; a step call that finds it changed judges the controls again, so a form whose action turns into a script that does something is refused before the next value is typed. A submit `AutofillFillCall` gains the optional `scriptSubmit`, which `guardedSubmit` sets for a step with no part of a payment card.
  - The auth skill says the host judges such a form by where its fields sit. A recorded minter response keyed on the exact auth skill text needs recording again.
- A sign-in step whose submit is a link whose own address is a script that does nothing, by the same rule as a sign-in form's own action above, is no longer refused for that address. Many sites open their sign-in panel from a header Sign In link written as `javascript:void(0)` outside any form, and a step that only opens the panel, with no fields and that link as its submit, was refused before anything happened as `credential_target_refused` (check `destination`, part `submit_destination`) even on the site's own page, so the sign-in could never start. Following such a link sends nothing; the page's own code opens the panel. A sign-in step's control whose own link address is such a script is now judged by its frame, its document and its other destinations, as a button outside a form is: a link in a form is still judged by its form's action and each `formaction`. A link whose script does anything else, a link off the site and such a link on a card-fill step are still refused.
  - `Targets` gains the optional `scriptHref`, read with each control; a step call that finds it changed judges the controls again, so a link whose script turns into one that does something is refused before the next value is typed. The auth skill text is unchanged.

## 0.3.0

This release lets the person answering a minting question use their own words on any choice, records each Guardian review attempt's timing, stops runs of a built integration from calling Guardian, and starts runs, live examples and a write session's first step from the site root. It changes answer and question shapes that 0.2.0 cannot read: upgrade every host that reads stored requests or answers before any that writes them.

### Breaking changes

- `ValidAnswer` values change type. A choice's value is `ChoiceValue` (`string | { other } | { option, note }`), and a multiple choice's is `MultiChoiceValue` (`readonly string[] | { options, other?, note? }`). Code that reads `.other` after a `typeof value !== "string"` check, or treats a multiple choice's value as an array, no longer compiles.
  - Migrate with `pickedOption(value)` for a choice's picked option, and an `"options" in value` check for a multiple choice.
- `ChoiceQuestion` gains `allowNote`, and `MultiChoiceQuestion` gains `allowOther` and `allowNote`. Every choice and multiple choice the minting agent asks carries `allowOther: true, allowNote: true`.
- `InvalidAnswer`'s `reason` adds `note_not_allowed`, for a note on a question without `allowNote`.
  - Migrate by giving any exhaustive check or list of reasons the new one.
- `AgentRequest`'s choices are `ProposedChoiceQuestion` and `ProposedMultiChoiceQuestion`, without `allowOther`. A `request_input` call that sets `allowOther` or `allowNote` is answered `question_invalid` and asked again without it.
  - Migrate any recorded model responses that set `allowOther`.
- `isReadOrWriteChoice` now accepts a read-or-write choice with `allowOther`. `AnsweredQuestion` gains optional `other` and `note`.
- Version 0.2.0 cannot read requests or answers in the new shapes: its strict decoders refuse the new question fields, and its `validateAnswer` and `validateKeptAnswers` refuse `{ option, note }` and `{ options, … }` as `malformed`. Upgrade every host that reads stored requests or answers before any that writes them, and do not roll back once they are stored.
- Guardian now reviews the read-or-write question the minter asks first, since it accepts the person's own words. A host whose question review refuses that question must allow it, or the first turn fails.
- New exports: `ChoiceValue`, `MultiChoiceValue`, `pickedOption`, `ProposedChoiceQuestion` and `ProposedMultiChoiceQuestion` from `pomerado/core/runtime/input-request`, and `withOwnWords` from `pomerado/core/mint/contracts`.
- Runs of a built integration, through `run` or a served integration MCP, no longer call Guardian and need no model key. Guardian still reviews minting. A run no longer checks its `intent`, `effect` or `authenticationOrigins`.
  - Migrate by checking a call's intent and effect in your host before it runs, if you relied on the run doing it.
- `pomerado run` no longer requires `--intent`, and it ignores `--intent` and `--effect`.
  - Migrate by dropping both flags. Neither limits what a run does.
- A run, through `run` or a served integration MCP, starts at the site root of its URL and no longer loads the URL's path. `pomerado run` and each served call start fresh, in a new browser context. A library caller's run closes the scope's other tabs and keeps its cookies and storage.
  - Migrate by having an operation that needs a deeper page navigate there itself, as its example already starts at the root.
- A live example, a live test and a write session's first `act` step start at the site root after other tabs close. A build that hasn't signed in clears the cookies and site storage exploration left. A signed-in build restores the session saved right after sign-in, or keeps its session when none was saved. Later `act` steps, explorations and inspections continue the current page. The first live step that isn't reset still loads the request's URL.
  - Migrate by writing example, test and first-step source that runs its flow from the root, as the authoring guide already asks.
- A sign-in check counts only after the build's own sign-in steps typed the login's identifier and a password or code, or the user completed an approval. A code the site sent for that sign-in, which the agent asked for, counts as its code once an explore made a completed `fill`, `type` or `pressSequentially` call with it on a page, frame, locator or keyboard, in a frame on the site or one of its `authenticationOrigins`. A frame with no address of its own, such as `about:srcdoc`, isn't on the site. Keys typed with nothing focused count only when every frame of the page is on the site or one of those origins, since they reach whichever of its documents has the focus. Source that only holds the code, or typing it anywhere else, counts nothing. Before that, the check returns `signedIn: false` with `failed: "credentials_not_submitted"` and leaves the build signed out. Every sign-in step drops the session saved after the last sign-in, a check included, and a check after a confirmed sign-in starts a new one, so checking again is refused.
  - Migrate by sending the sign-in screens' `signInStep` fields before the `signedIn` check, and checking once per sign-in.
- `MintDependencies.priorAttemptMayHaveChanged` is removed, with the notice it added to the agent's first input.
  - Migrate by giving the agent your own notice through `drainStartIncidents` if a restarted write build must read back before it writes again.
- In `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-step`, `AutofillInspection.screen` adds a required `origin`, which `inspectAutofillStep` sets. A filled `AutofillStepReport`'s `submit` adds `"stayed_disabled"`: the fields were filled, but the submit stayed disabled through the wait, so the host never clicked it.
  - Migrate by setting `origin` on any inspection you build yourself, and by handling `"stayed_disabled"` in any exhaustive check on `submit`.
- `misplacedHandleRule` is no longer exported from `pomerado/core/mint/secret-handles`.
  - Migrate by calling `secretHandleRefusal`, which returns the whole refusal.
- A local build follows these read and write rules:
  - A read build may run its live example again. Its owner may approve turning it into a write build before it runs one.
  - `testInput` runs only on a read build's live test, as JSON text, at most twice per attempt. Any other use is refused before review.
  - `exampleInput` is a JSON object and runs only when the caller's input is empty: on a read build's example, or on a write build's act steps. The first act step that passes it and that Guardian allows fixes it for the session, whichever step that is. Act steps before it run the caller's empty input. Later act steps run it, and may repeat it unchanged or omit it. A different one is refused before review. Guardian reviews these act steps under a stricter effect: every value in that input, and any add-on or optional purchase the step chooses, must come from the request or an answered question, while values the page supplies follow the general policy. Publication decodes the composed contract against that input.
  - A write build refuses a live example or live test, and a live explore once its first act step ran. An unchanged act step right after one that may have changed the site is refused until another act step reads the result.
  - Once a write session started, an `authenticate` step without `signInStep` is refused, since it would run the agent's own source outside the session's act steps. A `signInStep` the host fills still runs.
  - `inspect` and `residual` are refused before review. A local build keeps no write maintenance, so it has no possible write to recover.
  - A secret handle is refused before Guardian reviews the step, instead of failing after review, when this attempt never issued it, when it sits in the source an example publishes, or when it is misplaced. A misplaced handle's refusal names its file and line. An offline step runs handle text as written.
  - A Guardian outage is retried for up to five minutes before the step reports the review as unavailable. A spent model quota ends the build with `model_quota_exhausted`.
- A local build's `finish_build` runs a publication review after its own checks. Guardian reads the files that would ship, the public definition and the example's output, or for a write each act step's source and the named step's output. A denial goes back to the minter with Guardian's reason and findings. Input feedback gets two rounds of fixes. If it remains after them, the build ends incomplete with Guardian's categories and rationale, and `pomerado mint` exits 1.
- A saved integration holds every file under `src/`, whatever its extension, the entrypoint, and the files under `explore/`, `test/` or `scratch/` that they import. Other files under those three folders are no longer saved. Every file under the four folders is saved, as before, when the workspace has a `package.json` or one of the folders holds `node_modules`, or when a file those imports reach could load one they don't name: through `fs`, `createRequire`, a `#` import, a computed import, its CommonJS wrapper's arguments or Playwright's internal `playwright/lib/` modules, as a WebAssembly module or native addon, or because it doesn't parse as JavaScript, such as an extensionless file that isn't.
  - Paths and these names match in any letter case and Unicode normalization, as macOS loads files. An import of `../Explore/Look.mjs` saves `explore/look.mjs`, and a `PACKAGE.JSON` or `Node_Modules` counts.
  - A property or TypeScript type member named `arguments`, such as a tool call's `call.arguments`, is not the wrapper's arguments. The property still counts when read straight off a call's result, as in `frame.getFunction().arguments`.
- When a host's `specialize` returns no `policy` for a publication review, the OpenAI reviewer gives that review the core publication policy after the outcome policy, its file index as `trusted_publication` before `trusted_execution_environment`, and 32 turns unless `specialize` sets `maxTurns`. A reviewer with no `specialize`, as the local host uses, gets all three.
  - A host that returns its own `policy` from `specialize` for a publication review needs no change: it keeps exactly its policy, input and turn limit.
- A harness with no `inputFeedbackFallback` tells the minter that the build ends unpublished once input feedback outlasts its two rounds, then ends the build incomplete with the last review's categories and rationale. A harness with a fallback is unchanged.
- A sign-in step can record a security question's answer field with the new `private_answer` slot, on every host. `SignInStep` and `ExecutionRequest` in `pomerado/core/mint/contracts` take it, so the execute tool offers it to every host's minter. `SecretSlots` in `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-contracts` adds it, and so do `ExtraSecretSlot` and `AutofillSlot` in `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-step`. A `private_answer` field may name the observed question it answers (`questionSelector`), and only that slot may. `RejectedMarker` does not accept it.
  - Migrate by handling `"private_answer"` in any exhaustive check on a slot. A host that fills sign-in steps itself must supply each `private_answer` field's value, the owner's answer to the question the page shows, or fail that step clearly.
- `checkAutofillSignedIn` in `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-step` returns `failed: "challenge_form_visible"`, which `AutofillSignedInCheck`'s `failed` adds, while a recorded challenge field still shows. It takes an optional `challengeScreens`, the current sign-in's screens, which are `screens` when left out, and an optional `authenticationOrigins`, the configured sign-in origins, which are none when left out. Each field of `AutofillScreens` takes an optional `slot` and `identity`.
  - Migrate by handling `"challenge_form_visible"` in any exhaustive check on `failed`. To have the check refuse a challenge that still shows, pass each field's `slot` and, from its inspection's screen field, its `identity` (`label`, `ariaLabel`, `placeholder`, `type`, `autocomplete`, `name` and `id`). A field with no `identity` counts for nothing.
- `SignedInPage` in `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-page-code`, the answer of `autofillSignedInCode`, requires `challengeFormVisible`.
  - Migrate a host that decodes its own page code's answer with `SignedInPage` by returning `challengeFormVisible`, `false` when it checks no challenge.
- `InsertionRefusal` in `pomerado/core/destinations/credential-keyboard` adds `"question_changed"`: a private answer's question no longer reads as the host inspected it. The fill reports it under the `change` check, and `HostRefusal` in `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-refusal` takes an optional `cause`, `"question_changed"`, for it.
  - Migrate by handling `"question_changed"` in any exhaustive check on a native insertion's answer.
- A local build asks for the website login once, as one `credential` question for a username and password that is never saved, when its first sign-in screen needs it. It no longer asks a secret question for each field. After the site rejects the login, the correction is asked the same way, once. Codes, recovery codes, dates of birth, ZIP codes and identifiers the login lacks are asked on their own, in the site's words, and a security answer each time a screen shows its question. An approval is a yes-or-no confirmation.
- A local build counts a sign-in value as sent only once a request the page sent carried it. While a sign-in is open, the local browser reports each request the page sends to the host, with a body up to 64 KiB, which the host holds in memory and only matches against the values it filled. It reports nothing while no sign-in is open. A signed-in check verifies the sign-in only when a request carried the login's identifier and the sign-in proved it: a request carried a password or code, the owner completed an approval, or an exploration typed a sign-in code.
  - A screen that asks for a password before any identifier screen, while the build holds no login, is refused with `login_identifier_unobserved`. It asks and types nothing.
  - A password or code is filled twice at most in one sign-in, and any other value once. A selector, page address or signed-in check that names the account's identity is refused before anything is typed.
  - A rejected value is accepted only when this sign-in sent it. A rejected code gets two fresh ones. A rejected login gets one correction, and the build ends with `credentials_rejected` when the site rejects it again or the correction repeats it.
  - A screen's result holds its report as `step`, with a `nextStep`.
- A local build publishes its verified sign-in with the integration, with no value in it. `auth-fill.json`, beside `pomerado.json`, holds the sign-in recipe: each screen's page, field selectors and slots, submit, and the signed-in check. It is version 3 only when a security answer names its question, and version 1 or 2 otherwise. `pomerado.json` names it as `signIn: { recipe: "auth-fill.json", entryUrl }`, where the entry is the minter's `loginUrl`, else the first screen's address, without any credentials or fragment it held. `MintArtifact` and `Artifact` take the same optional `signIn`.
  - A login URL that holds a value the build was given, such as the login's email in its query, is not published. `finish_build` answers `login_url_contains_credential` for the login URL and never shows the value, and the build can sign in again from a URL without it. A recipe that holds such a value is not published either.
  - `writeArtifact` writes `auth-fill.json` with each screen's keys in one fixed order, indented two spaces, with no final newline, so the same recipe gives the same bytes.
  - `auth-fill.json` is reserved, like `pomerado.json`: an artifact's source may not use the name.
  - `readArtifact` refuses an artifact whose recipe it cannot read or whose version it does not know. An artifact with no `signIn` reads as before.
- A run of an artifact with a published sign-in signs in with its recipe before the operation runs.
  - It first replays the recipe without values, and asks nothing when the session already shows the signed-in check. Otherwise it asks for the login once, as one `credential` question that is never saved, and for each code, date of birth, ZIP code or missing identifier a screen needs, in the site's words. A security answer is asked on every run, from the question its screen shows then. Values stay in memory for the run.
  - A rejected username or password is asked for again at most twice. A correction that repeats a value the site rejected is never sent, and counts as one of the two. A rejected code gets two fresh ones at most, as in a build.
  - The entry page must be on the site or a configured sign-in origin, and each screen on the origin its build recorded.
  - A run that can't sign in fails with `SignInRunFailed` before its operation runs. Its `code` is `CredentialsRejected`, `NeedsInput`, `RecipeFailed` or `MissingRecipe`, and its `reason` names a field or step, never a value. A security question that changed before its answer was typed is `NeedsInput` with `question_changed`, and an entry page that didn't load is `RecipeFailed` with `entry_page_unavailable`. A browser failure during the sign-in fails the run as it would during the operation.
  - An MCP call reports it as `Sign-in failed (code)` with what to do next, and without the warning that a website action may have taken effect, since the operation never ran.
  - `readArtifact` fails with `SignInRunFailed` and code `MissingRecipe` for a recipe that is missing, unreadable or of a version the host doesn't know, with `reason` `missing`, `invalid` or `unknown_version`.
  - An artifact with no `signIn`, as 0.2.0 wrote it, runs as before.
  - `pomerado` exports `SignInRunFailed`.
  - Migrate a library caller that runs a signed-in artifact by answering the login question through its `ask`, and by handling `SignInRunFailed`.
- `makeLiveAuthentication` is no longer exported from `pomerado`. `createPomerado` signs in itself.
- `HostRefusal.cause` in `pomerado/core/destinations/autofill-refusal` is now any `InsertionRefusal` from `pomerado/core/destinations/credential-keyboard`, not only `"question_changed"`. `typingRefusal` sets it to the finite cause of a native insertion that inserted nothing, under the `typing_refused` check. `sameHostRefusal` compares it, so refusals with different causes no longer count as one repeated refusal. The minter's sign-in notice explains each group of causes with its own next step: the focus left the field, the field or page was replaced or navigated, the bound field could not be found, or the browser rejected the inserted text. No notice names a binding, a selector or a value.
  - Migrate by handling every `InsertionRefusal` in any exhaustive check on `HostRefusal.cause`.

The package now holds the code the local host runs, the hook interfaces another host implements, and the signed-in marker checks behind `MintDependencies.checkSignedInMarker`. Local use through `pomerado`, `pomerado/mcp` and the CLI needs no change for the following.

- `makeOpenAIReviewer` from `pomerado/core/guardian/openai` takes all three arguments, and its options need `executionEnvironment`. That option is a `GuardianExecutionEnvironment` object instead of `"hosted"` or `"native"`, and nothing defaults it. The `"hosted"` text is gone.
  - Migrate from `"native"` by passing `nativeExecutionEnvironment`, which gives the same policy text. Import it and the `GuardianExecutionEnvironment` type from `pomerado/core/guardian/openai`.
  - Migrate from `"hosted"` by passing your own `GuardianExecutionEnvironment`. Its `name` reaches the model as `trusted_execution_environment`.
